# airuleset internals — deep archive (on-demand, NOT path-scoped)

This file has **no `paths:` frontmatter on purpose** and lives outside `.claude/rules/`, so Claude Code NEVER auto-injects it — it is read on-demand only (grep/sed for the area you need). It holds the deep incident-archaeology, long rationales, measurement narrative and the overflow of the big areas (watchdog/hooks/tests/cli) that no longer fit under the 50 KB per-area cap. The currently-actionable, recent lessons for each area live inline in the path-scoped `.claude/rules/internals-<area>.md` files. Nothing here was summarised or dropped — moved VERBATIM from the former 973 KB `.claude/rules/airuleset-internals.md` (#482).

---
## Services — what each one is and how it actually works

Moved VERBATIM from the project `CLAUDE.md` (#93): these are per-service
internals, so they belong on the surface that loads when the service's own
files are touched, not in the always-on prefix of every session.

- **Playbook system** — per-project knowledge capture enforced after every autopilot ticket. Machinery in airuleset: rule module `modules/core/project-playbook-maintenance.md` (routing rule + `📔 Playbook:` marker mandate), `playbook-review` skill (runs post-ticket, inspects what was learnt, emits the gated marker), `playbook-cleanup` skill (one-time consolidation of accumulated notes into canonical how-to sections), and the Stop gate `hooks/stop-check-playbook-review.sh` (blocks completion reports missing the `📔 Playbook:` line). Content lives per-repo in `.claude/skills/<area>/SKILL.md` (per-area skill directories, indexed by a lean `## Playbook router` in the project CLAUDE.md — never in airuleset). Autopilot worker runs `playbook-review` at step 10 (after autopilot-log, before the final evidence block).

- **api-watchdog** (`watchdog/` package) — systemd `--user` timer (every 60s, every managed box), currently **28 numbered jobs, 6 of them REMOVED — 15 and 17 (#102), 12, 18, 23 (#132), and 26 (#402) — numbers retained, never reused, for historical addressability of prior comments/logs**. The newest pair, **(27)** NET-ISSUE-DRIFT ALARM and **(28)** STUCK-MAIN SWEEP (#137), close the observation gap that let camera-box's own +101 net-open backlog drift and 15-day merge deadlock both run undetected: both sweep EVERY repo `discover_managed_repos($HOME)` finds (not just repos with a currently open pane, unlike job 24) on an hourly cadence — job 27 via a `gh` opened-vs-closed trailing-7-day count, job 28 by reusing job 24's own `delivery_state` measurement repo-wide instead of pane-gated. `run_once()`'s own docstring (`watchdog/__init__.py`) is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for the exact behavior of job N — this bullet is a POINTER + a highlight reel, not a re-narration, precisely so it stops silently rotting every time a new job lands (#57 — this bullet used to say "EIGHT jobs" while the code had grown to 16). Operationally the most important: **(1)** auto-resumes a session STALLED on an API error (`continue` via `send-keys`, retry/backoff, ping on give-up); **(2)** pings (never acts) when a session is waiting on the user; **(4)/(4a)** nudge a session idle on `⏳ WORKING` with no advancing subagent, or one whose last turn emitted a tool call as unexecuted TEXT — in case its launched work died silently (the in-session rule is `modules/quality/verify-launched-work-liveness.md`; these are the model-independent backstop); **(6)** the 5-hour session-limit banner: ping once, retry the auto-resume only AFTER the reset clock passes (never before — that just re-hits the limit); **(7)** routes an owner's Discord REPLY back into the exact session that asked the `❓` (known-owner + explicit-reply-to-our-`❓` + idle-pane gated); **(8)/(11)** BOUNCE / gk-request BACKSTOPs so a cross-stream gatekeeper↔sub-dev hand-off (`## Cross-stream protocol` in the autopilot skill) never silently rots — job 8 only ever queries/nudges a repo actually enrolled in `_CROSS_STREAM_REPOS` (#89: `prio:bounce` has no protocol meaning on a repo outside the flow, and a bare label used as a generic priority marker on an unrelated repo must never be read as one); **(9)** `/goal` ARM DELIVERY — collapsed (#403, 2026-08-12) from job 9's old arm-question viewport-scan + "virgin candidate" guessing heuristic down to a thin per-sweep re-evaluation of EXPLICIT pending arm requests (`watchdog/goal.py`'s own module docstring is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for the model). A request exists ONLY when `airuleset.py goal-arm --self` records one — the `/autopilot` skill's own Step 2, called as its own last tool call right after printing the `/goal` line (the printed line itself stays as a manual-paste fallback, made benign by a tri-state already-armed check so a race between the two is never destructive). ONE function, `deliver_goal()`, checks the owner kill-switch, a hard non-refreshable age cap (expired → one deduped "arm failed, re-run /autopilot" ping — a silent-dead-autopilot failure class, unlike compact's harmless drop), pane resolve, copy-mode, dialog-open, and clear-suppression (a `cleared` marker NEWER than the request → drop, never retry) before typing — deliberately EXEMPT from the recent-human-activity gate that guards job 20's lane-nudge below, since the request's own origin already IS a human having just typed `/autopilot`; applying that gate to arm delivery would refuse essentially every legitimate arm for the loop's first 30 minutes; **(12)/(18)/(23)** REMOVED (#132) — the three restart jobs. All drove one `_restart_pane` helper that typed `/exit` into a pane and relaunched the session; it did so to a session the user was working in. **Nothing in this repo can end a Claude session any more, and `tests/test_no_session_kill.py` fails if that capability returns;** **(13)/(16)** hourly per-host burn snapshot + (dev1-only coordinator) fleet-wide merge, feeding `airuleset.py burn --compare`; **(14)** `/compact` delivery — collapsed (#402, 2026-08-12) from ~4500 lines / 46 functions spread across `watchdog/__init__.py` down to one ~740-line module, `watchdog/compact.py` (its own module docstring is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for the model). A request exists ONLY from two proven origins (`compact-request --self`, or the SubagentStop hook on an autopilot-worker's return — jobs 15/17's old context-size/idle heuristics stay REMOVED #102, and #400's earlier text-sniffing Stop-hook channel stays a permanent no-op). ONE function, `deliver_compact()`, checks pane-idle/no-live-tasks/not-on-a-⏳-or-❓-marker/30-min-cooldown/a hard non-refreshable age cap — every check unconditional, no time-boxed override on any of them — and this job is now just a thin per-sweep loop (`compact_sweep`) re-evaluating each still-pending request through that SAME function. The claim/lock file, the "substantiality"/"thin context" heuristics, both grace-window overrides, the retry-until loop, and the stash-delivery variant are ALL deleted, not merely disabled — they existed only to compensate for the trigger being a GUESSED boundary, and the two proven origins make that guessing obsolete; **(20)** GOAL DARK-WATCH + LANE-OCCUPANCY NUDGE — collapsed (#403, 2026-08-12) from `goal_rearm`'s 968-line heuristic re-arm/template-drift/outage-forensics/achieved-marker-verification machinery down to two small functions in `watchdog/goal.py` (its own module docstring is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH). `goal_dark_watch` first runs the shared janitor stuck-stash recovery (`_janitor_recover`, renamed from `_goal_janitor_recover` since it was never goal-specific — job 14's `/compact` sends share the same provenance dict) for every live candidate pane — the one sweep still guaranteed to visit every pane every ~60s regardless of pending requests, matching the old job 20's visit cadence — then, NEVER typing a single keystroke, pings ONCE (2-sweep debounced) when a session's transcript marker says armed but its footer has gone dark; a `cleared` marker or no marker at all stays silent by construction (clear-suppression is now trivial, not a heuristic). `goal_lane_occupancy_nudge` (#365/#351, migrated functionally unchanged) is the ONE watchdog-INITIATED keystroke action left in the whole family — a genuinely armed session sitting idle with an unfilled implementation lane and open backlog. Re-arming a dead/cleared/drifted goal is no longer this job's concern at all: only another genuine `/autopilot` invocation (job 9's own callback, above) ever re-arms; **(21)** LONG-TURN WATCH — a turn running past a threshold is its own fault state (nothing compacts, no question is delivered, keystrokes pile up unexecuted), read from the PANE's spinner elapsed label because CC logs a Stop-hook-rejected continuation as a fresh turn (#84); **(22)** STALE EXEC-MARKER CLEANUP — removes an orphaned `block-main-implementation.sh` one-shot bypass marker only once it is BOTH older than 6h AND no live pane's transcript stem still matches its session id, so a still-running session's deliberately granted exception can never be silently revoked (#97); **(26)** COMPACT-STALL WATCH — REMOVED (#402, 2026-08-12). Existed only to detect a stuck entry in the `/compact` claim/lock system #402 deleted wholesale; with no claim file left to get stuck, a request that cannot be delivered simply ages out via job 14's own hard, non-refreshable cap (`COMPACT_REQUEST_MAX_AGE_S`, `watchdog/compact.py`), logged the moment it lapses. Number retained, never reused; `python3 airuleset.py watchdog --once [--dry-run --verbose]`.
  - **Fork false-positive in the delivery/stuck-main jobs (24/27/28), #441 (`watchdog/repo_health.py`):** `_git_base_ref` (`watchdog/cards.py`) resolves the delivery base to `origin/HEAD` → the repo's own `main`. For a FORK that main is deliberately FROZEN — David works on fork feature branches and integration goes UPSTREAM via the gatekeeper — so measuring `delivery_age` against it reads as a permanent stall and pinged David daily for `kvaskodev/odoo-erp` (the "skákajúce čísla" were `rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD` changing with whichever feature branch was checked out). Job 28 now SKIPS a fork, detected PURELY LOCALLY (preserving its "no gh / no auth" invariant) by a distinct `upstream` remote (`_repo_is_fork`) or the `AIRULESET_STUCK_MAIN_SKIP` opt-out list, BEFORE the per-repo fetch, dropping any stale dedup entry. Job 24 (`delivery_stall_watch`) shares the EXACT same latent shape — a live pane in a fork would misfire its 📦 alert — but is deliberately left untouched: the FREEZE says fix only what actually failed, not the same defect pre-emptively where it hasn't misfired.

- **Transcript retention** (`discover_old_transcript_candidates` / `_compress_transcript_file` / `sweep_old_transcripts`, #410, split from #380 point 3) — closes the missing half of #376's own regression: #376 force-set `cleanupPeriodDays=3650` fleet-wide, disabling Claude Code's own native transcript auto-delete with nothing replacing it, so `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` grows unbounded. `discover_old_transcript_candidates()` walks each project directory's DIRECT `.jsonl` children only (v1 scope — MAIN transcripts, never a `subagents/` descendant, which neither `/resume` nor `claude-history` ever read; a live census found 0 reclaimable bytes there anyway) and applies 6 non-negotiable safety criteria: the NEWEST file in its OWN directory is unconditionally protected (compared by mtime VALUE, not object identity, so a genuine mtime TIE for newest protects BOTH tied files, #410 review F5), a symlink is refused individually and never followed, an age floor (default 30d, `AIRULESET_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_AGE_DAYS`) and a size floor (default 100KB, `AIRULESET_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_SIZE_BYTES`), and a live-process check reusing `_target_in_live_use()` (#315) VERBATIM. `_compress_transcript_file()` is the compress-verify-swap protocol: a single read pass compresses the source into a UNIQUELY-named tmp file (`tempfile.mkstemp()` in the same directory/filesystem — never a predictable `<name>.gz.tmp`, which #410's own adversarial review live-reproduced a concurrent-sweep data-loss race against, #410 review F1) while hashing the source bytes; an INDEPENDENT fresh decompress+SHA-256 re-read of the just-written tmp then proves the bytes ON DISK genuinely round-trip (never trusting gzip's own internal CRC alone); a TOCTOU re-stat of the source catches a resumed session that wrote more mid-compress; only then does `os.replace()` (atomic rename) + `os.utime()` (mtime preserved) + `os.unlink()` (original removed) proceed — ANY failure at ANY step leaves the original COMPLETELY untouched. Both this function's own decompress step AND claude-history's embedded `_read_jsonl()` (below) widen their exception catch to `(OSError, EOFError, zlib.error)` — a truncated/corrupt gzip stream raises `EOFError`/`zlib.error` from inside gzip's own decompressor, NEITHER an `OSError`, and catching only `OSError` let it escape uncaught (#410 review F3/F4, live-triggered: a raw traceback instead of a controlled failure). `sweep_old_transcripts()` is the cadence-gated orchestrator (mirrors #315/#345/#355's own sweep shape) whose OWN `dry_run` parameter DEFAULTS TO `True` — deliberately different from every sibling sweep — belt-and-suspenders on top of `cmd_install()`'s own env-var gate (`_run_transcript_compress_step()`, factored out specifically so a test can drive the REAL wiring rather than a re-implemented copy of its two gating lines, #410 review F2): `AIRULESET_TRANSCRIPT_COMPRESS_LIVE=1` is REQUIRED for live compression on a box, and this env var is never set anywhere by this repo's own code — report-only (zero writes, a printed candidate count) is the deployed default EVERYWHERE until the user personally signs off following `/resume` + history-browsing verification of a real compressed transcript. `claude-history`'s own `find_transcripts()` globs BOTH `*.jsonl` and `*.jsonl.gz` together (sorted by mtime), so a mixed project directory (some compressed, some not) still resolves the genuinely newest file regardless of which form it's in — this landed in its OWN commit, tested, BEFORE any compression code existed, satisfying the hard requirement that history-browsing of a compressed transcript is never broken by a later commit. Honest, deliberately-accepted residuals (documented, not chased, per FREEZE — none observed live): a full `/proc` live-use scan runs on EVERY `cmd_install()` report-only sweep rather than being cached/cadence-gated for the dry-run path (perf only, #410 review F7); a narrow TOCTOU window remains between the final source re-stat and the actual `unlink()` (#410 review F8); a symlinked PROJECT DIRECTORY (as opposed to a symlinked transcript FILE, which is already refused) is followed via `d.is_dir()` (#410 review F9). `python3 airuleset.py sweep-transcripts [--dry-run] [--min-age-days N] [--min-size-bytes N]` is the manual/testable CLI entry point (always `force=True`, bypassing the cadence gate; `--dry-run` is OPT-IN here — LIVE by default — matching #355's sibling sweep commands' own convention, the opposite of `cmd_install()`'s automatic wiring). **Browsing / exporting** these transcripts as HTML / Markdown / a TUI is the ADOPTED external tool `claude-code-log` (#420, audit #416 verdict "ADOPT, don't build") — see the on-demand `claude-code-log` skill. It reads ONLY plain `*.jsonl` (a `.jsonl.gz` is invisible to its `*.jsonl` glob — silently skipped, never a crash, the same horizon as native `/resume`), so an OLD compressed session is browsed by `gunzip -k`-ing a copy first OR via `claude-history` itself, which IS gzip-aware (`find_transcripts`/`_read_jsonl` read `.jsonl.gz` transparently) — the two are complementary, not competitors.
- Python stdlib only — no third-party dependencies
- **Wiring local (non-git) config to dev2 while the tree is dirty:** the `pre-deploy-clean-tree.sh` hook blocks `scp`/`rsync` from a dirty tree. For a genuine non-repo transfer (e.g. writing the local `~/.claude/.../.env`), pipe the helper via `ssh dev2 'python3 - <args>' < script.py` (stdin) instead of `scp` — no file-copy, no bypass marker needed.
- **A "re-capture fresh, right before send" guard (this file's own established `#176-F3` pattern, used whenever a blocking fetch sits between a sweep's stale `captured` snapshot and the eventual keystroke) must re-verify EVERY precondition the ORIGINAL stale check verified, not just the ones that happen to occur to you first.** #365-review round-2 MINOR (fresh-context adversarial re-review of an already-reviewed-and-fixed diff — SAFE TO SHIP AS-IS overall, but this one gap had zero test coverage): the round-1 fix added a fresh recapture that re-checked ONLY at-rest-ness (`_classify_boundary`/`pane_at_idle_prompt`), mirroring `_goal_template_drift`'s pattern by memory rather than by re-reading it — but that sibling ALSO re-checks `pane_goal_armed(fresh) is not True`. The blocking `_cached_backlog_count` fetch can span a whole final turn; if the loop finished and disarmed WHILE it ran, the pane returns to a bare, at-rest prompt (passes the narrower check) but the goal itself is gone, and the nudge would still get typed into a session that should never receive one. When copying an established "re-verify against a fresh capture" pattern from a sibling function, diff your new check against the SIBLING'S actual code (grep it, don't recall it) — a re-check that verifies fewer things than the pattern it's modeled on is a silent regression with no distinguishing symptom until the exact race window is hit live.

- **The api-watchdog timer executes the WORKING TREE live** (`~/devel/airuleset`, every 60 s, no install step) — an UNCOMMITTED edit to watchdog/\*.py is in production on dev1 the moment the file is saved (job 11's first false Discord ping fired mid-development, before any commit — 2026-07-24). Treat watchdog edits like live-prod edits: keep the tree runnable at every save, and expect real side effects during development; dedup/state files bound the blast radius.
- **GitHub issue SEARCH tokenizes quoted phrases** — `--search '"GATEKEEPER-ACTION:" in:title'` also matches titles merely containing the words (gatekeeper + action: the live #1768 "gatekeeper GitHub Actions runner" false match, 2026-07-24). Exact-marker semantics need a client-side literal filter over `--json number,title`; never trust the server-side phrase query alone.
- **CC hook payload ground truth (live-captured 2026-07-24, #28): `SubagentStop` carries `background_tasks`** — the harness's authoritative live-task list (`{id, type: shell|subagent, status}`), but it is **SESSION-WIDE**: it lists SIBLING workers' tasks too (#29 — a healthy worker was blocked over 5 sibling tasks it could not TaskStop; ownership must come from the agent's OWN launches: the PostToolUse ledger ∪ its transcript), and it INCLUDES a SELF entry for the stopping subagent (`id == agent_id`, always "running" at its own stop — exclude it); `transcript_path` is the PARENT session's file, the subagent's own is `agent_transcript_path`; PreToolUse inside a subagent carries `agent_id` (the subagent-context discriminator). Transcripts are written ASYNC — a hook parsing them can miss a fresh entry or see a stale one (observed both ways live); prefer payload fields, keep transcript parsing as fallback + retry-cap fail-open. Never trust docs alone for a hook payload — capture one live (`tee -a /tmp/…-debug.log` entry added to the LOCAL settings.json, removed by the next install).
- **A tmux pane's AGENT STRIP rows (`◯ <agent> <label>`) are model-generated ARBITRARY text** — never substring-match viewport "busy" signals (`Waiting for`, `esc to interrupt`) on a raw capture that includes below-prompt chrome; peel it first (`_is_bottom_chrome` / `_above_input_box`, watchdog job 9 gk incident 2026-07-24 — a worker label "Waiting for deploy-prod.yml jobs" blocked goal-autoarm for a day). The agent strip also has a SELECTED-row shape — `❯ ● main` / `❯ ◯ <agent>` — that renders BELOW the real input box and is easy to misread as the input box itself if only `s[0] in "●◯"` is checked (misses the leading `❯`); `_is_bottom_chrome` must also match the `❯ ●`/`❯ ◯` prefix and the strip's `↑/↓ to select · Enter to view` hint (watchdog#36, live david@subdev incident 2026-07-25 — every keystroke gate silently skipped the pane for as long as the strip stayed selected).
- **`⧉` (U+29C9 "two joined squares") is CC's pinned-preview / attachment row (`⧉  <project>`) rendered at the very BOTTOM of the chrome zone, below the `● main`/`◯ <agent>` agent strip — and `_is_bottom_chrome` must ENUMERATE it (`s.startswith("⧉")`), the same as `●◯`/`⏵⏵`/the statusline.** #458: on ordinary ultracode panes `goal_autoarm` logged `skip:undeterminable` (goal.py:546) purely because that row was unrecognised. `pane_goal_armed`'s #383 footer-chrome-EVIDENCE check (`_trailing_bottom_chrome`, a BACKWARD walk collecting `_is_bottom_chrome` rows and stopping at the first non-chrome one) broke on the `⧉` row as its very first (bottom-most) footer row → `trailing=[]` → `None`. **The distinction that matters: box-FINDING (`_input_box_rows_raw`, `_classify_boundary`) is IMMUNE to whatever renders below the box (structural strategy between the box's own separators), so `_classify_boundary` was already `("input","")`-armable on the montalu3 fixture — `pane_goal_armed` was the SOLE blocker.** `pane_goal_armed`'s footer-in-view proof has NO structural alternative (it must POSITIVELY confirm chrome is on screen so it never calls a scrolled-off footer "dark"), so it is the one place that genuinely NEEDS the chrome enumeration current. Adding a KNOWN glyph is not "enumeration is a dead end" (that lesson is about TRULY-novel chrome, `▶ brand-new-widget`, which stays handled structurally + fail-closed); the `⧉  <project>` shape is thrice-observed (upomienky-prehlad 2026-07-25, presenter-dev2 #243, email_redesign_preview #458). A draft row `❯ ⧉…` starts with `❯` not `⧉`, so the input box is never peeled; the undeterminable→skip fail-closed default survives for a genuinely-novel glyph (locked by `test_goal_undeterminable_pane.py`'s `▶` control). **Consequence for existing tests:** any fixture that used `⧉` as its example of UNRECOGNISED chrome (to isolate the structural box-finder from the glyph fallback) must SWAP to a genuinely-novel glyph after this — `test_labelled_box_border.py::LABELLED_BORDER_ONLY`'s `test_the_bottom_edge_still_has_to_be_a_strict_separator` broke exactly this way (with `⧉` now recognised, the glyph fallback could reach the box and defeat the strict-bottom-edge isolation), fixed by `⧉`→`▶`; the bordered `test_watchdog.py::UNKNOWN_CHROME_*` fixtures kept `⧉` (their STRICT borders make its recognition immaterial — strategy 1 resolves regardless) with only a comment correction.
- **CC's prompt STASH (Ctrl+S) is SINGLE-SLOT with a SILENT overwrite, and AUTO-RESTORES the parked draft the instant the delivered turn completes — NOT via a second Ctrl+S, despite what the docs imply** (empirically verified against CC 2.1.220, watchdog#35). Before stashing, a capture containing `› stashed` means the slot is ALREADY occupied — stashing over it destroys whatever's parked there, so abort instead. One Escape into a pane holding a draft is safe (shows an "Esc again to clear" hint); a RAPID DOUBLE-ESCAPE permanently deletes the draft — never send two Escapes back to back into a live pane, anywhere. See `deliver_with_stash` (watchdog/__init__.py).
- **Manually reviving a stalled pane by hand (`tmux send-keys`, NOT the watchdog's own `deliver_with_stash`/`send_continue`): the ONLY payload you may ever deliver is the literal `continue` (or a genuinely deliberate, short, hand-typed literal/control key you composed yourself) — NEVER text you just READ off that same pane's screen.** #174: reviving a stalled `simap` pane by hand during the #172 livelock incident, the MAIN session read the pane, saw `❯ pošli mi to zhrnutie pre Mala` in the input box, concluded it was the user's undelivered draft, and typed that text back and submitted it — `continue` was never sent to simap at all. It was not a pending draft; it was a frozen render of a session that had died 6.5h earlier (a dead pane's rendering is stale by construction, and prompt auto-complete text was never typed by anyone). The watchdog's own `deliver_with_stash`/chrome-peel/stash-around machinery does NOT make this mistake (verified live during the same diagnosis — its whole design exists to tell a real draft from stale chrome); the failure is specific to AD-HOC manual revival, where nothing stops you from composing a `tmux send-keys` command out of what you just read. Correction in the moment: a single `Escape` to interrupt, then `continue`. A mechanical, narrowly-scoped backstop lives in `block-main-implementation.sh` (#174): it blocks a Bash command that captures a pane's content (`tmux capture-pane`/`display-message`, via a shell variable OR an inline `$(...)`) and then feeds that SAME captured value into a `tmux send-keys` literal payload in the same command line — the exact "read the screen, type it back" shape. It cannot see the cross-tool-call case (read the pane in one turn, compose the send-keys command from memory in a later turn) — THIS bullet is what covers that one; there is no mechanical substitute for it.
- **Pane-driven automation must NEVER extract a MULTI-LINE payload from the rendered viewport** — CC's renderer HARD-wraps long lines (a 3100-char /goal template rendered as ~8 visual lines; a single-line regex armed 166 chars and the evaluator lost the release/window conditions, gk 2026-07-20). The viewport is for STATE detection (arm question present? idle?); the PAYLOAD comes from the session transcript's exact bytes (`_transcript_goal_line`). `capture-pane -J` does NOT help — it rejoins tmux soft-wraps, not renderer line breaks.
- **Truncating user-visible UTF-8 in a bash hook: NEVER `cut -c` / `awk substr`** — both count BYTES on this stack and chop multi-byte Slovak mid-character (the "…sklad zač" truncated-question incident, 2026-07-04). Use `jq -Rrs '.[0:N]'` (codepoint slice; jq is already a hook dependency). Negative slices (`.[-N:]`) work too and clamp safely on short strings.
- **`set -euo pipefail` + `VAR=$(failing_cmd)` in a hook script: the shell exits IMMEDIATELY, before the next line (e.g. `RC=$?`) ever runs** — bash's `set -e` treats a failing command substitution used in a plain assignment as an unhandled failure, not something the caller "checked". Discovered writing `block-test-skips.sh`/`block-history-rewrite.sh`, which both delegate pattern-matching to an embedded Python script and need its exit code to decide whether to print a block message. Fix: `VAR=$(cmd) || RC=$?` (put it in a tested/`||` context so `set -e` doesn't fire), never a bare `VAR=$(cmd)` followed by `RC=$?` on the next line.
- **Adding a new PreToolUse hook with a genuine RED→GREEN commit pair** (not just presence-of-tests): writing the hook + its tests in one pass never observes real RED, since the file exists on disk regardless of git-tracked state. Get true RED by `mv`-ing the new hook script out of `hooks/` (and `git stash push -- <wiring file> <doc file>` for the settings.json/module-doc changes), re-running the new tests to confirm genuine failures, committing that test-only state as the `[red]` commit, then restoring the hook + wiring and committing as `[green]`. See b6fe3ba/a2b19da/e7105b0 (#7), 88ab805/62d8245 (#10), 0ce0a47/0d3afd4 (#11).
- **An inline bypass marker (`# airuleset:xxx-ok <reason>`) hook: strip QUOTED spans before matching the marker — never a plain substring grep on the whole command.** A real bash `#` only starts a comment OUTSIDE quotes; a naive `grep -q 'marker'` (or a Python regex with no quote-awareness) ALSO matches the marker text merely being MENTIONED inside a commit message body (this hook's own commit messages document its bypass syntax, and got wrongly logged as bypasses — twice, live, while authoring #4). Fix: `re.sub(SQ+"[^"+SQ+"]*"+SQ, "", cmd)` then the same for double quotes (build quote chars via `chr(39)`/`chr(34)` since the whole script is inside a bash `python3 -c '...'` single-quoted block — a literal `'` in the source breaks the outer quoting), THEN search the marker in what's left. See test_block_staged_content_values.py `test_marker_mentioned_inside_a_commit_message_body_is_not_a_bypass`, 0d1d91f→d1fde9b (#4).
- **A Python regex `\b` boundary silently fails right after `_`** (underscore is a `\w` char, so `\b` never anchors between `ghp_` and the token following it) — real leaked tokens are routinely prefixed exactly like that (`ghp_xxx`, `sk_live_xxx`). Don't reach for `\b` or a hand-rolled `(?<![chars])`/`(?![chars])` lookaround to bound a high-entropy-blob regex; a greedy `{N,}` quantifier over a tight character class (e.g. `[A-Za-z0-9+]{32,}`) already stops correctly at the first non-matching char with NO boundary assertion needed — adding one only reintroduces the `_`-adjacency bug. See hooks/block-sensitive-staging.sh `HEX_PAT`/`B64_PAT`, f3d648e (#4).
- **A short-lived CLI process (`python3 airuleset.py <cmd>`) cannot use `os.getpid()`/`os.getppid()` as a "stays alive for the whole campaign" liveness token when the campaign spans TWO SEPARATE Bash tool calls** (e.g. a lock's `acquire` now, `release` much later) — each Claude Code Bash tool call spawns a fresh ephemeral shell that dies the instant that ONE call returns, so `os.getppid()` (the per-call shell) looks "dead" moments after `acquire` prints success. Verified live via `ps`: a Bash tool call's own PPID is the per-call ephemeral shell; THAT shell's PPID is the stable, session-long `claude` CLI process. Walk one extra level via `/proc/<pid>/status`'s `PPid:` line to get a PID that actually persists across the gap. See `_campaign_pid()` in airuleset.py, 74c2660 (#8).
- **subdev VPS runs fail2ban — NEVER probe its ssh with guessed users/keys.** A few wrong-key attempts (default key as `david@`, `root@`, …) ban dev1's source IP for 1h+ on ALL interfaces (tailscale AND public; "Connection refused" = tcp-reset REJECT, not an outage — verify from another vantage, e.g. gk), which then fails every subdev push target. The identities are FIXED in `REMOTE_HOSTS`: montalu = default newlevel key, marek + david = `~/.secrets/gatekeeper_access_ed25519`. Read them from there before any ad-hoc subdev ssh (live self-inflicted incident, 2026-07-25 #35/#36 investigation).
- **Dedup/trim pass on the rule modules: phrase-locks in `tests/` are DELIBERATELY MULTI-HOME — a "restatement" across 2+ modules is often intentional, pinned in each home by a test.** Before assuming a repeated passage is removable, `grep -rn "<phrase>" tests/` FIRST: e.g. `test_question_policy.py` asserts the sleep-window phrases in BOTH `message-status-marker.md` AND `milestone-notifications.md`, and `60`/`UNLIMITED`/`AskUserQuestion` in BOTH `message-status-marker.md` AND `user-questions-slovak.md`; `test_playbook.py` pins `po každom tickete` + `📔 Playbook:`; `test_model_tiering.py` pins the SHAPE/ADVISOR lines in two model files. The genuinely-removable duplication is only the CROSS-DOMAIN kind (a module re-teaching another module's canonical policy) — trim that to a pointer and keep each file's unique angle; never delete the intentionally-multi-homed teaching. Hooks parse the assistant's OUTPUT, not module text, so trimming a banned-phrase enumeration is safe ONLY for the exact phrases the hook regex matches — verify per-phrase and KEEP the uncovered variants (`stop-check-prose-violations.sh`). See #14 (ef9aa9b/7e96662/5cd9228/7db9509/979cda1).
- **A running Claude Code session's AVAILABLE-MODEL LIST is fixed at its own session start — `/model <new-id>` can NEVER select a model released AFTER that start, no matter how many times it's retried.** Live incident (gatekeeper, 2026-07-25, #42): the box ran a CC build that DOES offer the newer model, with `settings.json` requesting it, yet a resumed (`-c`) session stayed on the stale model; opening `/model` by hand there confirmed the newer model wasn't even LISTED, and watchdog job 12 typing `/model` into it burned a prompt-cache-invalidating context re-read on every retry for nothing. Only a session RESTART picks up a model released after that session started. **That was job 12's premise and it is the one premise here that held — but the job is still gone (#132): its remedy auto-downtiered a model the user had deliberately chosen, which `model-awareness.md` forbids outright, and no automation may type `/exit` into a live pane to achieve it.** Note the narrow scope of the #42 finding: it is about a model released AFTER the session started. WITHIN a generation the list is complete — measured 2026-07-28, a session launched on `claude-opus-5` listed and selected `Opus 5 (1M context)` live, no restart.
- **Resolving a transcript for a job that has a specific (sid, cwd) pair (not just a live pane's cwd): reuse `_transcript_for_session(projects_dir, sid, cwd)`, never `find_active_transcript(projects_dir, cwd)`.** `find_active_transcript` returns the NEWEST `*.jsonl` in the cwd's encoded dir regardless of which session wrote it — correct for job 15 (which only ever has a live pane's cwd). A job driven by a STORED request keyed by session id (job 14's `compact-requests.json`, `{sid: {"cwd":...}}`) needs the transcript for THAT EXACT sid, not merely the newest one in that dir — `_transcript_for_session` tries the cwd-encoded path first then falls back to a sid-only glob across the whole projects tree (already used by `prune_answered_questions` for the identical reason). Added while wiring #48's context-threshold gate into job 14.
- **Manually smoke-testing a PreToolUse(Bash) hook: build the JSON payload with `python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.dumps({"tool_input":{"command":sys.argv[1]}}))' "$cmd"`, NEVER by interpolating the raw shell command into a hand-written `"{\"tool_input\":...}"` bash string.** A command containing its OWN literal double quotes (e.g. `ssh host "ls"`) breaks the outer JSON when pasted in unescaped — `json.load` then raises, the hook's `except Exception: pass` swallows it silently, and the STDIN-read fallback (`[ -z "$INPUT" ] && INPUT="$PAYLOAD"`) feeds the whole malformed JSON blob to the detector instead of just the command, producing a confusing FALSE NEGATIVE that looks like a hook bug but is actually a test-harness bug. Hit live while manually verifying `block-subdev-ssh-misuse.sh` (#51) — `ssh newlevel@subdev "ls"` appeared to pass through un-blocked until the payload was built via `json.dumps` instead of hand-escaping.
- **`gh issue close` has NO `-F`/`--body-file` flag — only `-c`/`--comment <string>`** (unlike `create`/`comment`, which DO take `-F`). A `gh issue close <N> -F file.md` call exits 1 with "unknown shorthand flag: 'F' in -F" — but the error is only a USAGE-check rejection, so it does not silently corrupt anything; the correct pattern for a long evidence body is `gh issue comment <N> -F file.md && gh issue close <N> -r completed` (comment first with the full body, then close with a reason, no `-c` needed since the comment already carries the evidence). Hit live closing #52/#53 with the standard evidence-file pattern from `gh-cli-recipes.md`, which only documents `-F` for `create`/`comment`.
- **Any `.get()` call on a dict crossing a network/subprocess/legacy-file boundary must validate `isinstance(row, dict)` first — a malformed/legacy line parsing to a bare scalar/list crashes with `AttributeError` on the very first `.get()`.** And any timestamp-based sort over data that MAY fail to parse must FILTER unparsable entries out BEFORE sorting (mirror `compare_changes`'s own `(c for c in changes if _parse_ts(...) is not None)` pattern) — never `sort(key=lambda x: _parse_ts(x) or datetime.min)`, since that fallback silently mixes a NAIVE `datetime.min` into a comparison against AWARE datetimes from the good samples and raises `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes`. #55's fleet job (`merge_fleet_row`, `observed_pct_per_day`) hit both — neither was in the ticket's own grounding digest; both surfaced only during self-review before the RED/GREEN pairs were committed, and both got their own dedicated pair once found.
- **A watchdog job gated on WHICH HOST this box is (not just "does the caller pass a param") is a new shape — job 16 (#55, `fleet_burn_job`) is the first.** Every prior job's "wired = on" convention (`target_model`, `burn_snapshot_path`, `compact_requests_path`, …) is param-presence-only; job 16 additionally needs coordinator-only behavior (every OTHER managed box already writes its own row via job 13 — only dev1 should fan out over ssh to merge them). The host check (`os.uname().nodename == "dev1"`) lives ONLY in `cmd_watchdog`'s wiring (airuleset.py), computing the injected callable (`fleet_fetch = _watchdog_fleet_fetch if <on dev1> else None`) — `watchdog/__init__.py` and `run_once` stay host-agnostic, exactly like every other injected fetch. Reuse this shape for any future box-specific job; never hardcode a hostname check inside `watchdog/__init__.py` itself.
- **Live-verifying a new watchdog job needs no forced run** — the api-watchdog timer executes the WORKING TREE every 60s with no install step (already documented above), so a job wired into `run_once` starts running for real on the very next systemd tick after the file is saved/committed. Check its OWN output artifact directly (e.g. `~/.claude/burn-history/fleet.jsonl` for job 16, or the relevant `state[...]` key in `~/.claude/api-watchdog-state.json`) rather than trying to force a manual cycle — #55's job 16 had already collected + merged all 6 fleet hosts for real, zero errors, before any manual `watchdog --once` was run.
- **A `/goal` command's ARMED/CLEARED state can be read from a session's transcript ALONE — Claude Code writes it as a plain `<local-command-stdout>Goal set: ...</local-command-stdout>` / `Goal cleared: ...` marker, as a TOP-LEVEL "user" entry's `.message.content` string (arm) or a "system"/`subtype:"local_command"` entry's `.content` string (clear) — never JSON-escaped beyond normal quote handling, so a plain `grep`/`jq` finds it verbatim.** The LATEST such marker in the file decides state (set with no later clear = armed) — no need for CC's own internal state, a `/goal`-clear command, or a pane capture. **Danger:** the SAME marker text can appear NESTED inside a `tool_result` array entry when a session greps/pastes ANOTHER session's transcript (e.g. auditing a foreign repo's autopilot loop) — a bare full-text grep would then wrongly read the CURRENT session as goal-armed. Fix: only match TOP-LEVEL string content (`.type=="user" and (.message.content|type)=="string"`, or `.type=="system" and (.content|type)=="string"`) via a `jq` filter BEFORE the `grep -oE`, which structurally excludes any `tool_result` (its `.content` is always an array, never a bare string). Discovered building `hooks/block-main-implementation.sh`'s goal-armed detection (#54) — reused this shape for any future hook/watchdog code needing `/goal` state from a transcript; never re-derive a pane-capture-based detector for a HOOK context (a hook has no reliable pane access — that's a watchdog-only mechanism, `goal_autoarm`).
- **`hooks/block-sensitive-staging.sh`'s Gate 2 content scan false-positives on a long CamelCase identifier — a Python test class/function name ≥32 chars containing at least one digit matches `B64_PAT` (`[A-Za-z0-9+]{32,}` + a digit lookahead) as a "possible secret".** Live-hit committing `tests/test_goal_turn_boundary.py` (#58): a 36-char `TestStep5Names...Shape` class name containing the digit "5" blocked the commit (since renamed with an underscore break). The Claude Code harness reports this as an opaque `PreToolUse:Bash hook error: ... No stderr output` — which looks like the HOOK crashed, but it did NOT: the hook exited 2 with a real `🚫 BLOCKED: ...` reason on STDOUT (the harness's generic wrapper just doesn't surface stdout content for this failure shape). To see the REAL reason, reproduce manually: write a JSON payload file (`{"tool_input":{"command":"git commit -m x"}}`) via the Write tool (not inline in a Bash command, or the literal substring "git commit" in YOUR bash command re-triggers the SAME meta-hook on the diagnostic command itself), then `bash hooks/block-sensitive-staging.sh < payload.json` with stdout/stderr redirected to separate files. Fix a genuine false positive by renaming/breaking the identifier with an underscore (`_` is outside `[A-Za-z0-9+]`, so it splits the run) rather than reaching for the `# airuleset:secret-ok` bypass, which is for content that truly cannot be renamed.
- **A test asserting "same clock-hour = no-op" (`now` then `now + 30`/`+ N minutes`) must use a FIXED timestamp with a safe minute (well clear of both `:00` and `:59`), never `time.time()`** — a real wall-clock `now` landing in the last `N` seconds of an hour makes the offset call land in the NEXT hour bucket, spuriously failing the assertion (#59, hit live at 19:59:47–20:00:14 CEST). This bit BOTH `TestBurnSnapshotJob` and `TestFleetBurnJob`'s `test_second_call_within_same_hour_is_a_noop`, and (#60 follow-up) ALSO every `TestFleetBurnJob`/`RunOnceFleetWiring` test once `fleet_burn_job` grew an `HH:05` collection-delay gate — any test driving an hour-bucketed job needs a `_fixed_now(hour, minute)`-style helper, not a bare `time.time()`.
- **Settling "does Claude Code natively support X undocumented mechanism" — read the installed CC binary's minified bundle, don't guess from vague docs strings.** `strings <path-to-claude-versions-dir>/<ver>` finds the feature keyword (e.g. `.claude/rules`), then `python3` `bytes.decode(errors="ignore")` + `text.find("function <name>(")` on the SAME binary lets you read the actual minified source around it — cross-reference a known-behavior function (e.g. the User/Project/Managed/Local switch that computes the well-known `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` path) against the unknown one; if both compose the SAME base-dir helper the same way, the unknown mechanism follows the known one's location. This settled #40 (confirmed `~/.claude/rules/` is real, not `.claude/rules/` project-only) — beware minified single/double-letter names (`fn`, `t`, `e`) are reused across unrelated closures, so match on the SURROUNDING call shape, not the bare name.
- **Before deciding a rule module is safe to trim/convert (any future #37-style pass): don't stop at a few manual `grep -rn "<phrase>" tests/` guesses — sweep every line of the file as n-grams (8-10 consecutive words) against the full `tests/*.py` corpus.** A manual guess only catches the phrases you thought to check; the file may lock content in ways you didn't anticipate (ask-before-assuming.md's table row 55 and its narrative were locked by phrases scattered across two different paragraphs, easy to under-check by hand). A ~15-line python script (read every `tests/*.py` into one string, for each module line try 10/8/6-word sliding windows as substrings) finds every lock in one pass and is cheap enough to run before touching any candidate module.
- **A "producer stamps hour N-1 / consumer must request hour N-1" convention across TWO separate jobs (or a job + one of its own inputs) is easy to get wrong on ONE side only — extract the shared bucket arithmetic into ONE function both sides call, never let each side re-derive "which hour" independently.** #63: job 13 (`burn_snapshot_job`) has always stamped its row with the hour that JUST completed (`bucket(now) - 1`) — correct on that side. Job 16 (`fleet_burn_job`) asked its injected `fetch()` for the CURRENT (still-open) bucket instead, so a producer row for that bucket could never exist; WORSE, the job's own local `snapshots.jsonl` tail row (the exact same producer, read directly instead of over the injected fetch) had **no freshness check applied to it at all** — it was trusted unconditionally regardless of which hour it was actually for. That second gap is the more dangerous shape: a caller that reads "its own" data via a different code path than the one it validates remote data through will silently skip validation on the local path. Fix: compute the shared bucket ONCE (`want_hour_bucket = hour_bucket - 1`) and apply the SAME `burn.hour_bucket_of_ts()` freshness check to every data source feeding the merge — local and remote alike — never assume a locally-read row is exempt just because it didn't come from the injected callable.
- **A short `tmux send-keys` command reliably QUEUES even into a BUSY pane (mid-turn, spinner showing, no free `❯`)** — CC's own type-ahead accepts it and runs it as the next turn once the current one ends. Verified live 2026-07-26 for both `/compact` and `/goal clear`. This means a delivery job does NOT need to poll for an idle pane if it only needs the command to eventually land — waiting for idle is what LOSES a timing race against something else (e.g. an armed `/goal` loop's own rapid re-dispatch) that can start before the next poll tick. Only a genuinely unsafe state (copy-mode, an open dialog, no locatable boundary, a real unsent draft) is a reason to defer, never "not idle" by itself.
- **Resolving "the ONE live pane hosting session X" OUTSIDE of `run_once`'s own per-sweep `panes_by_sid` map (e.g. a one-off synchronous CLI call) — match by TRANSCRIPT STEM via `find_active_transcript(projects_dir, pane_cwd).stem == sid`, iterating `list_claude_panes()`, never by cwd alone.** A cwd can be revisited later by an unrelated session; 0 or >1 matching pane must return `None` (fall back to the caller's retry path) rather than guess. See `_find_pane_for_session` (#65) — the same discipline `run_once`'s own `by_transcript` grouping already uses, just needed as a standalone reusable helper for a caller that isn't sweeping every pane.
- **Two "same shape" FakeTmux test helpers in this repo differ in whether their FIRST `capture-pane` call is special-cased** — `RestartFakeTmux` (test_watchdog.py, job 12/15) treats call #1 as the job's OWN initial `capture_pane()` read (returns `initial_captured`) and only feeds `cap_seq` from call #2 onward, because job 15 captures the pane itself before calling `deliver_with_stash(..., captured=that_value)`. `CompactFakeTmux` (test_compact_request.py, job 14) has NO such special case — job 14 never captures on its own (it's handed `captured` directly via `panes_by_sid`), so `deliver_with_stash`'s OWN internal re-captures are the FIRST real `capture-pane` calls and `cap_seq` must start at index 0. Mixing up which convention a given fake uses silently misaligns a scripted stash sequence by one entry — verify which "captures itself first" shape a job actually has before writing `cap_seq`.
- **Multiple watchdog jobs that can each independently act on the SAME session within ONE sweep (e.g. job 14's ticket-boundary /compact and job 17's hard-ceiling backstop, #69) need a SHARED per-sweep "already handled" set threaded through them in wiring order, not just a same-pane text check.** A pane-text dedup (`_pane_compacting` scanning for "Compacting conversation") is real defense-in-depth for a LATER sweep, but it races within the SAME sweep: job 14 sends `/compact` via its own internal `send_continue` capture (not the caller's `captured`), and a later job's OWN fresh `capture_pane()` call in that same `run_once()` invocation can still read the pane BEFORE CC's spinner text updates — a naive text-only guard would let the second job fire anyway. Fix: `run_once` builds one `compact_handled_this_sweep = set()` and passes it as `handled=` through `compact_ticket_boundary` -> `compact_stale_context` -> `compact_hard_ceiling` (each records every sid it actually compacts); a later job checks `sid in handled` FIRST, before any tmux call. Reuse this exact shape for any future pair/trio of jobs whose trigger conditions can legitimately overlap on one session in one sweep.
- **A state machine's own "confirmed below threshold, clearing the claim" transition is NOT by itself proof that a real action executed — it can pass coincidentally when the ACTIVE threshold used for the check differs from the one that supposedly triggered the send.** Live-verifying #69 on gatekeeper: forcing `AIRULESET_COMPACT_HARD_CEILING=1000` for ONE manual run correctly delivered `/compact` (real ctx 208700 >= the forced 1000), but the VERY NEXT systemd tick re-evaluated the PENDING_CONFIRM claim against the REAL default ceiling (300000) — and 208700 was ALREADY below 300000 the whole time, so it "CLEARED" instantly regardless of whether the queued `/compact` had executed yet (it hadn't; the pane was still busy). Don't report a lowered-threshold live test as "confirmed" from the state machine's own CLEARED/GAVE-UP transition alone when a second, different real threshold is also in play — watch the actual pane (or wait for a genuine trigger against the real default) for the authoritative "did it really compact" signal instead.
- **A watchdog job's "did I already act on this?" state must distinguish TYPED (keystrokes sent) from VERIFIED (the action actually landed) -- and its ONLY legitimate resend trigger is a DEMONSTRATED loss of what was typed, never elapsed time.** #72 (job 17, `compact_hard_ceiling`): the original state machine treated "still above the ceiling after N minutes" as license to resend, which is wrong whenever the FIRST send is still sitting unconsumed in a busy pane's own input queue (CC only drains that queue at a turn boundary -- a long-running turn can outlast any reasonable retry timer, live-observed at 1h14m). The fix drops the timer AND the attempt-cap entirely: a claim stays `queued` until either CONSUMED (the tracked metric -- here, transcript context -- reads back below the trigger threshold, proving the action really executed) or FAILED (a structurally DIFFERENT signal: the claim's target, here a `cwd`'s active transcript session id, has been REPLACED by something newer, meaning whatever held the original queued keystrokes is gone and they can never be consumed). Detecting FAILED needs no tmux round-trip at all -- a cheap pre-pass comparing `find_active_transcript(cwd).stem` against the sid the claim was queued under is sufficient, and it is the ONLY thing allowed to trigger a resend. Reuse this exact shape (typed vs verified vs demonstrably-lost, never a timer) for any future "send once, wait for real confirmation" watchdog job.
- **A hook script embedding a Python helper that itself needs LITERAL quote characters in its own source (a regex char class `['"]`, a string containing both quote kinds) should use `python3 - "$ARG" <<'PYEOF' ... PYEOF` (a quoted heredoc delimiter, argv for input), NEVER `python3 -c '...'`.** The existing `chr(39)`/`chr(34)` workaround (see the bullet above, #4) is for when you're ALREADY committed to `-c`; the heredoc form sidesteps the whole class of bugs — a quoted heredoc delimiter passes its body through bash completely literally (no variable expansion, no quote processing at all), so the Python source can contain any mix of `'`/`"`/backslashes with zero escaping. Caught this BEFORE it shipped while writing #66's heredoc-body-stripper for `block-gh-invalid-json-flag.sh`: a first draft used `python3 -c '...heredoc_re = re.compile(r"<<-?\s*([\"'"'"']?)(\w+)\1")...'`, embedding the standard-but-fragile `'"'"'` single-quote-escape idiom inside an ALREADY-single-quoted `-c` block — exactly the shape the existing #4 bullet warns about. Rewrote it in `block-history-rewrite.sh`'s established pattern (`python3 - "$CMD" <<'PYEOF' ... PYEOF`) instead: zero escaping, and the Python source reads exactly as plain Python. Reuse this pattern by default for any NEW embedded-Python hook helper; reserve the `chr(39)`/`chr(34)` `-c` workaround only for a case that can't use a heredoc (rare).
- **(Jobs 12/18 were REMOVED in #132 — no watchdog job restarts a session any more. The `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` recipe below is still the way to live-verify ANY job against a real CC session, so it is kept verbatim.)** **Live-verifying a watchdog job against a real Claude Code session: launch a genuinely ISOLATED throwaway session via `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=/tmp/<scratch>/config claude` — NEVER edit the real `~/.claude/settings.json` or point a manual job call at the real `projects_dir`/`state_path` to "test" a restart, even on a disposable-looking session, since job 12/18 scan ALL tmux panes on the box and would sweep in every OTHER real session too.** `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is a real, confirmed env var (`strings` on the installed CC binary: "Use CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=/tmp for ephemeral local writes with external mirroring") that relocates the ENTIRE `.claude` profile — including the top-level `~/.claude.json` (CC writes it INSIDE the config dir when the var is set, so copy the real one in too, alongside `settings.json` and `.credentials.json`, or the session hits fresh-onboarding/login) — while leaving `$HOME` itself untouched, so hook commands using `~/devel/airuleset/hooks/...` (tilde-expanded against the REAL `$HOME` at hook-execution time) still resolve correctly. Calling `hooks_reconcile()`/`model_reconcile()` directly with `projects_dir`/`settings_path`/`state_path` ALL pointed at the scratch dir is then safe even though the real `run` still lists every real tmux pane — `find_active_transcript(scratch_projects_dir, real_pane_cwd)` finds nothing for any unrelated pane's cwd (no matching encoded dir under the scratch tree) and is silently skipped, so only the ONE throwaway pane (whose cwd genuinely resolves under the scratch projects dir) is ever touched. Confirmed live for #70: bootstrap → edit scratch `settings.json` to add a new hook → re-run → real `/exit`+relaunch keystroke sequence → the new hook genuinely blocked a marker command post-restart, while the real dev1 `api-watchdog.service` journal showed stable cycles with zero restarts of any real session throughout.
- **Diagnosing "this fired more than once" on a managed box: NEVER trust a ticket's own stated cause without cross-checking `journalctl --user -u api-watchdog.service` against the SESSION TRANSCRIPT's own timestamps FIRST — the two together disambiguate "which of several possible senders actually sent this" precisely, and a plausible-sounding cause can be flatly wrong.** #71's own issue body blamed "job 14 vs the synchronous #65 path racing" — but `journalctl --user -u api-watchdog.service --since <window> --until <window>` showed ZERO job 14/17 log lines in the ENTIRE incident window (job print calls are unconditional per issue #36, so silence is dispositive, not just "didn't log this time"), while `python3 -c` reading the session's own `*.jsonl` transcript (filter `type=="assistant"`, print `timestamp` + tail of the joined `text` content blocks) showed the SAME `last_assistant_message` repeated verbatim across TWO Stop events (one rejected by another Stop hook's own feedback, one accepted) followed by TWO MORE bare `/compact` user-role entries minutes later with no new assistant turn in between at all — proving the real mechanism was repeated Stop-hook fires against an unchanged completion report (the armed goal loop re-evaluating), not a job-14 race. `ssh -i ~/.secrets/gatekeeper_access_ed25519 gatekeeper@100.90.94.41` reaches gk directly (see machine-identities.md); grep transcripts for a ticket number across `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl`, take the one with the freshest mtime as the live session, then correlate its `type`/`timestamp`/`message.content[].type` entries against the SAME window's watchdog journal before writing a single line of fix code.
- **Confirming `airuleset.py push` landed on all 6 targets: trust the push script's OWN idempotent second run ("Already up to date." for every remote) — do NOT follow up with manual `ssh ... git rev-parse HEAD` probes against subdev's marek/david users just to double-check.** #73: after a successful push, re-running `ssh -i ~/.secrets/gatekeeper_access_ed25519 marek@100.118.174.27`/`david@100.118.174.27` for a redundant confirmation (on top of the FIRST correct-identity check that already worked for montalu/gatekeeper) tripped fail2ban and both then "Connection refused" on a follow-up check — spending an extra ssh round for confirmation the push script's own output had already given. If a genuine live check IS needed later, use the fixed identities from `machine-identities.md`/`REMOTE_HOSTS` ONCE, not a repeat call moments after a working one.
- **`bash -c '...'` / `sh -c '...'` classification: extract the quoted script string via `shlex.split` on the WRAPPER segment (it already strips the outer quotes into one token) and recurse the SAME classify function on that string — never re-parse the wrapper's raw text with a regex.** #73 (`block-main-implementation.sh`'s Bash classifier): `shlex.split("bash -c 'grep -rn foo .'")` → `["bash", "-c", "grep -rn foo ."]` for free; finding the `-c`/`-Xc` flag token and reclassifying `tk[i+1]` handles arbitrarily nested `&&`/`;`/pipes inside the script with zero extra code, because `classify()` just calls itself on that string. The companion gotcha: stripping a loop's `do`/`then`/`else`/`elif` body-leader token must happen PER SEGMENT (after splitting on `;`/`|`/`&&`), never on the whole command — the loop HEADER segment (`for f in a b`) is deliberately left ambiguous (no command to classify), only the BODY segment gets the keyword stripped and reclassified as a standalone command.
- **Measuring the real $ cost of ONE `/compact`: the compaction's own summarization API call has NO logged `usage` entry anywhere in the session transcript — CC only writes `system`/`compact_boundary` with `compactMetadata.{preTokens,postTokens,durationMs}` (exact, trustworthy) and a `user`/`isCompactSummary` entry carrying the summary TEXT (no token count, only chars).** The one figure you CAN measure exactly is the elevated cost of the FIRST real `assistant` turn after compaction: its `cache_creation_input_tokens` is the genuine cache-rewrite cost (confirmed 100% `ephemeral_1h`, i.e. $10/Mtok for Opus, not the $6.25 5-min rate) of re-establishing the whole prefix (system prompt + rules + summary) that compaction just invalidated — typically 70-100K tokens, ~10x postTokens itself (postTokens only counts the compacted CONVERSATION, not the system-prompt/tool-schema overhead re-cached alongside it). The summarization call's own cost has to be MODELED (preTokens at cache-read price + summary-chars/~3.8 at output price), not measured — state that estimate explicitly rather than presenting a modeled number as directly observed (#74). Do NOT search for the summarization call's usage by literal-grepping `"isCompactSummary"` across `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl` and assuming a hit means a token count exists — it means the marker exists, nothing more; also note the `system`/`compact_boundary` entry and the `isCompactSummary` user entry are NOT in a fixed index order (observed both orders live) — search a small window both directions, never assume "compact_boundary always comes right after/before".
- **Diagnosing "how often does job 14's #48 small-context gate actually skip a compaction": the SYNCHRONOUS delivery path (`deliver_compact_now`, #65 — now the DOMINANT path since it fires before job 14's poll ever sees the pane) applies the identical `min_context` gate but logs NOTHING on either branch — a drop there leaves zero journalctl trace.** Only the POLLED path (`compact_ticket_boundary`, job 14) logs `"skip small-context (compact-request) <loc> ctx=<N>"` on a drop. So `journalctl --user -u api-watchdog.service | grep "skip small-context"` undercounts real skips — it only ever catches the rare case where the synchronous attempt itself failed (unlocatable pane, copy-mode, genuine draft) and fell through to the poll. Cross-check the CLAIM (not just the polled-path count) against the historical `compact_boundary` event distribution (`compactMetadata.preTokens` bucketed) across ALL local transcripts — that gives the real population size regardless of which delivery path handled each one.
- **A `~/.bashrc` shell FUNCTION is parsed ONCE at shell startup and then stays frozen in that shell's memory FOREVER — any behavior that must react to a future `push` (launch flags, a model pin, a mode toggle) belongs in a SEPARATE SCRIPT the function execs, never baked into the function body itself.** #77: `apply_ultracode_launcher()` used to write `--settings`/`--model`/skip-perms literals directly into the `claude()` bashrc function; rewriting `.bashrc` via `push` had ZERO effect on an already-running panel shell (tmux panes are long-lived — days old), so an opted-out setting (ultracode, #53) kept silently resurrecting on every relaunch of a stale shell no matter how many times the file was rewritten on disk. Fix: `.bashrc` now holds ONLY thin one-line wrappers (`claude() { "$HOME/.claude/airuleset-claude-launch.sh" default "$@"; }`, etc.) that exec a managed SCRIPT (`CLAUDE_LAUNCH_SCRIPT_DEST`, `render_claude_launch_script()`) carrying ALL the actual logic — the exact same shape as the caveman stable statusline shim (`render_caveman_shim()`), and `apply_ultracode_launcher()` now unconditionally rewrites+chmods the script every call (self-heals tampering, like the shim) while the bashrc block stays idempotent. **Live-verifying "did the shell pick up the change without a restart": don't just check the rendered file — reproduce it against a REAL persistent `bash -i` subprocess** (`coproc` + a marker-delimited read loop, or `subprocess.Popen` with a stdin pipe): source `.bashrc` ONCE, run the launcher, mutate ONLY the script file (never touch that shell again), run the launcher again in the SAME process, and diff the two outputs — that is the one proof that actually rules out "frozen in memory" instead of merely asserting the new file's content looks right. A plain `bash -c "source ~/.bashrc; ..."` per check is NOT equivalent (each call is a fresh, unrelated shell) and also silently no-ops against the REAL `~/.bashrc` (Debian's stock file starts with `case $- in *i*) ;; *) return;; esac` — non-interactive `bash -c` hits that guard before ever reaching the managed block; use `bash -i -c "..."` when testing against the real file, not a bare temp file without that header).
- **#77 fixed the launcher SCRIPT but never reaches a shell that already loaded the OLD `.bashrc` at its own start — `_restart_pane()` (job 12/18) must re-source `.bashrc` in the SAME command it relaunches with (`"source ~/.bashrc && claude"`, `RELAUNCH_CMD`), never a bare `claude`.** #79: a `type claude` on a shell started before #77 shipped still showed the frozen fat pre-#77 function with `--settings '{"ultracode":true}'` baked in — bash reads `.bashrc` once, at shell start, and `_restart_pane` typing bare `claude` just invokes whatever THAT shell already has loaded. This is the general lesson beyond this one bug: an automated `send-keys` relaunch of any managed shell command must assume the target shell's `.bashrc` may be arbitrarily stale and re-source it explicitly, never rely on "the file on disk is already correct" — a shell only re-reads `.bashrc` when told to. **Safely proving a fix like this WITHOUT touching any real pane or spending real API tokens:** stand up an ISOLATED throwaway tmux session, put a fake `claude` executable earlier in `$PATH` that only logs its own `argv` to a file, load the OLD frozen bashrc snippet into that one session to simulate a stale shell, send the exact new keystrokes the code sends, and read the fake-claude log — this proves the shell-resolution behavior precisely (which function/script actually gets invoked) without ever launching a real Claude Code process or risking a real pane.
- **A SHARED gate meant to block a job's SEND across multiple independent senders (e.g. a `/compact`-style dedup) must be checked AFTER the job's own pre-existing state-resolution logic but BEFORE its own send point — never as a blanket check at the very top of the per-item loop.** #78: placing the new shared-claim check immediately after resolving the session id (before job 15's own `PENDING_CONFIRM`/give-up state machine got a chance to run) silently broke that job's own legitimate ability to CLEAR its own state via a context-drop observation — the loop `continue`d away before ever reaching that logic, so a session correctly confirmed as compacted (by job 15's OWN mechanism) stayed claimed forever. The fix: let each job resolve its OWN bookkeeping first (exactly as before), and insert the new cross-cutting gate only at the point where the job is about to actually type keystrokes.
- **A `compact_boundary` transcript entry (`{"type": "system", "subtype": "compact_boundary", "timestamp": ..., "compactMetadata": {...}}`) is Claude Code's own durable, authoritative record that a REAL compaction landed — the only trustworthy proof of "did `/compact` actually execute", never a context-token read (which is an indirect proxy that can lag or race).** #78 confirmed this shape live against the exact incident transcript it was filed from; read it with a bounded TAIL read (mirroring `_last_human_prompt_ts`'s `f.seek(-tail_bytes, 2)` shape), not a full-file load, since a long-lived session's transcript can be huge.
- **A NEW unconditional (non-opt-in) cross-cutting state file that MULTIPLE existing watchdog jobs/paths must now consult on EVERY call (unlike an opt-in dedup gated behind e.g. a non-empty hash) needs every EXERCISING test class isolated via a `setUp`-level `unittest.mock.patch.object` of the path-resolving function — not per-call-site path-parameter threading.** #78's shared `/compact` claim (`compact_claims_path`) is read/written on every send decision across 4 senders and dozens of pre-existing tests (many of which never pass an explicit path param) — the live systemd watchdog executes this repo's WORKING TREE every 60s on THIS box, so an un-isolated test process would race a real production job on the real `~/.claude/compact-claims.json`/`compact-sync.log`. A `setUp(self)` calling one small helper (`_isolate_compact_claims`) that patches both the claims-path AND any companion log-path function to point at a per-test temp file gives every test in that class automatic isolation with zero changes to individual test bodies.
- **A queued claim/state machine gating a job's resend that ALSO has its own PARALLEL, older local state machine (job 17's `state['compact_ceiling']`, predating #78's SHARED `compact_claims.json`) needs the SAME third resolution signal applied to BOTH, not just the newer shared one — fixing only the shared mechanism leaves the older duplicate independently wedgeable by the identical bug.** #82: the live incident's actual frozen JSON (`{"status": "queued", "cwd": ...}`, no `ts` field) matched job 17's OWN `compact_ceiling` shape exactly, not the shared claims file's shape (`{"cwd", "ts"}`) — because the wedge originated BEFORE #78 unified the claim, so only job 17's own dict held the frozen state at incident time. Trace which mechanism a live incident's own JSON snippet actually belongs to (field names, not just "looks similar") before assuming a single fix location covers it.
- **Testing a watchdog restart that must CONTINUE the same transcript (`claude -c`, not a fresh session): `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=/tmp/<scratch>/config claude` as a ONE-SHOT PREFIX to a single command does NOT persist into a later `/exit`+relaunch typed in the SAME shell — a `VAR=val cmd` prefix scopes the env var to that one child process only, never exports it into the shell's own environment.** `export CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=...` FIRST, then launch bare, so it survives every subsequent command in that shell. Separately, `~/.claude/airuleset-claude-launch.sh`'s own `_has_conversation()` check reads `$HOME/.claude/projects/...` — the REAL `$HOME`, never honoring `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` — so relaunching through the `claude`/`claude-new`/etc bashrc wrappers under a scratch config silently picks the WRONG continuation source (usually "No conversation found to continue", or worse, a stray session written into the REAL `~/.claude/projects/`, needing manual cleanup). When a scratch-session test specifically needs `-c` semantics: bypass the wrapper entirely (the raw binary path, e.g. `/home/newlevel/.local/bin/claude -c`, or `command claude -c`) so CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR alone decides which conversation gets continued. Confirmed live for #82: this exact sequence (export once, `/exit`, explicit `<binary> -c`) reliably reproduced "same sid, same transcript file, conversation history preserved" — the precondition every restart-continuation live-proof needs.
- **Flipping a claim/state field's resolution semantics (e.g. #82's "proc absent = no-op" → #83's "proc absent = drop immediately") breaks EVERY pre-existing test that relies on the OLD semantics as a side effect, not just the tests for the specific bug — audit the WHOLE call surface, not just the reported incident.** #83 (fixing #82's own gap: a claim written before the fingerprint existed never resolved) touched only 2 functions but broke 19 pre-existing tests across both compact test files, because `RestartFakeTmux`/`CompactFakeTmux`/`DeliverCompactNowFakeTmux`'s `display-message` fakes ALWAYS return a bogus non-numeric pane pid (`"sess:0.0"`), so `_pane_claude_proc_fingerprint` NEVER resolves through them — every test that drove a REAL send through one of these fakes (not just ones manually constructing a proc-less dict) ended up with a "proc"-less claim, which is now (correctly) dropped on its next evaluation. The fix pattern, applied per test: (a) a test whose whole POINT is "this entry should persist/block" gets an explicit live fingerprint via `_spawn_dummy_proc(self)` + `wd._proc_fingerprint(pid)` (new shared helper `_alive_proc_fingerprint(testcase)`, added to both test files); (b) a test that drives the REAL send flow (job14/15/17/sync path) through a fake and then re-checks persistence gets `unittest.mock.patch.object(wd, "_pane_claude_proc_fingerprint", return_value=<alive fingerprint>)` wrapped around the call(s), so the fake genuinely attaches a fingerprint like real production does. Both patterns keep the pre-existing regression exercising the SAME path it was written for, rather than silently exercising the NEW proc-less-drop path instead (a false-positive-pass risk, since a test asserting "no resend" would ALSO pass — for the wrong reason — if the claim were simply dropped and the pane happened not to re-qualify). Before committing a genuinely-changed-semantics fix, run the FULL affected test file(s), not just the new/targeted test, and treat every unexpected failure as "audit whether this test's fixture needs updating for the new semantics", not as evidence the fix is wrong.

- **An armed `/goal` SURVIVES `/exit` + `claude -c` (verified live on an isolated session: pid 993139 → 1078067, same transcript, `◎ /goal` lit again on its own) — so a watchdog restart (jobs 12/18) does NOT silently disarm a loop and needs no re-arm patch.** What DOES kill a loop silently is a different failure entirely (#76): the goal stops without CC writing any `Goal cleared:` marker, so the transcript keeps saying "armed" forever. **Never build a goal-state detector on the transcript alone** — and specifically, do NOT reach for `stop_hook_summary.preventedContinuation` as a "goal resolved" signal: montalu's own HEALTHY loop wrote `preventedContinuation: false` every ~19 minutes while working perfectly. The only truths available are CC's `◎ /goal` footer indicator (armed or not, read from the region BELOW the input box) and its `✔ Goal achieved` conversation line (finished legitimately — printed to the screen, NEVER persisted to the transcript). Both are pane-only, so goal decisions read the VISIBLE VIEWPORT (`capture-pane` with no `-S`): scrollback bites BOTH ways — a stale `/goal` line there once armed a wrong goal into a fresh session (gk 2026-07-20, job 9), and a stale `✔ Goal achieved` there vetoes a legitimate heal after a restart (#76).
- **Reading a "does the footer show X" signal: define the footer as everything BELOW the input box (the last real `❯` line, excluding a SELECTED agent-strip row `❯ ●`), NOT as the `_is_bottom_chrome` peel and NOT as "the row starting with `ctx `".** A freshly launched session renders the managed statusline WITHOUT caveman's `ctx …` block — a row `_is_bottom_chrome` does not classify as chrome at all — so the peel stops ABOVE the very row the indicator sits on AND a `ctx `-prefix guard declares the whole pane unreadable (live, #76). The by-product is correct too: a BUSY pane has no `❯` boundary, so the read returns "undeterminable" instead of a wrong "dark".
- **A watchdog job that reads a marker which may be arbitrarily FAR BACK in a multi-hundred-MB transcript should scan INCREMENTALLY from a stored byte offset, not re-tail a fixed window every sweep.** `scan_goal_markers(path, off)` bootstraps from a 4 MB tail on first sight of a session and thereafter reads only the bytes appended since — so montalu's 240 MB single-session transcript costs one small read per sweep and the marker's AGE stops mattering (a fixed tail silently misses a goal armed hours ago). Two invariants: the returned offset must stop after the LAST COMPLETE line (a live-appended transcript's final line is often half-written, and consuming it drops a real marker), and an offset past EOF (truncation/rotation) must fall back to the bootstrap.

- **Claude Code drains its type-ahead keystroke QUEUE only where a turn's Stop is ACCEPTED — a Stop-hook-REJECTED continuation is logged as a fresh turn but does NOT drain it.** So "CC executes queued keystrokes at a turn boundary" is too loose to reason from: under an armed `/goal` loop (whose Stop hook keeps rejecting the stop to force continuation) the queue can stay unexecuted for HOURS across several internal turns. Live-proven on gk 2026-07-26 (#84): the transcript logged 13:25→14:36, 14:36→15:30, 15:30→15:44 as three separate turns, yet `queue-operation enqueue content="/compact"` entries from 14:31 and 15:41 both only `popAll`-ed at 15:44:15, right after the user's manual interrupt — and then fired back-to-back (first really compacted, the rest answered "Not enough messages to compact", which IS the duplicate-compact spam users report). Consequence for any "how long has this session been stuck" detector: read the PANE's own spinner elapsed label, never a transcript-derived turn duration — the pane label runs from the last genuine EXTERNAL input, which is exactly "how long the queue has been unable to drain", while the transcript would report three ordinary turns and miss the incident entirely (`pane_turn_elapsed`, watchdog job 21).
- **An `Agent` dispatch in this Claude Code harness is ASYNC BY DEFAULT — a missing `run_in_background: true` does NOT make it foreground-blocking — so "a foreground subagent dispatch blocked the parent turn" is never a valid explanation for a long-running turn here.** Verified on the #84 forensic read: all five `Agent` tool_use calls in the incident window had NO `run_in_background` key in their `input` and every one still returned in ~100–200 ms with the identical 1062-char `"Async agent launched successfully. ... The agent is working in the background."` payload. When investigating a multi-hour turn, skip that hypothesis and instead histogram the tool_use NAMES across the window (`type=="assistant"` entries' `message.content[].name`) — the #84 answer was an unbroken chain of correctly-bounded foreground `Bash` CI polls, each individually right, with nothing ever yielding control back.
- **Reading the rows CC renders ABOVE the input box (queued commands, the spinner): walk UP from the box and require ADJACENCY — stop at the first non-`❯` content row — never grep the whole capture for the shape.** `_above_box_scan` (#84) skips blank spacers and the box's own top separator, collects the contiguous `❯ …` queued rows, and returns the first other row as the spinner; both `_pane_has_queued_compact` and `pane_turn_elapsed` consume that ONE walk. The adjacency requirement is the guard that a bare substring/regex scan lacks: a session whose CONVERSATION quotes a panel (e.g. one working on this very ticket) has real content BELOW the quoted rows, so the walk terminates before reaching them. This is the above-the-box counterpart to `_is_bottom_chrome`'s below-the-box peel (#36) — together they mean neither the agent strip's arbitrary label text nor quoted transcript content can be misread as live pane state.
- **Adding an integration test for a watchdog job that ALREADY has an established harness in `tests/test_watchdog.py` (jobs 12/15/17/18 → `RestartFakeTmux` + `_seed_context_transcript`): write it THERE, never re-mock the job's inputs ad hoc in a new test file.** Jobs 15/17 resolve their own panes AND derive context from a real seeded transcript AND consult the shared claim before any pane work, so a new-file harness patching just `list_claude_panes`/`find_active_transcript`/`transcript_current_context` silently returns ZERO log lines (every pre-pass bails before the code under test) — it reads like the feature is broken when it is the harness that is wrong. Hit live writing #84's job-17 guard test. Corollary already noted above but worth pairing here: ANY test exercising a `/compact` sender must isolate BOTH `compact_claims_path` and `compact_sync_log_path` in `setUp`, or it races the live systemd watchdog running this repo's working tree every 60s on this box.

- **`/goal <new text>` REPLACES an already-armed goal — no `/goal clear` first (live-verified, CC 2.1.220) — and a `/goal` typed into a BUSY pane writes NO `<local-command-stdout>Goal set:` marker at all.** The queued path logs a `queue-operation` entry instead, so any detector built only on the `local-command-stdout` shape is blind to exactly the arms an automation delivers. BOTH paths write one common record: a top-level `user` entry whose string content STARTS with ``A session-scoped Stop hook is now active with condition: "<payload>"`` — that is the authoritative "what is armed right now", and `/goal clear` then reports that same payload back in `Goal cleared:` (which is how the replace was proven). Two parsing traps: the payload itself contains `"` (every shipped template has `--search "-label:autopilot-skip"`), so terminate on the LAST `". `, never the first quote; and a raw-BYTES pre-filter over the JSONL must stop BEFORE that opening quote, since JSON escapes it to `\"` and the full prefix never matches. Verify with the isolated-scratch-session recipe (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, below) — do not reason about `/goal` semantics from the docs.
- **`_typed_landed` structurally CANNOT verify a MEDIUM-length payload — one long enough to WRAP in the input box but too short to collapse into `[Pasted text #N]` — and that refusal is correct, not a bug to loosen.** CC renders a wrapped draft HEAD-first on the `❯` line with indented continuation rows, so the boundary line holds a PREFIX of the typed text; accepting a prefix match is exactly the signature of a TRUNCATED type, which is the #36 disaster the check exists to prevent. Consequence for any new long-keystroke delivery: it verifies only for payloads that either fit one rendered line or exceed CC's paste-collapse threshold. The real autopilot templates (3.1–3.7 KB) collapse and verify fine — but a scaled-down ~450-char stand-in in a test harness silently fails "delivery not verified" forever and looks like a product bug (cost two live runs on #64). Size any live delivery proof at PRODUCTION payload length.
