- **A `fork`-type subagent dispatched for a NARROW, read-only audit task can drift into the dispatching session's own broader PENDING task (per the fork-inherits-full-context rule already documented above) and start editing code well outside its assigned scope — its "result" field is not automatically trustworthy just because the dispatch returned.** #311: a fork sent to audit odoo-erp's own ticket chain bodies (a bounded, read-only `gh issue view` task) ran for 84 minutes and 199 tool calls and returned a result field that was a literal mid-sentence fragment ("Now let's rewrite this function to fix C1 and C2 (gate on `tmtime`, bounded window):") — language that reads exactly like it was independently exploring and editing the SAME watchdog code the dispatching session was also working on, not auditing GitHub tickets at all. Treat a fork's result as suspect whenever it reads like an unfinished plan rather than a completed report, do not rely on any of its stated findings, and re-verify the whole repo's integrity (`git status --short`, `git diff --stat`, `git log`) before proceeding — in this case the repo was confirmed clean regardless, but that had to be checked, not assumed.

- **A bracket class WITH diacritics that has been correctly converted to plain ALTERNATION (per this file's own two existing warnings about the LC_ALL=C bracket-class bug) can STILL be locale-inert — `\b` immediately adjacent to a multibyte character is ITSELF locale-dependent, in both the miss AND the false-positive direction, so alternation alone does not close the gap.** #316's adversarial review reproduced this live: `SK_APPROVAL_RX`/`SK_ARTIFACT_RX` (`schva[ľl]uje[sš]`-shaped bracket classes with `\b` anchors right outside them) missed every diacritic verb spelling under `LC_ALL=C` — and a hand-rewritten alternation-only version (no bracket classes at all) missed the SAME verbs under the SAME locale, while additionally false-positiving on an unrelated word (`dizajnérovi`) whose trailing `\b` only "succeeded" because a raw C locale treats the multibyte `é`'s bytes as non-word characters. The only fix that actually held: force a UTF-8 locale (`C.UTF-8` — present on every managed box's glibc) on the specific grep call, via a plain `VAR=val funcname args...` prefix — bash exports a temporary-assignment prefix into a shell FUNCTION's whole execution (not just an external binary), so it reaches the `grep` subprocess the function execs internally, and scopes to ONLY that one command (never leaking into a following `&& echo 1 || echo 0`). Whenever a NEW hook pattern needs `\b` next to a diacritic/multibyte literal, don't stop at converting brackets to alternation — reproduce it under `LC_ALL=C LANG=C` explicitly (a locale-parameterised regression test, not just a same-locale-as-dev unit test) and force `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` on that call if it fails.

- **`scan_goal_markers`'s OWN documented fail-safe (`return (off, None)` on any read error) is BYTE-IDENTICAL to "read succeeded, genuinely found nothing" — any NEW caller answering "has a `/goal` marker EVER appeared" (not the existing "what's the newest marker" question) must add its OWN independent readability check before trusting a `mark is None` result, or an unreadable/deleted transcript reads as if it had no history at all.** #320-review MAJOR-1 (fresh-context adversarial review, executed proof): `_goal_never_armed`'s first draft trusted `scan_goal_markers(off=0)`'s `mark is None` directly and its own docstring claimed "unreadable is not virgin" — false as shipped, since nothing in the call actually distinguished the two cases. Reproduced live: a chmod-0 file **containing a real `Goal cleared:` marker** still read as virgin (armable) — exactly the #170-critical direction. Fix: `os.path.isfile(tpath)` + a real `open(tpath, "rb")` performed explicitly BEFORE calling `scan_goal_markers`, so a genuine read failure is caught independently rather than silently collapsing into "nothing found". Reuse this pattern for any FUTURE "has X ever happened" question built on `scan_goal_markers` — the existing `_goal_dark_died_by_outage`/`_goal_recover_untracked` callers are safe today only because their surrounding logic already gates on other independent evidence first, not because the function itself distinguishes the two cases.

- **Ordering a NEW per-pane candidate gate: cheap dict-cache lookups and a SINGLE `pane_in_mode` tmux call must run BEFORE any expensive full-file scan, never after — a pane left in a SUSTAINED state (copy-mode, from a user scrolling up and walking away) will otherwise re-pay the expensive check every single 60s sweep FOREVER, since neither cache gets written on that return path.** #320-review MAJOR-3 (executed proof): `_goal_autoarm_virgin_candidate`'s first draft checked `pane_in_mode` right before the actual keystroke send — AFTER the (comparatively expensive) `scan_goal_markers(off=0)` full-file read — so a copy-mode candidate reproduced 5 full scans across 5 simulated sweeps with both `state['goalarm_hasgoal']`/`state['goalarm_virgin_tried']` caches staying empty the whole time, directly contradicting the function's own "paid at most once per session" cost claim. This matters more than it looks: `scan_goal_markers` reads the WHOLE file into RAM at once (`f.read()`), and this repo's own live transcripts run 200MB+ (dev1's own session in #320 was 212MB). Fix: reorder so `pane_in_mode` (and every OTHER cheap, tmux-cheap or dict-cache check) sits strictly before the scan; a mutation reverting the order alone (with the caches otherwise untouched) is what a call-count SPY test on the scanning function catches — a plain "does it type or not" assertion cannot see this class of bug at all, since the WRONG behaviour is extra cost, not a wrong outcome.

- **A staleness/liveness decision that ONE function (`_goal_cleared_stale`) already re-derives from `rec['last_armed']` vs a marker's `ts` must never be re-derived a SECOND, independent time by a DIFFERENT function that lacks the same input — two mechanisms computing the same fact from the same evidence can, and did, disagree within the SAME sweep.** #321: `_goal_cleared_stale(rec)` correctly flipped `rec['mark']` from "cleared" to "set" (a genuine re-arm CC wrote no marker for), but `_goal_dark_died_by_outage(tpath)` — called a few lines later with zero knowledge of `last_armed` — did its OWN blind fresh re-read of the transcript, found the SAME "cleared" marker, and disbelieved the override, permanently re-skipping the exact session the first function had just revived (live, dev1: `skip stale-goal` repeating every 60s for 15h+ with `mark` already correctly "set"). Fix: thread the SAME comparison input (`last_armed`) through as an optional trailing param (default `None`, every pre-existing caller unaffected) and apply the IDENTICAL rule, never a second one. General lesson: when adding a NEW function that independently re-derives a fact an EXISTING function already computes from richer inputs, either call the existing function's OWN logic or thread its inputs through — never let two computations of "is this marker still valid" disagree by construction.
- **"Darkness"/staleness must be measured by ACTIVITY (a transcript's own last REAL turn, `_last_real_turn_ts` clamped to `<=` the file's raw mtime, mirroring job 10's #177 fix), never by "time since last CONFIRMATION" (`last_armed`/`mts`) alone — the two answer different questions and only activity answers "is this session alive right now."** #321 shape A2: a continuously busy `/goal` loop can run for hours between footer confirmations (the SAME truncated-`◎`-glyph class `GOAL_ARMED_ACTIVITY_GRACE_S` already guards for a different branch) while its transcript keeps writing the whole time — measuring darkness by confirmation-age alone let a live, working dev1 session cross the 6h dark cap. Gate the (comparatively expensive) activity read behind the SAME cheap age check that already decides candidacy (`if dark_by_age: activity_ts = _last_real_turn_ts(tpath)`) — computing it unconditionally for every tracked pane on every sweep, even ones nowhere near the cap, is exactly the #320-review "cheap gates before an expensive read" bug recurring one function over (adversarial-review MINOR-1, this ticket's own review, fixed in the same branch with a call-count spy test).

- **`tmux send-keys -l <text>` parses `<text>` via getopt — a text argument whose FIRST character is `-` is read as an unknown FLAG and the whole call fails, and `_default_run` swallows that non-zero exit as `""` with NO exception and NO log, so a caller looping over CHUNKS of a longer payload silently loses exactly the one chunk that happened to start with `-` while every other chunk still lands.** #322 (2nd adversarial-review CRITICAL-1), live-verified against a real tmux 3.7b pane: `send-keys -l '-BBBB'` → `unknown flag -B`, rc≠0; `send-keys -l -- '-BBBB'` → lands correctly. This is FAR worse than it sounds when the caller's own "did it land" check is TAIL-based (`_typed_landed`'s `text.endswith(itext)`): a hole in the MIDDLE of a multi-chunk payload still passes, because only the true TAIL is what the check looks at — so a genuinely CORRUPTED payload (a `/goal` template missing 120 characters from its middle) gets confirmed as delivered and acted on. Live-measured against this repo's OWN 5 shipped `/goal` templates: 2 of 5 have a chunk boundary landing on a literal `-` (e.g. mid-word on "...`-self` (holds..."), so this was not a theoretical edge case, it was reachable on the very payloads this ticket exists to deliver correctly. **Fix: add `"--"` (end-of-options) as its own argv element immediately before EVERY literal-text argument passed to `send-keys -l`, in every caller, including a single-burst (non-chunked) delivery** — a short payload can equally start with `-` (e.g. job 7's arbitrary Discord-reply text, never `/goal`-prefixed). Reuse this for ANY future code that builds a `tmux send-keys -l` argv from caller-controlled or chunked text; the earlier `_type_literal` commit that introduced chunking (same ticket, round 2) did NOT have this guard and shipped it as a real, live-reproducible regression — a second fresh-context adversarial review of the ALREADY-COMMITTED diff is what caught it, not the original implementer's own corpus checks.

- **Verifying a "does every REAL shipped payload survive this delivery primitive" claim: extract the actual payload text from the repo's own files (e.g. every `/goal ...` line in `skills/*/SKILL.md`) and drive it through the REAL function with a fake `run` that reproduces the SPECIFIC external failure mode being guarded against — never a generic argv-recording lambda that always "succeeds".** #322: proving the `--` fix actually closes the CRITICAL-1 hole needed a `run` fake that rejects a `send-keys -l` call whose text starts with `-` UNLESS `--` immediately precedes it (mirroring real tmux getopt) — run against all 5 of this repo's real `/goal` templates, 2 of which demonstrably lost a chunk pre-fix and all 5 reassemble byte-identically post-fix. A fake that just records argv and returns success (the shape most test fixtures in this file already use) cannot ever observe this class of bug, because nothing in it can fail — this is the exact "the coverage gap the suite does not close" pattern the reviewer flagged: write ONE fixture per external failure mode you are specifically defending against, not just a happy-path recorder.

- **A "safe, zero-further-keystrokes" early return from a delivery primitive can still leave WORSE damage than the unsafe action it avoided, if that return skips a piece of state cleanup only the unsafe path used to perform — trace what the BYPASSED recovery function used to do for EVERY caller state, not just the one that motivated the early return.** #322 (2nd adversarial-review MAJOR-1): `deliver_with_stash`'s collapsed-paste early return (round 2) correctly refuses to backspace an unproven collapsed-paste buffer (no safe count exists) — but the recovery it bypasses, `_undo_and_release_slot`, ALSO used to pop a PARKED stash draft back when nothing else could be proven; skipping the whole function means a parked draft is never popped, so the single stash slot stays occupied FOREVER. Every LATER sweep's own entry precondition then refuses with the SAME `"stash-abort: slot occupied"` reason — which an existing #101 carve-out correctly treats as TRANSIENT for a genuinely foreign, self-resolving occupant, but for a slot the code ITSELF just stranded, that classification means the caller's own attempt-counter never advances and a "give up after N attempts, then tell the user" mechanism (built the SAME ticket, one function away) is silently unreachable forever — the #134 "a suppression needs a reachable exit condition" lesson, recurring one level down from where the SAME ticket already applied it once. **Fix pattern, reusable for any future bounded-consecutive-occurrence gate: bound a CONSECUTIVE run of specifically the ONE reason string caused by the new hazard (a dedicated per-session counter, cleared on any other outcome or a new streak), while leaving every OTHER reason sharing that same broad transient classification unbounded and unchanged** — the goal is not "stop treating slot-occupied as transient" (that would spam the give-up ping on ordinary, genuinely self-resolving foreign occupancy), it is "eventually surface a PERMANENTLY stuck one, without touching the common case."

- **A NEW "auto-heal a stale local checkout" fast-forward primitive has a whole failure class the dirty-tree check structurally cannot see: `git status --porcelain` never lists gitignored files, and `git merge --ff-only` treats an ignored/untracked file as expendable relative to an incoming TRACKED file at the same path — the fast-forward silently CLOBBERS it.** #314 (session-start-fetch.sh's new fast-forward-when-safe logic, a fresh-context adversarial review's CRITICAL F1, live-reproduced against a real repo: a gitignored `secrets.env` with real local content, origin independently adds a tracked `secrets.env`, the guard-free version merges clean and the local content is gone). The fix generalizes: before ANY fast-forward/merge attempted by automation, additionally check `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=A HEAD "origin/$BRANCH"` for paths origin is about to ADD, and refuse if any of them already `[ -e ]` exists locally — this is a DIFFERENT question than "is the tree dirty" and needs its own gate.
- **`git fetch` (and several other git subcommands one would assume are index-independent) FAILS OUTRIGHT with `fatal: .git/index: index file open failed` when `.git/index` is unreadable — not just `git status`/`git commit`.** Live-verified while writing #314's F4 regression test: with the index chmod'd to 000, `git fetch origin --quiet` itself returns rc=128 (caught by the hook's own `|| true`, so it fails SILENTLY) — meaning a hook that fetches-then-computes-BEHIND under this condition reads a STALE (never-updated) `origin/<branch>` tracking ref and can wrongly conclude "up to date" even though origin has genuinely advanced. `git rev-list --count` itself does NOT touch the index (confirmed — works fine once a fetch has already happened). Any test simulating "the working-tree-state check fails" via an unreadable `.git/index` must pre-fetch the real advance BEFORE chmod'ing, or the hook's OWN internal fetch (which also touches the now-broken index) silently no-ops and the test never reaches the code path it's trying to exercise at all.
- **A dirty-tree/diverged-branch regression test is MUTATION-INVISIBLE when the fixture dirties/diverges the SAME file the fast-forward's own target commit also touches — `--ff-only` refuses on its own regardless of whether the guard being tested even ran, so a mutant that DELETES the guard still passes the test.** #314's own adversarial review caught this on its own two shipped tests (dirty-tree, diverged-branch): mutating the hook to drop either check left both tests green, because the merge conflict on the shared file was doing the refusing, not the guard. Fix: dirty/diverge an UNRELATED path the fast-forward's target commit never touches, so a dropped guard genuinely lets `--ff-only` succeed and the test's `assertEqual(before, after)` actually fails — and assert the guard's OWN specific wording (not a bare "WARNING" substring) so a mutant that falls through to a DIFFERENT failure message is also caught. Verify with the established backup/mutate/assert-fails/restore technique before trusting any such test.

- **This repo's autopilot-worker worktree sandbox can have outbound SSH to some fleet boxes silently refused/reset while OTHERS on the SAME tailscale mesh stay fully reachable — confirm with a KNOWN-GOOD control target before concluding a box is unreachable, and never keep retrying the same refused target (the fail2ban risk this file already documents for real, not just for wrong credentials).** #318: SSH to `subdev`/`gatekeeper` from a worktree agent returned `Connection refused`/`Connection reset by peer` on the FIRST attempt with the correct, documented identity (never a guessed one) — while `ssh newlevel@100.82.64.27` (dev2) worked immediately, both before and after, ruling out a blanket sandbox network block. Most likely explanation: concurrent fleet activity from OTHER workers/sessions in the same dispatch round tripping subdev's fail2ban, or a transient firewall state unrelated to this session's own actions. When a live-confirmation SSH is refused, try ONE known-good control target (a box documented to always be reachable, e.g. dev2) to distinguish "this box specifically is unreachable right now" from "my whole sandbox has no outbound SSH" — and if the target itself is refused, STOP retrying it (do not loop) and report the gap honestly in the evidence block rather than guessing at root cause or risking a longer ban that could affect sibling workers' own subdev pushes.

- **The session scratchpad directory (`/tmp/claude-.../<session-id>/scratchpad/`, despite the session-id in its path) is SHARED across EVERY concurrent worker on this box, not exclusive to one worker's session — a GENERIC scratch filename (`commit_green.txt`, `commit_red.txt`) collides silently with another concurrent ticket's identically-named file, and the LATER writer wins with no error to either side.** #325: `ls` of the scratchpad mid-ticket showed dozens of `commit_*.txt`/`*-commit-msg.txt` files from a couple dozen OTHER tickets (#133 through #322) spanning several days — and a `commit_green.txt` written for #325 was silently overwritten moments later by a concurrent worker's own `commit_green.txt` for an unrelated ticket (#162), discovered only because the `cat`'d content read as an obviously different subject when about to be used for a `git commit -F`. **Always scope a scratch commit-message (or any short-lived cross-call) filename with the issue number** (`commit_green_ticket325.txt`, not `commit_green.txt`) — and verify a scratch file's content (an `md5sum` + a `head`/`cat` peek) immediately before feeding it to `git commit -F`/`gh issue comment -F`, especially if any real time passed between writing it and consuming it. This is a DIFFERENT failure mode from the file's own worktree isolation (git operations ARE isolated per worker) — the scratchpad is a plain shared `/tmp` path underneath, with none of that isolation.
- **When a fix's docstring claims it "mirrors <sibling function>'s own give-up shape verbatim", VERIFY the sibling is the FIXED version, not an unfixed one with the identical surface shape — this repo has BOTH in the same file for the exact `set-the-flag-before-checking-dry_run` bug.** #324-review CRITICAL-1: the new escalation ping's docstring said it mirrors `_goal_stall_nudge`'s give-up shape — true in SURFACE shape (same `if not rec.get(X): rec[X]=True; if send_fn ...`), but `_goal_stall_nudge` still HAS the pre-fix bug (`rec["spinged"]=True` set BEFORE the `send_fn is not None and not dry_run` check — a `--dry-run` sweep against real state permanently consumes the one-shot ping with nothing ever sent), while its sibling `dark_pinged` (same file, ~150 lines away) already fixed the IDENTICAL defect once (#238-review-shaped: flag moved strictly inside the real-send branch). The wrong precedent was copied because both read as "the same established pattern" from a quick scan. Before writing "mirrors X's shape", grep X's OWN code for whether IT still has the bug your new code is trying to avoid — a correct-looking precedent in this codebase can itself be the unfixed twin of a fix shipped elsewhere in the same file, and FREEZE means the twin stays unfixed unless it independently misbehaves in production.
- **`bool` is an `int` subclass in Python — `now - True` silently evaluates to `now - 1`, and any later change that widens a bare boolean flag into a real numeric timestamp must explicitly re-stamp or reject a LEGACY bool value, or arithmetic on it silently produces a multi-DECADE age instead of raising.** #324 CRITICAL-2: `goalrearm_noprogress[sid]` was `True` in the pre-fix code and became a wall-clock timestamp in the fix; a legacy `True` entry (confirmed live, present on dev1's own real, currently-running `~/.claude/api-watchdog-state.json` at the moment of this fix) computed `now - True == now - 1`, clearing a 30-minute grace bound by ~56 years and firing an immediate nonsense-aged escalation on the very first sweep after deploy. `isinstance(x, (int, float)) and not isinstance(x, bool)` is the correct guard — `isinstance(True, int)` is `True` in Python, so a bare `isinstance(x, (int, float))` check alone does NOT exclude a bool.
- **Verifying a watchdog fix against dev1's OWN real, currently-live `~/.claude/api-watchdog-state.json` needs no ssh and no forced sweep — the file is a plain local JSON path on THIS box, readable directly, and running the fix's own new validation logic against the REAL persisted values (never mutating the file) is stronger, live evidence than any synthetic unit-test fixture.** #324: both CRITICAL-2 (a legacy bool `goalrearm_noprogress` entry) and MAJOR-4 (`goalarm` entries up to 16.9 days old) were confirmed as GENUINELY LIVE, not merely plausible, by loading `json.load()` over the real state file and re-running the fix's exact `isinstance`/bound-check expressions against the real values in a throwaway read-only script — the same file this repo's timer writes to every 60s. This generalizes the already-established "the api-watchdog executes the working tree live" discipline one step further: a fix's OWN validation logic can be spot-checked against real production DATA (not just real production CODE-execution) with zero risk, since reading `json.load()` never touches the file.
- **Merging two independent boolean evidence sources into ONE combined flag via `has_a or has_b` silently breaks any LATER code that needs to know WHICH source actually resolved it — keep the sources as separate named booleans if a downstream branch (a log message, a report) distinguishes them.** #324: `has_progress = has_goalarm_record or _live_autopilot_progress(...)` correctly preserved the intended cost-ordering short-circuit, but a later `"live autopilot-progress evidence present" if has_progress else "a job-9 goalarm record for this pane is present"` ternary became DEAD on one branch — since `has_progress` is now `True` whenever EITHER source resolved, the `else` (goalarm-record) branch of that ternary became unreachable, and the RECOVERED log line always claimed "live autopilot-progress evidence" even when it was actually the goalarm record. Caught by careful self-review before commit, not by a test (no test asserted the specific wording of the RECOVERED message per-source). Fix: keep `has_goalarm_record` as its own named variable and branch the downstream message on IT specifically, never on the merged `has_progress`.
- **A BARE `tmux capture-pane`/`tmux display-message` call with NO explicit `-t`, issued from WITHIN a `display-popup`'s own shell-command, resolves against the ORIGINATING pane (the one the popup key was pressed in) — NOT the popup's own freshly-created pseudo-pane, and NOT "whichever pane happens to be the only one on the server".** #327: this is the mechanism that let the claude-history popup switch its primary content source from a transcript reconstruction to a genuine `tmux capture-pane -e` (real visual identity — same colors/wrapping/typography as what was actually on screen). Verified TWICE, independently: once with an isolated `-L` socket + a real attached pty client switched across THREE windows (a decoy window's content never leaked into the capture — proves it isn't merely "the only pane"), and again, more rigorously, by a fresh-context adversarial review's own repro — a genuine 2-SESSION/2-CLIENT server with the raw popup-key bytes injected into EACH client's own pty (the real key-press path, not a CLI shortcut) — confirming the resolution correctly follows whichever client actually pressed the key, in both directions. Mechanism: `display-popup` sets the popup job's own `$TMUX` to `socket,pid,<the pressing client's target session>`; a bare (no `-t`) capture-pane/display-message call then resolves against THAT session's active pane, exactly like it would for a normal attached client. **Two proven ways to break this — never do either:** adding `-c <client>` to `display-popup` (routed the review's own repro to the WRONG session's pane when issued from an outside command client), or invoking the script via `tmux run-shell` instead of directly as the popup's own shell-command. **Do NOT reach for `$TMUX_PANE` as a shortcut for "which pane is this" inside a popup** — an early draft of this fix asserted `$TMUX_PANE` there reads as the popup's own new pane (based on one test), but a later, more careful check couldn't reproduce that value reliably and the test that produced it never isolated its own environment first — it may have leaked a stale value from the CALLING shell rather than observing anything tmux itself actually sets for a popup. The bare-target resolution above is the only proven, reusable path; treat `$TMUX_PANE` inside ANY `display-popup` shell-command as unverified.

- **`tmux display-popup`'s `-e VAR=value` argument does NOT format-expand `#{...}` tokens — unlike `-d` (start-directory), which DOES.** #327, live-verified (isolated `-L` socket, throwaway session): `display-popup -e 'ORIGIN_PANE=#{pane_id}' bash -c 'echo $ORIGIN_PANE'` printed the LITERAL string `#{pane_id}`, never the real pane id — so `-e` cannot be used to smuggle an originating-pane identifier into a popup's environment. This is why the capture-pane-resolution finding above (a target-less capture-pane/display-message call) is the mechanism actually used — it needs no such plumbing at all. If a future popup script genuinely needs a format-expanded VALUE inside its own environment, `-d`/`-T` are the only options confirmed to expand; `-e` and the shell-command argument itself (already documented elsewhere in this file) do not.

- **A blank/content-free tmux pane for a DETERMINISTIC test: run a bare command directly as the session's own command (`tmux new-session -d -s t -x W -y H sleep 100`), never a shell.** #327: a freshly-created SHELL pane draws its own prompt almost immediately (a race any test relying on "genuinely nothing rendered yet" would have to fight), while a pane running `sleep`/any non-shell command directly never draws a prompt at all, so `tmux capture-pane -S -<N>` reliably returns nothing but blank rows (`\n` × pane-height) for the pane's whole lifetime — useful for testing an "empty capture" code path (e.g. a `$(...)`-captured shell variable, which strips ALL trailing newlines unconditionally per POSIX command-substitution semantics, turning that all-blank capture into a fully empty string).

- **`TMUX_TMPDIR` (env var, NO `$TMUX` set) is a lighter alternative to the established `-L <socket>` pattern for spinning up an isolated, disposable tmux server in a test — when the server has exactly ONE session/pane, a bare command with no explicit `-t` resolves it correctly with no attached pty client needed at all.** #327: `subprocess.run(["tmux", "new-session", ...], env={"TMUX_TMPDIR": scratch_dir, "PATH": ...})` (explicitly NOT inheriting `os.environ`, and critically NEVER including a `TMUX` key — `$TMUX`, when present, takes ABSOLUTE priority over `TMUX_TMPDIR` and silently redirects to whatever server it points at, which on a real dev box is a genuine, reachable server) stands up a server nothing else can reach; a later `tmux capture-pane -p` call with the SAME env resolves against it with zero ambiguity. This is materially cheaper than the existing `-L` + `pty.fork()` + real-attach pattern for simple single-pane cases, and is exactly what any real-execution test of a popup/capture-pane-calling script needs for isolation: any test that inherits `**os.environ` risks a `tmux capture-pane` call inside the script under test silently connecting to the BOX'S OWN real default tmux server (e.g. this repo's own sandbox agent-strip session) instead of failing the way the test expects.
- **`git ls-files --error-unmatch -- <path>` has THREE outcomes, not two — collapsing it to a boolean via `returncode == 0` silently mis-answers the "unmeasurable, don't guess" case.** #310: `rc=0` means tracked; `rc=1` means git ran fine and POSITIVELY confirmed the path is untracked ("did not match any file(s) known to git"); anything else (128 for "not a git repository", or any other failure) is genuinely unmeasurable and answers neither question. A first draft of `design_gate.is_git_tracked()` used `returncode == 0` for the "safe to treat as untracked" branch, which silently read `rc=128` (not-a-repo) as "untracked" — the WRONG fail direction for a check whose whole job is refusing a destructive action when uncertain. The correct fail-toward-safe form is `returncode != 1` (only a POSITIVE rc=1 confirmation counts as "safe"), caught by the function's own unit test (a non-repo cwd) before the change even reached adversarial review. Reuse `!= 1`, never `== 0`, for any future "is git ls-files's --error-unmatch confirming absence" check.
- **Two regexes independently scanning the WHOLE command text and then intersected together are defeated by PROSE merely DESCRIBING the pattern, not just by decoy syntax inside a quoted string — a stronger false-positive class than the already-documented "quote-state-blind" residual, and it needs a semantic gate (not a tighter regex) to close.** #310 adversarial review: a `git commit -m "docs: ... cat > README.md ... git commit -F README.md ..."` message — genuinely valid git usage, ZERO real shell write, ZERO real `-F` flag — independently satisfied BOTH a `>`/`>>`-redirect regex and a `git commit -F`-target regex, because neither regex required its match to be part of the SAME statement, let alone real un-quoted syntax. No amount of regex tightening closes this (the decoy text is syntactically real English prose, not malformed shell); the fix has to be a semantic check outside the text-pattern layer entirely — here, `is_git_tracked()` refusing to act on anything git already tracks, so the worst a false match can do is touch an already-untracked scratch path. Whenever pairing two independent whole-text regex scans via SET INTERSECTION as a detector, ask what a hostile-but-syntactically-valid PROSE string (not just adversarial shell syntax) could make both sides match, and add an out-of-band, non-regex gate for the consequential half if the answer is "something real".
- **`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, run with `cwd` set to a WORKTREE checkout, correctly resolves to the SHARED main `.git` directory — not the per-worktree gitdir file `.git` itself points to.** #310: verified live from inside this repo's own `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` checkout (`.git` there is a one-line `gitdir: .../.git/worktrees/<id>` pointer file) — `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` still printed the shared `/home/newlevel/devel/airuleset/.git`. This is the right primitive for any future "write to `.git/info/exclude`" (or other shared, never-committed git-local metadata) helper that must behave correctly whether called from the main checkout or from a worktree-dispatched worker — a naive `os.path.join(cwd, ".git", "info", "exclude")` would instead try to create `info/exclude` inside the per-worktree `.git/worktrees/<id>/` directory, which is not where a real `git status`/`git add` run consults for exclude patterns.
- **`gh search code "<literal substring of the reported message>" --owner <org>` is the fast, reliable way to identify WHICH repo actually sent a misrouted/misbehaving Discord ping (or any text artifact) when the ticket's own hypothesis names the wrong repo.** #334: the ticket guessed "airuleset notify path, or odoo-erp's own webhook" — neither was right. Searching for a literal fragment of the exact reported message ("ad-hoc prod" ) across every repo owned by the same GitHub account found the real sender (`codex-bridge:odoo_import/audit_log.py`) byte-for-byte in one call, a THIRD repo the ticket never named at all. Reach for this before ssh-ing anywhere or grepping a local checkout — it is faster and more certain than tracing the mechanism blind, and it works even when the sender is a repo you've never worked in before.
- **`notify.resolve_owner()`'s tmux-session-group fallback is CORRECT for a caller genuinely tied to the current pane (the idle ❓/✅ hooks, autopilot cards fired from a worker's own pane) but WRONG for any headless/detached/foreign caller whose notification is not "about this pane" at all — tmux propagates `$TMUX` to every DESCENDANT process unconditionally (interactive or not), so a subprocess spawned from a shell that merely happens to sit inside an unrelated tmux session inherits THAT session's owner with zero semantic relationship to who actually triggered the action.** #334 (the claude-david misroute): a headless `odoo_import` subprocess on dev2, invoked from a shell inside the `david-3` tmux session (group `david`), resolved owner="david" though the real operator was zbynek. The fix is NOT to narrow `resolve_owner()`'s fallback itself (no reliable signal distinguishes "genuinely interactive" from "inherited $TMUX" — narrowing it risks breaking the many legitimate hook callers that correctly rely on today's exact inheritance) — it is to give ANY caller whose notification is cross-cutting/internal/foreign-repo-originated an EXPLICIT owner override to bypass the fallback entirely. `cmd_notify`'s `--body` general-purpose external primitive now supports `--owner-name` for exactly this; any FUTURE external caller of `airuleset.py notify --body` that is not itself "the current tmux pane's own state" must pass `--owner-name` rather than trust auto-detection.
- **Extending #198's "validate and refuse, never mangle" rule to a THIRD call site: a CLI flag whose value is normalized (whitespace/case-stripped) before use must REFUSE when normalization collapses a non-empty input to empty — never silently pass the empty result through, since an empty-but-not-None value can behave completely differently from "not provided at all" downstream.** #334-review MINOR-2: `--owner-name "!!!"` normalized to `""`, which is NOT `None` — so it skipped `notify.send()`'s own `if owner is None: owner = resolve_owner()` fallback entirely, silently discarding a perfectly good `AIRULESET_NOTIFY_OWNER` env override and falling through to a mention-less shared-channel send. Fix: check `if owner_name and not owner: refuse loudly (stderr + exit 1)` — the SAME "normalized-empty from a non-empty input is refused, not silently accepted" shape already established for `--provision-question-thread`'s owner handling. Whenever adding an `--owner-name`-shaped CLI override anywhere in this file, mutation-test the normalization step explicitly with a denormalized AND a normalizes-to-empty input — a test suite that only ever passes an already-clean value cannot catch either regression (confirmed: a mutant dropping normalization entirely survived the first cut's own test suite untouched).

- **Two INDEPENDENT detectors for the SAME real-world event (a TRANSCRIPT-based one and a PANE-based one) that never hand off to each other is a recurring watchdog shape — the transcript side often pings once and gives up ("only the reset clock fixes it"), silently trusting the pane side to finish the job, and the pane side's own entry gate can structurally never see the event at all.** #336 (job 1's `is_usage_cap` transcript detection vs job 6's `pane_session_limited` pane-only entry gate): a session-limit hit that lands on a background Agent/subagent (not the top-level session's own foreground turn) settles at an ordinary idle prompt WITHOUT ever rendering the banner as the pane's bottom-most content, so `pane_session_limited` can legitimately never fire for that pane — job 1 pings once and marks the episode permanently dormant, job 6 never engages, the session stalls until a human types `continue` by hand (the real montalu2 incident). Fix pattern, reusable whenever a NEW ticket finds this shape elsewhere in this file's many watchdog jobs: have the TRANSCRIPT-side detector seed the PANE-side job's OWN tracking state directly (parsing whatever resume-time/target info it needs straight from the transcript TEXT, no pane/box scoping needed for that parse), and widen the pane-side job's entry gate from "pane shows it" to "pane shows it OR my own state already exists" — never invent a second parallel mechanism. Resolution/cleanup must ALSO stop depending solely on "the pane-visible signal went away" (a transcript-only episode never had one to lose, so that alone would leak the state forever) — re-derive resolution from the SAME transcript source whenever the pane doesn't currently show the signal.
- **Claude Code's own `/exit` transcript marker is a COMPOSITE string, not the bare `<command-name>/exit</command-name>` tag alone — a strict-equality match against just the bare tag NEVER matches a real transcript, silently making the whole detector inert.** #336 (`session_user_stopped`, the narrow session-limit-scoped counterpart of #335's own general user-stop invariant — same underlying gap the design comment on #335 also needs to watch for): verified live against real transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` — the real `message.content` for a user's `/exit` is `"<command-name>/exit</command-name>\n            <command-message>exit</command-message>\n            <command-args></command-args>"`. Match by PREFIX (`content.lstrip().startswith("<command-name>/exit</command-name>")`), anchored on the closing tag so a differently-named command sharing the substring still can't false-match — never `content.strip() == "<command-name>/exit</command-name>"`. Any FUTURE code detecting `/exit` (or any other slash command) from a transcript should grep real `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` files for the actual composite shape BEFORE writing the match, rather than assuming the bare tag this repo's own test fixtures elsewhere sometimes use (`_user(...)` in `test_question_prune.py`, `<command-name>/compact</command-name>`) is what a real session ever writes.

- **A worktree-dispatched worker can be forked from a point in `main` history that PREDATES a sibling ticket its OWN dispatch prompt assumes has already landed — a commit message reading "Closes #N" on an UNMERGED branch does NOT mean the issue is closed on GitHub, and only a live `gh issue view <N> --json state` check catches the gap.** #337: the ticket's own body described S-F1's CURRENT behaviour as already "capture-pane primary, claude-history fallback" (the sibling #327 ticket's own design) — but `gh issue view 327` returned `state: OPEN` throughout, and `git log`/`git branch --contains` confirmed that work sat entirely on a separate, unmerged worktree branch never reachable from this worker's own base commit. A `git cherry-pick --no-commit`/merge of that sibling branch WOULD have applied cleanly (verified as a dry-run) but was deliberately rejected: the sibling branch was itself STALE relative to current `main` (missing several later, unrelated files — `design_gate.py`, several hooks — confirmed via `git diff main <sibling-branch> --stat`), and merging a sibling ticket's own unmerged work is scope creep on a ticket this worker was never dispatched to finish. The safe resolution: build ONLY the shared primitive the CURRENT ticket structurally needs (here, a capture-pane-reading fallback for S-DC's OWN new mode), reusing already-validated tmux mechanism knowledge already recorded in THIS file (the bare-capture-pane-resolves-to-originating-pane finding, the `$VAR`-expansion-at-conf-parse-time landmine, the `_tmux_conf_quote` refuse-rather-than-guess shape) rather than depending on the sibling's own unmerged, possibly-drifting branch — and document the discrepancy explicitly in the STEP 0 validation comment posted to the ticket, rather than silently proceeding on the ticket body's stale assumption or silently expanding scope to also finish the sibling. Reuse this check (issue state via `gh`, not local commit-message text) whenever a dispatched worker's own ticket body describes a "current" repo state that a quick `grep`/`git log` scan can't actually confirm is live on `main`.

- **A recency clamp comparing a timestamp against a `now` captured ONCE at the START of a multi-stage sweep — never re-captured at the moment of comparison — must be SYMMETRIC (`-window_s <= age < window_s`), not merely non-negative (`0 <= age < window_s`); this is the OPPOSITE clamp direction from the existing #177 `min()` lesson, and the two must not be conflated.** #339: `_goal_autoarm_recent_human_activity` (job 9's virgin-arm presence gate) captures `now` once in `run_once`, but runs at the per-pane sweep's TAIL — so a human's own FIRST prompt, written after `now` was captured but before this specific pane's turn arrives in the loop, stamps a marker/transcript timestamp genuinely NEWER than `now`. Under an asymmetric `0 <= age` clamp that negative `age` reads as "not recent", reproducing (live, adversarial-review-verified) the exact incident the whole check exists to prevent — a virgin `/goal` armed into a conversation that had, in wall-clock terms, already started. Fix: widen to `-window_s <= age < window_s` — a prompt up to `window_s` in the future still counts as recent (absorbs the drift), while a value grossly beyond the window (real clock skew, a transcript synced off another box) is still refused; being generous on the future side only ever makes the check MORE cautious, never less, since a false "recent" here just delays an arm rather than causing a wrong one. Contrast explicitly with #177's `_last_real_turn_ts`/`min()` clamp elsewhere in this file, which guards the OPPOSITE consumer (a future-dated value must never look artificially YOUNGER for a staleness/dark-episode check, where treating a bogus future timestamp as "fresh" would WRONGLY suppress an escalation) — the two clamps solve mirror-image problems for mirror-image reasons, and copying one shape onto the other's problem is exactly the bug this ticket shipped once already. Before writing ANY `now - ts` bound, ask (a) is `now` captured at the SAME instant as the comparison, or earlier in a multi-step process, and (b) does the caller need "genuinely can't be in the future" (staleness, #177's shape) or "recent, but tolerant of `now` being slightly stale itself" (activity/attendance, #339's shape) — the two need opposite-signed lower bounds.

- **`watchdog/__init__.py` has TWO independent "is this transcript entry human-typed vs machine-injected" classifiers, and they had drifted out of sync — `_MACHINE_PROMPT_PREFIXES` (near line 2396, feeding `_last_human_prompt_ts`) lacked the `"Stop hook feedback:"` prefix that `_STOP_FEEDBACK_PREFIX` (defined independently ~5000 lines later, for job 20/26's compact-boundary code) already carries.** #339-review MINOR: a routine Stop-hook-rejected turn (a real, common transcript entry — see the #108/#109 corpus measurements elsewhere in this file) was silently counted as human activity by `_last_human_prompt_ts`, which could delay any consumer relying on "no recent human prompt" (here, job 9's virgin-arm gate) for as long as that consumer's own window. Fixed by adding the literal prefix to `_MACHINE_PROMPT_PREFIXES` directly (duplicated, not import-shared — `_STOP_FEEDBACK_PREFIX` is defined too far below in the file to reference from this earlier definition point without reordering unrelated code around it). Before adding or auditing either classifier, grep for BOTH `_MACHINE_PROMPT_PREFIXES` and `_STOP_FEEDBACK_PREFIX` and confirm they still agree on what counts as "machine-injected, not human" — a new machine-injected prompt shape (a future watchdog job, a new slash-command echo) needs adding to both, or the two consumers will silently disagree again.
- **A real `/exit` slash command is NOT one transcript entry — it is a TRIPLE of `type=="user"` entries (a `<local-command-caveat>`, the actual `<command-name>/exit</command-name>` entry, and a `<local-command-stdout>Bye!`/`Goodbye!` entry), and the caveat/stdout companions routinely carry a timestamp a few MILLISECONDS NEWER than the exit command entry itself (same write batch) — any "release this suppression once a later real turn exists" check built on a BROAD reader (one that counts ANY `type in ("user","assistant")` entry, e.g. job 10's own `_last_real_turn_ts`) reads the exit's own bookkeeping as genuine post-exit activity and releases the very instant it is written.** #335-review round 2 (fresh-context adversarial review, executed proof over the real local transcript corpus): this defeated a round-1 `/exit`-release fix on 13 of 15 genuinely-exited-and-never-resumed sessions — a millisecond-timestamp-coincidence lottery, not a real release condition. Fix: a DEDICATED sibling reader (`_last_real_turn_ts_excluding_command_bookkeeping`, watchdog/__init__.py -- deleted by #403 once its own only caller, `_goal_was_cleared_by_user`, was itself deleted along with the rest of the heuristic re-arm machinery; the REUSABLE LESSON below still applies to any future consumer) that excludes entries whose content STARTS WITH `<local-command-caveat>`/`<local-command-stdout>`, used ONLY by the exit-release check — never widen the shared `_last_real_turn_ts` itself, since job 10 genuinely wants those companions counted as activity for its own, different (#177 idle-clock) purpose. Round 3's real-corpus replay confirmed the fix: the old broad reader released 24/28 real exit-carrying transcripts, the new dedicated one only 5/28 (all genuinely-resumed sessions), 0 false releases. Reuse this dedicated-narrow-reader shape for ANY future "release a suppression on later real activity" check built near a slash command whose own write leaves companion bookkeeping entries in the transcript — grep the REAL corpus for the command's actual shape before trusting a broad "any real turn" reader is safe to reuse.
- **"Two rounds if round 1 found anything substantive" is a FLOOR, not a ceiling — when round 2's own review of round 1's fix ALSO finds something substantive (not just a residual MINOR), dispatch a round 3, and keep going until a round returns genuinely clean.** #335: round 1 found 1 CRITICAL + 2 MAJOR (fixed); round 2, reviewing that fix, found the SAME CRITICAL finding (F1) still genuinely broken by a different mechanism (a millisecond-timestamp companion-entry trap, see the bullet above) plus one new MINOR; round 3, reviewing THAT fix, finally returned SAFE TO SHIP AS-IS with only cosmetic doc-precision notes. A P0 user-trust invariant that has already needed two rounds of correction is exactly the population where a third subtle gap is NOT surprising — never stop at "two rounds" as a fixed count when the second round's own verdict was still NOT SAFE TO SHIP.

- **A "next char is not literal X" negative-lookahead SUBSTITUTE (needed because `grep -E`/POSIX ERE has no real lookahead) only excludes the ONE character shape you named — it is a strictly narrower exclusion than "does not equal this specific longer string", and the gap between the two is a real false-negative class, not a cosmetic difference.** #331-review F1: `subdev-handoff-match.sh`'s `**GATEKEEPER` opening-comment exclusion was written as `^[[:space:]]*\*\*GATEKEEPER([^-]|$)` — "the character right after GATEKEEPER is not a hyphen, or end of line" — deliberately shaped that way so the ONE legitimate hyphenated marker (`**GATEKEEPER-ACTION:**`) would still be admitted. But the same narrow carve-out ALSO admits every OTHER hyphenated opening (`**GATEKEEPER-BOUNCE**`, live-reproduced: rc=0, a genuine gatekeeper comment gets mislabeled `ready-for-review`), because "next char is not a hyphen" says nothing about what follows a hyphen that IS there. The fix pattern, reusable for any future "exclude X except for one specific longer string that starts with X" rule: split into TWO greps — a POSITIVE match on the general exclusion pattern, AND a NEGATED match on the exact longer string to carve back out (`grep -qE '^...GENERAL' <<<"$X" && ! grep -qE '^...SPECIFIC-EXCEPTION' <<<"$X"`) — never try to encode the carve-out as a single character-class tweak on the general pattern, since a single-character lookahead-substitute can only ever describe what the VERY NEXT character is, not what a whole subsequent token is. Found by a fresh-context adversarial review (dispatched with a REFUTE framing + an explicit "construct an input that breaks this either direction" instruction) that live-reproduced the gap against the real shipped script rather than reasoning about the regex in the abstract — mutation-tested the fix afterward (backup, revert to the old regex, confirm the new test fails, restore) to confirm genuine teeth.
- **A regression test locking an already-correct scoping decision (e.g. "this exclusion check only ever reads FIRST_LINE, never the whole BODY") needs its OWN dedicated fixture proving the scoping matters — a full green test suite proves nothing about it if no existing fixture happens to exercise the shape the scoping was chosen to protect against.** #331-review F3: a mutant swapping `<<<"$FIRST_LINE"` for `<<<"$BODY"` in the exclusion check passed all 21 pre-existing tests silently (none placed a `**GATEKEEPER`-shaped line anywhere past line 1 of a genuine hand-off), while it would have dropped a real hand-off comment whose body quotes a gatekeeper reply on a later line. The shipped code was ALREADY correct — this was a pure coverage gap, closed with one new fixture (`READY-FOR-REVIEW: done\n**GATEKEEPER quoted verdict below` → rc=0) and confirmed via the same backup/mutate/confirm-fails/restore technique. Whenever a review names a scoping choice as correct-but-untested, add the ONE fixture that would fail under the "un-scope it" mutant — don't skip it just because the current behavior is already right.
- **A `Closes #N` trailer already baked into a PUSHED-TO-BRANCH (but not yet merged/pushed-to-main) commit, discovered SEVERAL commits later (in adversarial review) to be premature — because the ticket has a genuine, structurally-post-push-only residual (e.g. a live Discord delivery proof that needs the account's `~/.claude` actually deployed) — cannot be fixed by editing that commit (`commit-conventions.md` bans amend/rebase on this repo, and by the time it's discovered, later commits already sit on top of it).** #326-review MAJOR: this repo auto-closes an issue from ANY commit message reachable at push/merge time (line ~262/~525 above — no PR needed for a direct-push repo, and even in a PR-based flow GitHub scans every commit in the PR, not just the PR body), so a trailer written 2-3 commits before a MAJOR finding surfaces is already unrecoverable text. The correct mitigation, once this happens: (1) a loud, explicit residual comment at the CODE site the incomplete item lives at (so a future reader hits it without needing the issue thread), (2) an equally loud review comment on the issue itself instructing whoever integrates — "if this auto-closes on merge/push, REOPEN it, perform the missing live verification, comment the evidence, THEN close it again" — and (3) the SAME instruction repeated in the worker's own evidence block to the supervisor, since that is the one channel guaranteed to be read before the push actually happens. The lesson for the NEXT ticket: don't write `Closes #N` on ANY commit until you are certain every item — including one that's structurally unprovable from a worktree — is either done or has been explicitly, consciously deferred with the supervisor's own post-push step named; a mid-branch RED/GREEN pair's trailer placement (the existing #150 bullet above) is the SAME root discipline, just for the smaller within-one-ticket case.
- **A `until COND; do sleep N; done` loop wrapped inside `timeout N bash -c '...'` gets PAST the worktree-isolation checker even though the #256 entry above found bare `while`/arithmetic-bound `for` loops REFUSED — the wrapping shape, not just the loop's own internal syntax, appears to matter to whatever heuristic decides "too complex to verify stays inside the worktree".** #332 resume: waiting foreground for a dispatched async `Agent` review (the same SubagentStop "in-flight background work" gate #256 already documents) needed a genuine blocking wait; `timeout 550 bash -c 'until grep -qE "^(OK|FAILED)" <output-file>; do sleep 5; done; echo TERMINAL; tail -20 <output-file>'` passed cleanly on the first try — no refusal, no `# airuleset:poll-ok` bypass needed. This refines, not contradicts, #256's own advice: the PLAIN literal-list `for` loop is still the one proven safest when the exact wrapping shape is untested, but a `timeout N bash -c '<until-loop>'` invocation is a second, now-also-proven-safe shape worth reaching for when the wait needs to poll a FILE/artifact (rather than count fixed iterations) — e.g. waiting on a background task's own output file reaching a terminal marker, which a fixed-iteration `for` loop can't express as naturally.

- **A two-hop admin ssh command (dev1 -> gatekeeper -> root@subdev) built as a SINGLE command line, with the payload passed via an INLINE `$(base64 -w0 <local-file>)` command substitution embedded directly in the ssh argument, passes the worktree-isolation checker cleanly — but the SAME content split into a separate `VAR=$(...)` assignment statement followed by a `; ssh ...` use statement gets refused as "too complex to verify stays inside the worktree", even though neither shape touches any git state at all.** #326 reopen residual (2026-08-09): writing a new file into three subdev accounts (`echo "$(base64 -w0 provision.sh)" | ssh gatekeeper "ssh root@subdev 'echo <inline> | base64 -d | bash'"`, all on one line, no `B64=...` variable) worked on the first try; the identical payload staged as `B64=$(base64 -w0 provision.sh)` on its own line followed by a semicolon-separated ssh call was refused outright. The general shape this reconfirms (see #256/#332 above, same worktree checker, different trigger): a compound STATEMENT SEQUENCE (assignment; use) is more likely to be judged "too complex" than the equivalent single EXPRESSION with the substitution folded inline — prefer inlining a `$(...)` directly into the command that consumes it over a separate `VAR=...` line, for ANY future worktree-dispatched command that needs to pass sizable data through a nested-ssh admin hop.
- **Gatekeeper's own sshd (100.90.94.41) can show genuinely INTERMITTENT `kex_exchange_identification: Connection reset by peer` failures, uncorrelated with the failing command's length or complexity — a bare `echo OK` failed seconds after an elaborate nested-ssh command had just succeeded, and vice versa, on the identical target.** #326 reopen residual: most plausible cause is concurrent load on the shared admin path from other fleet-dispatch workers hitting gatekeeper/subdev at the same time (this repo's own #332/#318 entries above already document sibling-worker contention on this exact hop) — NOT a persistent block, since plain reachability checks kept alternating success/failure across the session. Recovery: a bounded poll-wait for reachability BEFORE each retry (`timeout N bash -c 'until ssh -o BatchMode=yes gatekeeper "echo OK" 2>/dev/null; do sleep S; done'`, tagged `# airuleset:poll-ok <reason>` since `block-local-poll-repeat.sh` fires on a repeated identical wait shape per #119) — never a blind hammer-retry, and never conclude "the whole hop is broken" from one or two resets before waiting it out once.
- **A `TODO-PROVISIONING.md` file left directly in a newly-provisioned sub-dev stream account's home directory (by gatekeeper, per the odoo-erp `/new-subdev` recipe) is a durable, authoritative, first-read source for "what remains to be provisioned on this account" — check it via the admin hop BEFORE trusting a ticket's own stale reopen-comment diagnosis.** #326 reopen residual: the reopen comment's own text said "the OS accounts do not exist yet" (true at push time), but by the time this worker ran, gatekeeper had already provisioned david2/david3/david4 far past that point (Linux account, home skeleton, `~/devel/airuleset`/`~/devel/odoo` checkouts, tmux session, claude binary, gh-app token) — and each account's OWN `TODO-PROVISIONING.md` explicitly named the SAME ticket (`#326`) as the one remaining blocker, with a precise "Working and verified — do not re-request" vs "Still pending" split. Reading that file first (rather than re-deriving provisioning state from scratch, or trusting the issue thread's own now-stale comment) is what correctly narrowed this ticket's real remaining scope from "provision the OS accounts" down to "add the missing `authorized_keys` entry" in one read.
- **The single sanctioned post-provisioning verification ssh (`ssh -i .../gatekeeper_access_ed25519 <user>@subdev 'echo ok'`, direct from dev1) getting an IMMEDIATE `Connection refused` (not a timeout) on the very FIRST account attempted is the expected shape of a real, already-active subdev fail2ban ban on dev1's source IP — stop there, do not attempt the remaining accounts, do not retry.** #326 reopen residual: this exact sequence played out live — dev1's IP had already tripped the ban earlier in the session from probing the (at-the-time) nonexistent accounts, and the very first `david2@subdev` attempt refused immediately (a REJECT-style TCP RST, not a hang) while the identical admin-hop path (gatekeeper -> root@subdev, a DIFFERENT source IP) worked throughout. Per this repo's own established fail2ban discipline (see the `machine-identities.md`-adjacent entries above), never chase this with a second/third account attempt or a raw-IP retry hoping DNS/routing is the real cause — a single refused attempt after successful provisioning is sufficient signal; rely on the admin-hop's own structural verification (file perms/ownership/key-fingerprint match) instead, and leave the genuine end-to-end connectivity proof for whenever the ban clears (or for the supervisor's own push, which may share dev1's IP and hit the identical wall until then).
- **`block-local-poll-repeat.sh`'s digit-blind shape hash treats a SECOND wait as a "repeat" even when it is for a COMPLETELY UNRELATED resource — a proven-safe `timeout N bash -c 'until COND; do sleep S; done'` wait for a background test suite, followed later in the SAME session by the identical wrapper shape used to wait for a dispatched async `Agent` review, got refused as a repeat on the SECOND use and needed the `# airuleset:poll-ok <reason>` bypass appended as a trailing bash comment on the command line.** #309: a `DEADLINE=$((SECONDS+N)); while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$DEADLINE" ]; do sleep S; done` shape (no `timeout N bash -c` wrapper — the deadline arithmetic and `while` sat directly at the top level) was ALSO separately refused, by the WORKTREE checker this time ("too complex to verify stays inside the worktree") — confirming #256/#332's finding that the `timeout N bash -c '<string>'` wrapper is what keeps the worktree checker satisfied, not just a preference. What worked cleanly on the third attempt: `timeout 555 bash -c 'until [ -f /tmp/some-never-true-marker ]; do sleep 25; done'  # airuleset:poll-ok <reason>` — a single self-bounded sleep-only wait with an intentionally-unreachable condition (since a dispatched Agent's own completion is delivered as an automatic `<task-notification>` system event, not something Bash can observe without reading the subagent's own transcript file, which is explicitly off-limits — "do NOT peek"). Reusable pattern for any future "wait for an async subagent, but don't touch its output file" need: a bounded sleep-only `until false-ish; do sleep S; done` loop wrapped in `timeout N bash -c '...'`, bypass-marked from the start if a same-shaped wait already ran once this session for ANY purpose.
- **A one-time-CREATE sentinel gate sitting in front of a function must be scoped to ONLY the decision it exists to protect — a downstream, logically UNRELATED read-only check placed textually after the gate inherits its scope by accident unless the refactor deliberately separates the two.** #309: `ensure_stream_tmux_session`'s `if sentinel.exists(): return early` was written (in #263) to gate ONE decision (never resurrect a session the user deliberately killed) but, being the function's first statement, ALSO silently gated a completely different, later-added (#308) read-only cwd-mismatch WARNING that has nothing to do with the create decision at all — making that check permanently inert for the whole fleet the moment any account's sentinel existed. The fix: read the sentinel into a local flag ONCE, at entry, and consult that flag ONLY at the two points that genuinely need it (whether to WRITE it this call, and which message to return for a no-mutation case) — never let an early `return` on the flag be the thing that ALSO skips unrelated logic added later. Whenever adding a new check to a function that already has an early-return gate near its top, ask explicitly whether the new check's own condition is a SUBSET of what the gate protects, or a genuinely independent concern that the gate is only accidentally blocking.
- **Widening an exclusion by ANOTHER opening SIGNATURE (a heading form alongside an existing bold form) is a SEPARATE OR'd grep, never a tweak folded into the original regex — and the same mutation-invisible FIRST_LINE-scoping gap #331's own F3 finding already fixed for the bold branch RECURRED, untested, for the new heading branch, on the SAME file, one ticket later.** #340: `subdev-handoff-match.sh`'s bold-only `**GATEKEEPER` exclusion missed a real markdown-HEADING gatekeeper opening (`## Gatekeeper review — BOUNCE`, odoo-erp#2878) — fixed as `{bold-check} || heading-check`, exact corpus casing ("Gatekeeper" title case, never bare "GATEKEEPER") so a sub-dev's own heading-decorated ALL-CAPS marker never collides. A fresh-context adversarial review then found the NEW heading grep's own FIRST_LINE scope was mutation-invisible (swap `<<<"$FIRST_LINE"` for `<<<"$BODY"`, all tests still pass) — the identical class #331's F3 already taught this exact file to guard against, just not re-applied to the SECOND branch added a ticket later. **Whenever adding a SIBLING branch to an already-hardened check, re-apply EVERY scoping/casing lock the FIRST branch already earned from its own prior review — a lock on branch A does not transfer to branch B just because they share a file and an `if`.**
- **A "requires the corpus's EXACT casing" design decision (chosen specifically to avoid a false-positive collision, e.g. title-case "Gatekeeper" vs ALL-CAPS "GATEKEEPER") needs a locking fixture from BOTH sides — a fixture proving the ALL-CAPS marker still works does NOT also prove the LOWERCASE/case-insensitive widening is caught; mutating to `-i` survived the whole suite until the missing-side fixture was added.** #340-review F5: same shape as #331's own F3 (a correct design choice with an asymmetric test), just on the case-sensitivity axis instead of the FIRST_LINE-vs-BODY axis — whenever a regex's safety rests on "matches THIS casing, not THAT one", lock BOTH the case that must match and the case that must NOT.
- **When a fresh-context adversarial review returns MINOR findings that ARE live-triggered (a real input reaching the real script) but the shape has NEVER been observed in the actual corpus, this repo's own established precedent (#331's own F4) is to document them as ACCEPTED RESIDUALS rather than expand the regex pre-emptively — consistent with the active FREEZE ("fix only what has actually failed in production").** #340-review F3/F4: a bold-inside-heading hybrid (`## **Gatekeeper review**`) and an all-caps-heading hybrid (`## GATEKEEPER review`) both still escape the new exclusion; neither shape appears anywhere in the repo's own corpus evidence. Left untouched, named explicitly in the ticket's review comment and in code, rather than chasing every combinatorial hybrid of two already-recognized signatures.
- **Waiting foreground for a dispatched async `Agent` review from inside a worker: a bare `sleep N && echo ...` chain is blocked by a SEPARATE harness-level guard ("Long leading sleep commands are blocked... use Monitor with an until-loop") that is DISTINCT from `block-local-poll-repeat.sh`'s repeat-shape detector — hitting one does not mean the other is also satisfied, and the fix for each is different.** #341: after the established literal-list `for i in 1 2 3 ...; do sleep 30; done` loop shape (per the existing #256/#332 entries above) had already been used twice and got refused by the airuleset repeat-poll hook on its third identical use, switching to a bare `sleep 570 && echo WAIT_DONE` (reasoning "a different shape won't trip the SAME repeat detector") was immediately refused by this SECOND, independent guard instead. The fix that satisfies both: go BACK to the proven literal-list `for`/`sleep` loop shape (never a bare `sleep`) and add the `# airuleset:poll-ok <reason>` bypass comment once it becomes a repeat within the session — this loop shape is the one surface that has now been confirmed to satisfy the worktree-isolation checker, the repeat-poll hook (with its documented bypass), AND the harness's own long-sleep guard, all three, across several tickets.

- **When trimming a trailing sentence-BOUNDARY from prose (a coda-strip, an ablation cut), distinguish CONNECTOR punctuation (`;`, an em-dash `—`) from TERMINATOR punctuation (`.`) — only the connector should be dropped from the result, since a terminator is legitimately still part of the sentence being kept, while a connector left dangling reads as a grammatical leftover.** #95 item 11 adversarial review: `strip_rewordings_coda()` only `.rstrip()`d whitespace after its boundary cut, so a real corpus line ending "...is banned; applies to all rewordings..." ablated to "...is banned;" (a semicolon with nothing left to connect to), while "...Do the real thing. Applies to..." correctly kept its own period. Fix: after the whitespace-rstrip, loop-strip trailing `;`/`—` characters specifically (re-rstripping after each), leaving `.` untouched by construction — reuse this discriminator (connector vs. terminator) for any future "cut prose at its nearest sentence boundary" helper in this repo.
- **A corpus-replay script's own PRE-FILTER (deciding which real transcript entries are even worth feeding to the real hook/classifier) must mirror that hook's FULL branch precedence, not just its keyword-based common case — a narrower pre-filter silently UNDER-samples the population, which looks like a clean measurement while quietly excluding real cases the hook itself would recognize.** #95 item 9 adversarial review: `measure_question_quality_baseline.py`'s marker pre-filter required the literal words "NEEDS YOU"/"ASKED" in the text, but `hooks/stop-check-question-quality.sh` ALSO recognizes a bare `❓` opening a message's own last non-blank line with NEITHER keyword present (its second, undocumented-in-prose branch) — grepping the hook's own source for its literal `ASKED_RX`/`LAST_LINE` regexes (never guessing from the module doc) and reimplementing BOTH branches in the pre-filter closed the gap. Same shape as #146's connect-a-stateful-hook-with-a-fresh-session-id-per-call litter lesson: this baseline script ALSO leaked one `/tmp` retry-marker per BLOCKED sample (~766/run) since none of the hook's own self-cleanup paths ever trigger for a never-reused session id — fixed by having the caller remove its own marker after each call, loudly on a genuine (non-"already gone") removal failure, never a silent `except: pass`.

- **Extending the #199 "empty read is not a genuine negative" lesson to an APPEARANCE/DISAPPEARANCE state machine: a single blip reading "absent" must be debounced across >=2 CONSECUTIVE sweeps before being trusted as a real transition, not just checked for a measurement-failure sentinel.** #353's `pane_absent_pending` mechanism (job 11's gk-request backstop) needed this because `list_claude_panes` can legitimately return an accurate-but-transient empty result even when the underlying tmux read genuinely succeeded (the pane briefly vanishing from `list-panes -a` during a resize/detach churn) — a DIFFERENT hazard from #199's own finding (a `run()` call itself FAILING and returning `""`). A fresh-context adversarial review caught the single-sweep version as MAJOR-1, live-reproduced with a synthetic single-blip fixture. General shape: any appear<->disappear dedup/reset decision built on a poll needs its OWN debounce counter (2 consecutive confirmations), independent of whatever measurement-failure guard already covers the underlying read.
- **A caller implementing its OWN staged/backoff re-notification schedule (24h -> 3d -> 7d, etc.) must make the downstream `notify.send()` `dedup_key` FRESH PER DECISION INSTANT, never derived from unchanging ticket/content identity — an unrelated, independently-owned dedup layer (`notify.send`'s own 14-day marker TTL) will otherwise silently swallow every scheduled reping after the first, defeating the caller's own escalation while the caller's OWN state (`reping_count`) keeps correctly climbing and LOOKS like it's working.** #353 (job 11's gk-request backstop): the fix keys the Discord dedup on `"gkreq:%s:%d" % (name, int(now))` — this function's own staged-schedule logic is now the SOLE authority on whether a send is due, so the content-based dedup one layer down must never independently veto it. Reusable whenever a NEW watchdog job builds its own staged-backoff schedule (mirroring #352's job-4 pattern) on top of `notify.send()`: check whether `notify.send`'s own unrelated dedup TTL can silently override the new schedule, and if so, make the key vary per real send decision.
- **A review's own "I fixed both/all remaining call sites" completeness claim can itself be off-by-one — a SECOND, independent fresh-context review found a THIRD `deliver_with_stash` call site (`_goal_template_drift`'s "goal-drift, stash" branch) still omitting `sleep_fn` after the FIRST review's fix commit explicitly claimed "both remaining call sites" were now threaded.** #354: neither review was wrong to have found what it found — the first review named 2 sites, they were fixed, and the commit message's own "both remaining" phrasing was accurate to what THAT review had actually enumerated, just not to the true population. `grep -n "deliver_with_stash(" <file>` across the WHOLE file (not just the sites a review happened to name) is the cheap check before trusting any "N remaining sites, now all fixed" claim. **But the grep only tells you WHERE — it does not tell you whether threading matters there**: a 4th un-threaded site found this way (job 1's api-error auto-resume, `run_once`'s own `deliver_with_stash(pid, NUDGE_TEXT, ...)`, `NUDGE_TEXT = "continue"`) was correctly left alone — an 8-character fixed payload has no realistic exposure to the render-lag class this ticket's fix addresses (which scales with backspace/paste SIZE), even though its enclosing function (`run_once`) genuinely DOES have a `sleep_fn` param it could thread. Run the grep to find every candidate; judge each candidate against the ACTUAL hazard the fix addresses (payload size here) before deciding it needs the same treatment — matching this ticket's own two prior correctly-scoped exclusions (three sibling call sites whose enclosing functions have no `sleep_fn` param at all).
- **`EnterWorktree` REFUSES to create a new worktree from a subagent whose cwd is already pinned by the DISPATCH's own `isolation: "worktree"`/explicit-`cwd` mechanism — even when the actual filesystem shows no real worktree was ever created for this subagent and its Bash `pwd` resolves to the plain shared main checkout.** #366: a worker dispatched with the documented "MODE — worktree isolation" instructions found its own `pwd`/`git branch --show-current` pointing at the SHARED main tree (no `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` path, `git worktree list` showing no entry for this agent) — yet `EnterWorktree({name: "..."})` errored with "cannot create a worktree from a subagent with a cwd override ... spawn an Agent with `cwd` set to it", confirming the harness DOES consider this subagent cwd-pinned even though the pin evidently pointed at the main tree rather than a real isolated worktree (a batch-dispatch/harness inconsistency, not something a worker can fix). Workaround that fully satisfies "work in an isolated git worktree, never touch the shared tree": create one manually via plain `git worktree add <path> -b <branch> origin/main` (or `origin/<base>`) — this is genuine git, not the `EnterWorktree` TOOL, and works from any cwd — then do ALL file edits via the Edit/Write tools using the worktree's ABSOLUTE paths and ALL git operations via `git -C <worktree-path> ...`/`git -C <worktree-path> commit`, never a bare `cd` into it (the Bash tool's cwd resets between calls anyway for a pinned subagent, and mixing `cd` with `git -C` invites exactly the kind of drift this pattern avoids). Report the manually-created worktree's path + branch name in the final evidence block exactly as the worktree-mode template asks, since it IS a genuine, valid git worktree — just created by hand instead of by the tool.

- **When a mid-ticket coordinator redirect makes a PREVIOUSLY-primary piece of already-shipped work no longer the primary answer, REFRAME it as an explicit, honestly-scoped FALLBACK rather than deleting it or leaving its old "primary" framing in place — and then grep the WHOLE file for every OTHER comment that still asserts the old primacy, not just the one the redirect was about.** #376: a coordinator relay (independently re-verified against the real GitHub ticket comment text via `gh api` before acting on it, per this repo's own standing "verify relayed instructions" discipline — never trusted as paraphrase alone) redirected the ticket mid-flight from "fix the popup's rendering" to "fullscreen's own native scrollback is the primary answer; the popup becomes a fallback for what fullscreen structurally cannot show (cross-session, post-exit history)." The already-shipped popup work (binding collapse, transcript-primary reconstruction) kept its real, narrower role and its own tests, docstring, and script header comment were all updated to say FALLBACK explicitly — but TWO SEPARATE comment blocks elsewhere in the same file, written before the redirect, still asserted the old renderer choice "is NEVER the default" and cited a function name that no longer existed, directly contradicting the ticket's own new primary fix. A single `grep -n "never.*default\|becomes the default"` swept the WHOLE file and found both stale blocks in one pass — reuse that sweep (or an equivalent phrase search for whatever claim the redirect reverses) whenever a design pivot changes what's "primary": the reframed piece of code is usually caught by its own tests, but a comment BLOCK three functions away making the old claim is not.
- **A deliberate escape-hatch/grace-timer mechanism ("force delivery once deferred longer than N minutes on live background tasks", #250) has an inherent design blind spot: its discharge fires at the FIRST moment its own defer-condition clears — and in a busy multi-worker batch session, that instant is disproportionately likely to coincide with the user's own reply landing (a dispatch just finished, the session goes briefly idle right as a human answer arrives), because both events share the same underlying cause (something just finished).** #377: neither of the two `/compact` delivery points (job 14's polled boundary check, the synchronous `deliver_compact_now`) had ANY human-presence check at all — the grace-timer's own discharge was the ONLY thing deciding "now", with nothing checking whether "now" was also mid-Q&A. Whenever a grace/backoff timer's discharge condition is "the OTHER blocking condition just cleared", ask whether that clearing event itself correlates with a state the discharge must never fire into — if so, the discharge point needs its OWN independent precondition check, not just the original request's.
- **Extending an existing human-vs-machine transcript classifier (`_last_human_prompt_ts`'s `_MACHINE_PROMPT_PREFIXES` exclusion set) for a NEW caller that needs a DIFFERENT answer to the same question — reuse job 9's own established "opposite exclusion set" shape (#350) via a threaded, additive, OPT-IN parameter, never a hardcoded second copy of the prefix logic.** #377: the compact-delivery Q&A-window gate needed Discord-relayed answers to count as "human present" (the opposite of job 9's own correct "did a human type this DIRECTLY" semantics, which the SAME function serves for a different purpose) — `extra_human_prefixes=()` threaded through `_last_human_prompt_ts` → `_goal_autoarm_recent_human_activity` → the new `_compact_recent_human_activity`, default a complete no-op (verified byte-identical for every pre-existing caller, plus a dedicated non-regression test pinning job 9's own default stays untouched). This is the SAME shape #350 already established for a sibling classifier one function over — reuse the parameter-threading pattern rather than re-deriving a parallel exclusion list, and always add the non-regression test proving the DEFAULT caller's behavior is unaffected by the new opt-in.

- **A test that plants a fixture file in a LIVE, producer-consumed /tmp glob (here `/tmp/claude-discord-pending-*`) with an mtime aged past the consumer's grace races the REAL system daemon that sweeps that glob — the api-watchdog timer (60 s cadence) delivered the decoy as a genuine phantom ✅ Discord ping to the user's phone TWICE (notify-delivery.log 2026-08-08T21:27:04 + 2026-08-11T16:20:08) and unlinked it, failing the test ~5% of full-suite runs (the ~3 s decoy lifetime vs the 60 s sweep).** The #377-wave fix (commit `5cf8875`): plant the decoy FRESH and thread `done_grace=-60` through the test's own `_run(**kw)` into `run_once` — the in-process sweep still treats the fresh decoy as aged (RED power for the #293 scoped-`pending_prefix` regression preserved, proven by a subclass runner reproducing the regression: `2 != 1`), while the real watchdog (def-time default grace 120 s, fresh mtime) can never touch it. Note the seam: `done_grace=PENDING_DONE_GRACE` is a DEF-TIME DEFAULT on both `deliver_pending_done` and `run_once`, so `setattr(wd, "PENDING_DONE_GRACE", ...)` provably reaches neither — the kwarg is the only in-process seam.
