General rule for any future test needing an "aged" file in a producer glob a live daemon also consumes: age it IN-PROCESS (a grace kwarg/patch on the code under test), never via `os.utime` on the real shared path.

- **A giant, unsent draft that fills the ENTIRE captured viewport does not just push the real footer chrome BELOW the bottom of the capture — CC can render the `◎ /goal` indicator's own row ABOVE the box's own top border (as the last bit of the conversation/task-list area) rather than below a closing bottom border, and that row keeps LIVE-updating its own elapsed-minutes count between captures a minute apart.** #383, live-verified read-only against camera-box's own actually-stuck job-20 re-arm draft (`tmux capture-pane -p -t %1`, session zbynek-4:0, 176x50, zero keystrokes): the pane's LAST line (of 50) was still wrapped draft continuation with no closing border/statusline/mode-hint anywhere below the box's `❯` head row, while a genuinely live `◎ /goal active (5m)` -> `(6m)` glyph sat several lines ABOVE the top border — structurally outside `pane_goal_armed`'s own search region (`footer = lines[idx+1:]`), which by design must never search ABOVE the box (a session's own conversation legitimately DISCUSSES `◎ /goal` as prose — `test_indicator_in_conversation_text_is_never_armed` already locks that). The bug: non-blank `footer` content was treated as proof the real footer chrome was in view, when it can just be MORE box continuation. Fix: require at least one `footer` row to independently match `_is_bottom_chrome` (excluding a bare border-rule-only match — a lone closing border proves the box closed, never that the STATUSLINE row carrying the glyph is itself visible) as part of an UNBROKEN trailing run walked BACKWARD from the capture's own last line (`_trailing_bottom_chrome`, stopping at the first genuinely non-chrome, non-blank row) — not merely present anywhere in `footer`. The contiguity requirement matters: a fresh-context adversarial review (fable) live-triggered a false-positive where a PASTED QUOTE of a rendered statusline (`I 41 core · str 12  5h 7%(4h)  wk 63%  caveman:lite  ~$12`, scoring 5 `_statusline_hits`) sitting mid-draft, with more draft still following it, was wrongly accepted as "real chrome present" by a flat (non-positional) check — real chrome is always the UNINTERRUPTED tail of a genuine capture, so walking from the bottom and stopping at the first non-chrome row excludes it by construction.
- **`_is_bottom_chrome("")` is `True` by its own documented design ("empty string is chrome") and `_is_border_rule("")` is `False` — so any new caller of `_is_bottom_chrome` that iterates raw (unfiltered-for-blank) capture lines and ALSO excludes bare-border matches must explicitly skip blank rows BEFORE testing them, or a blank row silently becomes "real, non-border chrome" out of pure whitespace.** #383-review Finding 1, mutation-verified: a mutant of `_trailing_bottom_chrome` that dropped its `if not s: continue` line survived every existing `TestPaneGoalIndicator` case (none had a blank row anywhere in a `None`-expecting footer) until a dedicated fixture was added — a multi-paragraph stuck draft (this repo's OWN real ❓ question templates genuinely contain blank lines between paragraphs, `user-questions-slovak.md`) whose capture cuts off right after a paragraph break. Reuse this exact check (mutate away the blank-skip, confirm a real wrong verdict, then lock it with a fixture whose TRAILING rows are blank with only non-chrome content further up) whenever writing a NEW backward/forward walk over `_is_bottom_chrome` that also excludes border-only matches — a bare `if s` PRE-filter on the whole collection is not equivalent once the check becomes positional (order-sensitive), and this repo has now shipped both shapes.

- **A new "prefer detector X's fresh source over the old fallback" branch that RETURNS EARLY when the fresh source is absent creates a NEW, mirror-image regression whenever the detector's OWN docstring already documents a false-positive residual on a DIFFERENT box class — the fix must fall through to the old fallback instead of disabling it outright.** #401-review MAJOR-1: `_gh_env()`'s App-token branch used to `return env` unconditionally the moment `_gh_app_token_dir()/"primary"` had no fresh token yet (timer lag) — but `_is_gh_app_token_box()`'s own docstring ALREADY documents a known residual (a stray/leftover App-token dir can exist on a genuine own-account PAT box, e.g. a misdirected delivery or an App→PAT migration leftover, #356). An unconditional early-return there would silently kill that box's real, working `.git-credentials` PAT auth the instant such a stray dir appears — a brand-new #401-shaped bug, just relocated to the opposite population. Fix: fall through to the SAME fallback logic every other box uses when the fresh source comes up empty; a second, independent belt further down (here: refusing an `x-access-token` username, which can only ever be the App mechanism's own corpse) still protects the ORIGINAL guarantee, so falling through never reintroduces the bug being fixed — it only ever additionally *finds* a real credential when one legitimately exists. **General check before shipping any "prefer fresh, else return early" branch:** re-read the detector's OWN docstring for any already-documented false-positive population, and ask whether an early return there silently disables something that USED TO WORK for that population.
- **A regression test that reuses the SAME masking fixture across two conceptually different assertions proves NOTHING about which of two competing designs was actually shipped, even though it passes.** #401-review MAJOR-2: the original "no token file yet" test seeded `.git-credentials` with an `x-access-token` corpse line — which the code's SECOND, independent belt refuses regardless of whether the FIRST belt falls through to it or returns early — so the test passed identically under BOTH "never fall through" (the bug, MAJOR-1) and "fall through but still refuse" (the fix) designs, because both produce the same observable (`GH_TOKEN` unset) for two entirely different reasons. The fix needed a SECOND fixture — a genuine, non-`x-access-token`-shaped credential — to actually distinguish "the branch fell through and found it" from "the branch never tried". Whenever a fixture could plausibly be refused by a LATER, independent guard regardless of the EARLIER branch's own behavior, that fixture cannot prove the earlier branch's design — pick (or add) a fixture the later guard would NOT catch.
- **A "fresh vs corpse" live-diagnosis technique worth reusing: compare `~/.git-credentials`' mtime/age directly against the sibling live-refresh source's mtime on the SAME real box, twice — once before writing the fix, once after, with zero code deployed in between.** #401: on montalu3@subdev, `.git-credentials` (an `x-access-token` corpse) stayed byte-identical and its mtime never moved across the whole session (2026-08-11 22:31 both times, going from ~11.5h to ~12.9h stale — App tokens live only 60 min, so unconditionally dead either time), while `~/.config/gh-app-tokens/primary` was independently reconfirmed freshly-refreshed (~31 min old, then ~26 min old) by the SAME gatekeeper systemd timer, live, with no action from this session. Re-reading BOTH files' mtimes read-only, before AND after implementing a fix that reads neither differently than before, is a cheap, honest way to reconfirm the diagnosis still holds at the moment of reporting — rather than relying solely on the FIRST read from earlier in the session.
- **Before trusting "leave a credential env-var unset and let a `~/.local/bin/<tool>` PATH shim self-mint it", compare PATH between a plain non-interactive `ssh user@host 'cmd'` invocation and an interactive login shell (`bash -lc`) on the SAME real box — the two can differ in whether `~/.local/bin` is even on PATH at all, which decides whether the shim is reachable from the exact call shape your OWN code uses.** #401: on montalu3@subdev, a bare non-interactive ssh command (the SAME PATH-inheritance shape a Python `subprocess.run(env=os.environ.copy())` call gets whenever the calling process itself was not launched from an interactive login shell) resolved `gh` to `/usr/bin/gh` — the real binary, never the self-minting `~/.local/bin/gh` shim — because that shell never sources `~/.profile`; only `bash -lc` put `~/.local/bin` first. This is why #401's fix reads `~/.config/gh-app-tokens/primary` directly inside `_gh_env()` instead of the simpler "just leave `GH_TOKEN` unset and trust the shim", which the ticket's own body had offered as an equally-valid option — the live PATH comparison is what correctly ruled it out as unreliable for THIS specific call path.


- **Recovering from an accidentally-bundled commit (implementation for fix B landed inside fix A's own commit by mistake) without rewriting history: add a dedicated retroactive test-proof commit that RED-proves against the LAST commit BEFORE the accident** (`git checkout <pre-accident-sha> -- <impl files>`, run the new tests, confirm they fail, then `git checkout HEAD -- <impl files>` to restore) **and GREEN-proves against current HEAD.** #400 Fix 3 used this shape (`1f79a00`, proven RED against `ba7f851`) after `GOAL_REARM_ABSOLUTE_DARK_S`/`GOAL_REARM_MIN_PAYLOAD` landed bundled into Fix 2's own commit (`5b17acf`). "Never rewrite git history" does not mean an accidental bundling goes untested — it means the PROOF has to be constructed around the immutable history instead of editing it.

- **When a mid-session scope change arrives (a coordinator/user message adding new fixes to an in-flight ticket), the design-comment-before-first-commit discipline can slip if implementation starts before the pause-and-post step — catch this by explicitly flagging the deviation on the ticket and in the evidence block rather than silently complying or fabricating a clean timeline.** #400: the coordinator's Fix 5/6 message arrived mid-investigation; the worker went straight into root-causing/implementing before pausing to post the mandated design comment, then posted it retroactively with the deviation stated at the top. An honest retroactive flag costs nothing; a silently-reordered narrative erodes the very audit trail the design-comment rule exists to provide.


- **Getting a genuine RED commit for a test written to prove an EARLIER round's review finding — after the fix and the test were both already drafted together, not test-first — is just `git stash push -- <impl file>`, commit the test-only diff, run the suite to confirm a REAL failure, `git stash pop`, commit the implementation.** Used twice in one session (#365 round-1 and round-2 review-fix commits): this reconstructs the RED→GREEN commit pair `regression-test-first.md` requires even when the actual authoring order was fix-then-test (common when a fix is written in direct response to a review finding whose own report already names the exact broken behavior). The stash/pop is safe here specifically because the test file and the implementation file are DIFFERENT files with no interdependency at commit time — never do this if the test file itself needs the new implementation to even *import* cleanly (that would need a different sequencing, e.g. a throwaway local revert of just the changed lines).

- **A daily-reping/retry loop that reposts content and then unconditionally drops the OLD tracking key on a `"sent"` status is deleting the very thing it is supposed to protect — `"sent"` proves the POST landed, never that the FOLLOW-ON re-tracking succeeded.** #368-review MAJOR-1: `reping_stale_questions` dropped the old question-map key the instant `send()` returned `"sent"`, without checking whether the freshly-posted message id was actually re-recorded — two real, reachable legs make that check necessary: `send()` returns `("sent", None)` when Discord accepts the POST but the response body doesn't parse (a documented `_post_discord` path), and `record_question` itself REFUSES (returns `False`) when the resolved channel is empty/non-numeric. Either way the old entry vanished with the new one never tracked — a silent, one-shot loss of exactly the thing the whole daily-reping mechanism exists to prevent. Fix: capture the SECOND action's own success/failure (`retracked = record_question(...)`) and gate the drop on THAT, never on the first action's status alone. This is the SAME "gate on the artifact the action leaves behind, not the intent to act" lesson (#134) one step removed — here the artifact needed is not the SEND's own success, but the RE-TRACK's.
- **A NEW test class's own `tempfile.mkdtemp()` calls need their OWN `addCleanup`, even when the test's whole POINT is proving an isolation/anti-litter fix — the fix protects the SUBJECT under test, never the test's own scratch-directory bookkeeping.** #385-review MINOR-1 (fresh-context adversarial review, fable-gate OPEN, live-triggered): `TestLockDirEnvOverride`'s own `override`/`repo` directories (`tempfile.mkdtemp()`, no `addCleanup`) leaked one small anonymous `/tmp/tmpXXXXXXXX` scratch dir per test run — a real, if much smaller, counterpart of the exact litter class the ticket existed to fix, hiding inside the very tests written to prove the fix. `self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, path, ignore_errors=True)` on every `mkdtemp()` call in a NEW test closes it; mutation-verified by reverting the `addCleanup` calls (2 leaked dirs reproduced from one run) then restoring them (zero). Grep any NEW test file for `tempfile.mkdtemp()` with no adjacent `addCleanup` before considering the test genuinely hermetic — this is a distinct, narrower check from whether the CODE UNDER TEST itself leaks.
- **`gh`'s ATTACHED short-flag syntax — `-lVALUE` and `-l=VALUE` — is genuinely accepted for a string-valued short flag like `-l`/`--label`, distinct from a truly unknown flag (which `gh` rejects with "unknown shorthand flag") — verified live against the real `gh` binary, not assumed from GNU-getopt convention.** #390's `_explicit_stream_labels` originally recognized only the separate-token (`-l value`) and `--label=value` forms; a fresh-context adversarial review caught that a compliant filer using either attached spelling was FALSE-BLOCKED for its own, correctly-applied label — the wrong direction for a hook whose whole stated bias is "degrade toward allowing, never toward a false block". Any future hook parsing `gh`'s (or any getopt-style CLI's) short flags for a specific value needs all three forms tested (`-l x`, `-lx`, `-l=x`), never just the one that happens to show up in a quick manual check.
- **A gate combining a CHEAP local check with an EXPENSIVE network lookup should put the cheap check first from the FIRST DRAFT, not wait for a review to catch the ordering** — this repo's own `#329` docstring already states the discipline ("cheap local checks before the network call") but nowhere generalizes it as a reusable lesson outside that one hook's comments, so it was re-discovered the hard way on #390's very next new gate. `_stream_routing_block_reason` (the stream-routing gate) called `_repo_is_stream_aware` (a `gh label list` round-trip) BEFORE `_filer_authority_and_own_stream` (a pure-local `getpass`/import check) — so a full-authority filer, who this gate never blocks at all, paid the network cost on every single filing fleet-wide. Fixed by swapping the order and mutation-verifying both call-site tests independently fail when the swap is reverted. Whenever a NEW gate function combines "is this filer/repo/context even in scope" (cheap, local, decides "skip entirely") with "what does the outside world say" (network, slow), write the cheap check FIRST — checking whether the code even needs the expensive answer is itself cheaper than the expensive answer.

- **A scripted mutation applied via `func_src.replace(old, new, 1)` silently mutates the FIRST textual occurrence of `old` in the function, not necessarily the one you intend — when a function legitimately contains the identical statement twice (e.g. `return candidates[0]` appearing once for a `len==1` early-return and again as the diverged-fallback), the mutation can land on the wrong copy and the resulting "kill" is spurious.** #380 review-fix: mutating `_worktree_sweep_base_branch`'s diverged-fallback `return candidates[0]` to prove `test_diverged_dev_and_main_falls_back_to_dev_preference` has teeth first replaced the EARLIER `len(candidates) == 1: return candidates[0]` line instead (both are byte-identical strings), and the test still passed — looking like a successful mutation-kill when it had mutated dead code for that test's own fixture (which always has 2 candidates). Caught only by comparing the mutant's actual diff against the intended target before trusting the "test still passes" / "test now fails" result. Fix: anchor the `old` string on enough SURROUNDING context to be unique in the function (here, the two-line comment immediately preceding the target `return`, not just the bare statement) — `assert func_src.count(old) == 1` before mutating is the cheap guard that catches this before wasting a whole mutation-verification cycle on the wrong line.


- **A concurrent-write tmp-file scheme needs `tempfile.mkstemp()` (unique per call, same dir/filesystem so the later swap stays a true atomic rename) from the FIRST DRAFT — a predictable name (`<name>.gz.tmp`, `<name>.tmp`) is a genuine, live-reproducible data-loss race, not a theoretical one.** #410 review F1 (fresh-context adversarial review, live-reproduced): two concurrent sweeps of the SAME source file, each writing to the identical predictable tmp path, let one writer's still-being-hashed tmp get clobbered by the other — and the first writer's own verify-hash could then match the SECOND writer's (also in-progress) bytes, reporting "compressed" for a `.gz` that does not actually round-trip. Confirmed the fix with a real 20-trial CONCURRENT-THREAD reproduction (two Python `threading.Thread`s racing the real function on the same source, `threading.Barrier` to force overlap) rather than trusting the mkstemp API alone — zero corruption, zero data loss across all 20 trials. Any future "compress/write-then-atomically-swap" function in this repo should reach for `tempfile.mkstemp(dir=..., prefix=..., suffix=...)` by default, never a hand-built predictable name.
- **`design_gate.classify_design_comment()` needs the LITERAL word family from `_APPROACH_RE` (e.g. "approach", "will implement/fix/add/use", "fixing it by") even when a design comment's prose already covers the chosen approach in full conceptually — a thorough comment describing "Chosen hook: extend X, reusing its existing tokenizer" without the standalone word "approach" (or a `will <verb>` phrase) itself gets classified `missing: chosen approach` (the SAME #127 pitfall recurring, one ticket later — #373). Sanity-check any design comment locally BEFORE posting: `python3 -c "import design_gate as dg; print(dg.classify_design_comment(open('f').read()))"` — if it prints `(False, 'missing: ...')`, add a short supplementary comment using the literal keyword families for whichever concept(s) failed (root cause / chosen approach / rejected alternative), re-check locally, THEN post via `gh issue comment`.
- **When adversarial review finds that a whole-command-scoped "narrowing" exemption lets an unrelated `&&`/`;`-joined sibling command exempt a genuine violation elsewhere in the SAME command, check whether ANY existing tested ALLOW case legitimately needs the wider (whole-command) scope before uniformly narrowing the check to per-segment** — #373's `hooks/block-destructive-remote.sh` secret-read narrowing (`SAFE_NARROW_RE`) had exactly this gap: `ssh box "wc -l mcp.env && cat mcp.env"` let the unrelated `wc` exempt the genuine `cat` leak. Category A (single-file reads) was safely narrowed to per-pipeline-segment scope since its own legitimate ALLOW cases never span segments; Category B (bare env dumps) deliberately KEPT whole-command scope because its own tested legitimate flow (`printenv | awk ...`) genuinely spans two segments, and narrowing it would have broken that ALLOW case — the residual (an unrelated wc/awk still exempts a bare env dump) is a documented, accepted gap rather than a forced narrowing that would regress a real ALLOW case. General lesson for any future narrowing fix in this repo's command-classifying hooks: BEFORE uniformly narrowing an exemption found too-broad by review, enumerate every EXISTING test asserting that exemption's ALLOW behavior and check whether any of them structurally needs the broader scope — narrow only the parts that don't.

- **A blocked worker needs to see WHAT is missing, not just THAT something is missing — `write_reject_reason`/`read_reject_reason` (a new sibling `design-rejected/` marker directory, never mixed into `design-posted/`) is purely diagnostic and gates nothing itself.** `hooks/block-commit-without-design.sh` reads the latest reject reason per still-unmarked issue and prints it as a "Your last posted comment was rejected — here's specifically what's still missing" section; a pre-existing test asserting NO cross-issue leak (`Architektúra` must not appear in a block message for a DIFFERENT issue's own reject reason) had to be rewritten mid-ticket once the STANDING guidance text started legitimately mentioning `Architektúra` on every block — the discriminator became the per-reason section header ("was rejected"), not the bare word, since the bare word is no longer an exclusive signal once it's also part of the always-shown guidance.
- **Owner scope-extension mid-ticket, VERIFIED against the live GitHub comment (never trusted from an in-conversation relay alone) before acting on it:** a second real comment on #414 (`gh issue view 414 --json comments`, author `zbynekdrlik`, timestamp AFTER dispatch) restored the interactive-`/brainstorming`-era "2-3 considered approaches + trade-offs" depth for a NON-TRIVIAL ticket, via the same `Triage:` line — folded into the SAME design-gate extension rather than a separate mechanism, and into `skills/autopilot/SKILL.md`'s new `1c.` DESIGN-TRIAGE sub-step (between the existing `1b.` ticket-validator and `2.` dispatch): classify trivial/design-heavy using the SAME HARD-task criteria `model-awareness.md` already defines (no parallel taxonomy), dispatch the EXISTING read-only `Plan` subagent_type through the SAME `fable-gate` the HARD-task escalation already uses for a design-heavy member's whole-repo architecture view, post its synthesis to the ticket immediately (`durable-decisions-to-tickets.md`), and feed a summary into the worker's own dispatch prompt as grounding — the worker's CYCLE step 2 design comment still writes the final `Triage:`/`Architektúra:`/2-3-approaches itself, now grounded rather than invented solo.
- **A one-shot Fable ADVISOR consult (gate OPEN, digest-in-decision-out, per `fable-advisor`/`model-awareness.md`) for the module-wording + regex-design + Step-1c-placement synthesis on a genuinely cross-cutting ticket like this one is worth the dispatch even when the implementer already has a solid draft** — it caught a real design gap (classify the `Triage:` LINE's captured VALUE, never the whole body, else prose elsewhere merely discussing "non-trivial" misclassifies) and supplied concrete keyword-list/min-length corrections (400-char floor for the non-trivial branch, `Variant`/diacritic-stripped alternates, word-bounded `pros`/`cons`) before a single line of `design_gate.py` was written, rather than discovering them via review-fix cycles afterward.
- **A worker's OWN CYCLE step 6 review-summary comment can accidentally satisfy `classify_validation_comment` instead of `classify_review_comment` — the same one-comment-one-kind collision #414's own MINOR-2 fix addresses for a design comment, just recurring on the reviewer's OWN prose.** Live-hit posting #414's own review report: a summary sentence using "confirmed"/"verified"/"re-confirmed" (natural words for describing what an adversarial review checked) satisfied `classify_validation_comment`'s action+evidence regex families, and since `ALL_KINDS = ("design", "validated", "reviewed")` tries validated BEFORE reviewed, it consumed the "validated" marker instead of "reviewed" — leaving the reviewed marker missing even though a genuine review pass had just been posted. Before posting a CYCLE step 6 review-summary comment, either check it locally first (`python3 -c "import design_gate as dg; b=open('f').read(); print(dg.classify_validation_comment(b), dg.classify_review_comment(b))"` — want `(False, ...)`/`(True, 'ok')`) or simply avoid the validation-action words ("confirmed"/"verified"/"checked"/"current code") in that specific comment and lead with review-action words instead ("/review", "requesting-code-review", "kontrola kódu", a finding-count summary). If it does collide, post a SECOND, deliberately narrow "reviewed"-shaped comment rather than trying to retroactively fix the first one — markers are additive, never overwritten.
- **A shared re-recordable entry keyed on ORIGIN (a caller-identity field), where TWO DIFFERENT callers can each independently re-record the SAME still-pending entry, must never let "the newest call's origin wins unconditionally" apply across EVERY origin pairing — a stronger, more-informative origin needs an explicit precedence rule against downgrade by a weaker one.** #402-review MAJOR-1 (adversarial review of #425's own fix): `record_compact_request`'s pre-existing #400 contract ("cwd/origin always take the newest call's values") was correct for the `ts` anchor but wrong applied to `origin` too — the SubagentStop hook fires under the SUPERVISOR's own sid on every worker return (#317's parallel-round shape), so it routinely re-records a STILL-PENDING `self-callback` entry with `origin="subagent-stop"` before the original request is ever delivered, silently stripping the #425 exemption from a turn that genuinely earned it. Live-confirmed on this box's own `~/.claude/compact-requests.json`. Fix: refuse the downgrade specifically (`self-callback` -> anything-else on an ALREADY-pending entry keeps `self-callback`); the reverse direction (upgrade) is untouched. Reusable check for any future "the newest re-record wins" field: enumerate every ORIGIN/CALLER-IDENTITY pairing that field can see, not just the `ts`-shaped "does it need freezing at all" question — a field encoding WHO is calling often needs asymmetric precedence, not the same "newest wins" rule a value field like `ts`/`cwd` correctly uses.
- **A Slovak (or any diacritic-heavy) word-family detector added to `stop-check-prose-violations.sh` needs AT LEAST two rounds of fresh-context adversarial review before shipping, and each round finds a DIFFERENT class of bug — this repo's own established "two rounds if round 1 found anything substantive" precedent held exactly true for #424's SK tester-handoff family.** Round 1 found bare "over"/"overí" colliding with plain English "over" AND with this repo's own MANDATED design-comment phrasing ("test overí, že X funguje" — the exact wording `durable-decisions-to-tickets.md`/CYCLE step 2 requires every worker to post), plus bare `\bide\b` colliding with the "IDE" acronym and the "ide o X" idiom the MANDATED Slovak question template itself uses (`user-questions-slovak.md`'s own "**Otázka — projekt...**" shape). Round 2, reviewing round 1's OWN fix, found a genuinely NEW class each time: a mutation-invisible negative-control test (the round-1 fix's own regression lock never actually exercised the ASCII-fallback design decision it claimed to guard), a real false negative at incident severity ("či to UŽ ide" — the incident's own iterative-retry idiom — escaped because the WORKS phrase required "to" immediately adjacent to "ide" with zero room for an adverb), and two live false positives from a shape round 1 itself had just added (a modal+infinitive request shape false-positiving on an epistemic "vieš, že..." clause and on "netreba <infinitive>", "X is not needed" — the grammatical opposite of a request). Do not skip round 2 just because round 1's CRITICALs are fixed — round 1 reviews the ORIGINAL implementation, round 2 reviews the FIX, and a fix is new code with its own new bugs (this exact lesson is already recorded above for #238/#160's compact-delivery fixes; #424 reconfirms it for a completely different subsystem).
- **ERE (POSIX extended regex, what `grep -E` uses throughout this hook) has no lookaround — so excluding a false-positive SHAPE from ONE alternative inside a big multi-shape `A|B|C|D` regex, without risking suppression of a genuine violation from a DIFFERENT alternative that happens to share nearby words, needs the sub-shape pulled OUT of the combined regex into its OWN separate check, combined via bash boolean logic (`msg_has MAIN || (msg_has SUB && msg_missing SUB_SAFE_EXCLUSION)`), never a single shared exclusion applied to the whole combined pattern.** #424-review round 2 MINOR-1: a global exclusion for "vieš, že..." (an epistemic clause, not a request) would have ALSO silently suppressed a genuine INSTALL+CONFIRM violation in a message that happened to also contain "vieš, že" somewhere else — a real risk in this class of proximity-window detector. The fix keeps every OTHER shape's own regex 100% unchanged (still one combined `msg_has` call) and evaluates ONLY the newly-problematic sub-shape (here: the modal+infinitive request, shape 2b) as a second, independent check whose own AND-NOT exclusion can only ever affect that one sub-shape's verdict. `msg_missing`'s existing fail-closed-on-UNKNOWN semantics (#194's own "resolve the unknown WHERE IT ARISES" design) apply unchanged to the new exclusion check with zero extra code — an undeterminable safe-shape check still returns "missing" (0/true), so the modal branch still blocks rather than silently exonerating on a grep failure. Reuse this exact shape (pull the problematic alternative OUT, recombine with `||`/`&&` bash logic, not regex lookaround) for any future single-shape exclusion inside this file's other multi-shape combined-alternation detectors.

- **A mutation-testing finding that survives the shipped suite is not automatically a
  missing TEST — trace whether the guarded state is even REACHABLE through the real call
  path before writing a test for it.** #428-review MINOR-1 flagged
  `is_trivial = triage_ok and dg.triage_class(body) == "trivial"` (post-record-design-
  comment.sh) as having a surviving mutant (`triage_ok and` dropped). Tracing precisely:
  `classify_design_comment`'s own `MIN_LEN` gate and `classify_triage_and_approaches`'s
  `MIN_LEN_TRIAGE_TRIVIAL` gate are the SAME constant, checked on the SAME
  `(body or "").strip()` string — so any body that clears the FIRST gate (a precondition
  for the design-kind branch to run at all) has ALREADY cleared the second, making the
  "too short, trivial" branch of `classify_triage_and_approaches` structurally
  unreachable at that call site. Writing a HOOK-LEVEL test asserting that combined
  scenario would misrepresent production reachability (it can't happen there) — the
  honest fix was a direct, standalone unit test of `classify_triage_and_approaches`
  itself (calling it directly, bypassing the hook's precondition entirely), closing a
  real but SEPARATE gap: its sibling non-trivial-branch length floor already had a test,
  the trivial branch's identical floor didn't. When a review's own proposed test scenario
  turns out to be unreachable via the path it claims to guard, look one level down for
  the genuinely-reachable gap the finding is actually pointing at, rather than writing a
  misleading test to satisfy the finding's own (possibly slightly-off) framing verbatim.

- **A path-ownership check anchored on "is this INSIDE my own boundary" (a repo's working tree, a worktree's own directory, any scoped container) cannot safely collapse "outside MY boundary" into "belongs to nothing" — a SIBLING boundary (another worktree sharing the same `.git`, per #317; a wholly different repo) can legitimately own the exact same path.** #431-review F1 (`design_gate.is_git_tracked`, live-reproduced with real throwaway git worktrees BEFORE fixing, then end-to-end through the real hook): `git ls-files --error-unmatch` returning rc=128 "is outside repository" for a path outside CWD's own working tree does NOT mean the path is untracked by every repo everywhere — a worktree's own `cwd` makes its sibling MAIN checkout (sharing one `.git`) read as "outside repository" too, and so does any wholly unrelated repo's own tracked file. Treating that rc=128 alone as a second confident "not tracked, safe to quarantine" signal let a blocked-commit stale-msgfile quarantine RENAME AWAY a real, git-tracked project file — invisibly, since the `.stale-*` pattern is written to the shared `.git/info/exclude`. Fix pattern, reusable for any future "does X belong to boundary Y, and if not is it safe to treat as ownerless" check: resolve the ACTUAL owner first (`git -C <dirname(path)> rev-parse --show-toplevel`), and only when NO owner exists at all (rev-parse itself fails) is the "confidently ownerless" branch safe to take; when an owner DOES exist, re-query `--error-unmatch` against THAT owner and fail toward the conservative answer on anything but a confirmed negative.
- **A meta-script (`python3 - <<'PYEOF' ... PYEOF`) that builds a TARGET Python file's new source via a plain (non-raw) triple-quoted string literal (`new = '''...'''`) silently corrupts the target the instant the embedded content itself needs a literal `\n`** (e.g. authoring a NEW test method whose own body calls `write_text("...\n")`) — the OUTER meta-script's own Python parser interprets that `\n` as a REAL newline inside the string VALUE of `new`, so the byte actually written to the target file is a genuine line break spliced into the middle of what should be one unbroken string literal, breaking the target's syntax with NO warning from the meta-script itself (caught only by a subsequent `ast.parse()` on the target — the exact discipline this repo's own "confirm syntax valid" habit already runs after every `str.replace`-based edit, which is what caught this live). Fix: always use a RAW triple-quoted string (`new = r'''...'''`) for a meta-script's own string-literal PAYLOAD whenever the generated content can contain `\n`/`\t`/`\\` escapes meant to survive VERBATIM into the target file — the same class of gotcha this file's own `chr(39)`/heredoc-delimiter bullets already document for embedding one shell layer inside another, recurring one layer up at the Python-string level.
- **A doc-guidance regression test locking "the WORKTREE AWARENESS section teaches X" via word-presence assertions run against the WHOLE section is vacuous the moment the section has MULTIPLE bullets** (see the earlier "#432-review F4" entry above for the live mutant this defeated) — **scope every assertion to the ONE markdown bullet PARAGRAPH that actually mentions the subject**, via a forward scan from a top-level `- ` line up to the next top-level bullet or end of section: `re.search(r"(?ims)^-\s.*?KEYWORD.*?(?=^-\s|\Z)", section)`. This is what makes "this specific guidance says X" testable at all, rather than "this section somewhere says X". Pair it with a NEGATION-AWARE guard whenever checking for an absent claim (e.g. "must never say IS private") — a bare `assertNotIn("private", ...)` false-triggers on a CORRECT sentence that says "is NOT private"; scrub every properly-negated occurrence first (`re.sub(r"(?i)\b(?:not|never|isn't|is\s+not)\s+WORD\b", "", text)`) before asserting the bare word is absent, or the guard cannot coexist with the very honest phrasing it's meant to protect.
- **`classify_triage_and_approaches`'s numbered-approach requirement is DIGIT-only, not letter-only — a well-reasoned NON-TRIVIAL design comment naming "Option A" / "Option B" (or any lettered scheme) gets REJECTED, even with genuine trade-off prose, because `_APPROACH_MARKER_RE` is `\b(?:Approach|Option|Variant|Prístup|Možnosť)\s*#?\s*([1-3])\b` — digits 1-3 only.** #426: a first design comment discussing "Option A (chosen)" vs "Option B (rejected)" with real risk-profile trade-offs still failed with `non-trivial ticket missing: 2-3 distinct numbered approaches` — the reject reason (`~/.claude/design-rejected/design_<repo>#<issue>`) names exactly what's missing, but the fix isn't obvious from prose alone: you have to relabel the SAME options as "Option 1"/"Option 2" (or "Approach 1"/"Approach 2") for the regex to count them, and separately add an explicit trade-off-family word (`trade-off`/`kompromis`/`výhod`/`nevýhod`/`pros`/`cons`/`za a proti`/`on the other hand`) since risk-profile prose alone doesn't satisfy `_TRADEOFF_RE` either. Also worth knowing: `post-record-design-comment.sh` evaluates each comment INDEPENDENTLY, not cumulatively — a second, supplementary comment posted to fix a rejected design comment must ITSELF pass `classify_design_comment` (root cause + chosen approach + rejected alternative) AND, if non-trivial, `classify_triage_and_approaches`/`classify_architecture_section`, all from its OWN body; you cannot "add the missing piece" in a short follow-up comment relying on the earlier comment's content to fill the rest. Before posting ANY non-trivial design comment, sanity-check it locally first: `python3 -c "import design_gate as dg; b=open('f').read(); print(dg.classify_design_comment(b), dg.classify_triage_and_approaches(b), dg.classify_architecture_section(b))"` — cheaper than discovering the rejection from a blocked commit or a stale reject-reason file.
- **A cleanup/sweep job's own "becomes a permanent no-op once cleared" claim needs to be checked against the REAL population's reclaimability, not just the mechanism's own idempotency (never re-deletes what's already gone) — a name-prefix sweep whose discriminator has ANY structural blind spot (a long-lived system pid like `1`/`init` a holder can coincidentally record, a legacy shape that is never empty) can have a LARGE PERMANENTLY-unreclaimable subset that gets re-discovered and re-logged every cadence cycle forever.** #409-review finding 2, measured live against dev1's own real `/tmp` (not reasoned about): ~2800 of ~14000 real candidate rows (a `pid=1`/`agetty`-recorded holder, which `os.kill(1, 0)` always reports alive; a non-empty legacy directory) are provably NEVER reclaimable by the shipped discriminator — so a naive "log one line per row, every sweep" design grows an UNROTATED log file without bound (~400 KB/run measured -> ~145 MB/year/box) even though the sweep's own DELETE actions genuinely are idempotent. Fix pattern for any future sweep design: log only the ACTED-ON rows (removed / would-remove / a genuine delete-time failure) — never a routine "not litter, still refused" skip that will recur identically on every future run with no new information in it — and state the honest residual ("idempotent for its actions, NOT a full no-op") rather than an absolute "no-op" claim the real population disproves.

- **The `post-record-design-comment.sh` 180s freshness window applies to EVERY marker kind (`design`/`validated`/`reviewed`), not just `design` (already documented for that kind at #127) — and it bites `validated` in a subtler way: a comment can read as thorough to a human ("Confirmed... still present, unchanged") while never containing `classify_validation_comment`'s own required EVIDENCE keyword family (`still\s+(?:valid|real|happens|reproduces)`/`current\s+code`/`obsolete`/etc — the ACTION word `confirmed` alone is not enough, both halves are required).** #436/#411 (2026-08-13): two STEP 0 validation comments posted in an earlier segment of the same session never actually set the `validated` marker, discovered only by directly checking `design_gate.marker_exists(..., kind="validated")` rather than trusting that a well-written comment must have worked. Re-invoking the hook by hand minutes later against those SAME already-posted comments still found nothing — the hook only ever evaluates the single freshest STILL-IN-WINDOW comment per issue (`candidates.sort(...); _, latest = candidates[-1]`), so a comment that aged past 180s while you were investigating is permanently unusable for classification, no matter how many times you re-run the hook against it. The only fix: post a NEW comment with the required keyword family, then check `design_gate.marker_exists` **immediately, in the same turn** — never assume an earlier comment satisfied a classifier just because its prose reads as complete, and never trust a stale comment to still be gradeable later.
- **Even a correctly-keyworded FRESH validation/design/review comment can fail to write its marker when `post-record-design-comment.sh` (PostToolUse) fires IMMEDIATELY after the `gh issue comment` that posted it — GitHub's API has not yet propagated the new comment, so the hook's own `gh issue view --json comments` re-read returns the PREVIOUS latest comment, whose URL is already in `claimed_urls`, and it writes nothing.** #465 (worktree worker): a first STEP-0 validated comment missed `classify_validation_comment`'s evidence-keyword family (the entry just above), so a second, correctly-keyworded comment was posted to fix it — and THAT one's marker STILL didn't land, because it hadn't propagated when the hook ran; verified by an immediate `design_gate.marker_exists(..., kind="validated")` returning False. Distinct from the "comment aged past the 180s window" failure above: the comment is fresh AND the keywords are right; the only problem is API write-propagation lag at the instant of the immediate re-read. It self-heals on the NEXT `gh issue comment` for that same issue (the hook re-reads and the now-propagated comment is the freshest still-in-window one) — but do not spam a comment purely to trigger it. In WORKTREE mode it is MOOT anyway: the sole marker CONSUMER, `subagent-stop-check-design.sh`, exits early unless the evidence block claims BOTH `merged` AND `closed` (its own `if not ev["merged"] or not ev["closed"]` gate, ~L64), neither of which a worktree worker claims — so a missing marker never blocks the stop, and the DURABLE validation evidence (the posted comment itself) is what STEP 0/#213 actually requires. General class: any "record a marker from the just-posted comment" PostToolUse hook that re-reads via the API races the API's own write-propagation — treat the marker as best-effort and the posted comment as the source of truth.
- **A RE-POST/RE-TRACK of an already-tracked item is NOT a new generation — any "newest wins" dedup/supersede over re-recordable entries needs an explicit GENERATION field carried verbatim through every re-record, never the record timestamp.** #407: the ghost-question fix's first cut superseded/collapsed question-map entries per (session, channel) by record time — correct for the hook path (a fresh record there IS a new ask), and A/B-proven WRONG the moment `reping_stale_questions`'s daily re-track re-recorded an OLD question onto the CURRENT questions channel with a fresh ts: the stale re-post out-competed the session's LIVE, newer question at both the record-time supersede and the sweep collapse, silently black-holing the live card's reply routing (a fresh-context fable review found it with an A/B repro against the parent commit; independently re-reproduced before fixing). Fix shape, reusable for any future re-recordable-entry dedup in this repo: store the item's ORIGINAL-event timestamp as its own field at first record (`asked`, defaulting to now = a genuinely new item), have every re-record path pass the OLD entry's own generation through (`asked_ts=ask_generation(rec)`), give unreadable/legacy shapes a fallback that reads as OLDEST (bool/garbage → 0 — losing to everything is the safe direction for a keep-the-newest rule), and compare generations — never record times — at EVERY consumer (grep for every sort key/guard the moment a second consumer exists; #407 had two: the writer's supersede and the sweep's collapse, and fixing only one would have relocated the bug into the other). Companion trap in the SAME fix: the integration test sequenced prune-then-reping ITSELF, locking nothing about `run_once`'s real call order — a `inspect.getsource(run_once)` index comparison is the cheap source-order lock (same family as this file's other structural source-scan locks).
- **A test that deadline-kills its own child subprocess (SIGTERM probe, readiness-timeout probe — any "the child must have reached point X before I act" shape) must synchronise on an OUT-OF-BAND SENTINEL FILE the child touches at the event of interest — never a fixed wall-clock sleep, and never the child's own stdout when that stdout's BUFFERING is the thing under test.** #444 (2026-08-13, three tests in one load-flake class): (a) the SIGTERM-pipe probe slept a fixed 1.5s for its child to import airuleset+watchdog and print — under this box's 6-19-concurrent-suite contention the imports alone outlast it, and the SIGTERM landed on an empty pipe (deterministically reproduced with an injected 2.0s pre-import delay); (b) the liveness-hook harness bound `timeout=15` (~400x the 0.03s normal hook runtime) was genuinely exceeded once by a HEALTHY hook — now a shared, documented `HOOK_TIMEOUT_S = 120` with a no-numeric-literal lock; (c) the upload readiness-timeout test's `deadline_s=1.0` was simultaneously the SUBJECT under test (the wait must fail carrying the server's stderr) AND an implicit bound on the fake server's own interpreter startup — the deadline kill landed before the diagnostic was written, so the assertion on the drained stderr failed. The general discriminator: **a deadline that is itself the subject under test must never double as a bound on FIXTURE startup** — decouple them by having the fixture announce readiness via a sentinel file (visible regardless of stdio buffering — which is exactly why it cannot mask a buffering bug: a non-flushing child still touches the sentinel with its probe stuck in the block buffer, and the kill still loses it, mutation-verified), waited on with a bounded `time.monotonic()` poll (monotonic, per this file's own `_wait_until_serving` precedent — a wall-clock NTP step stretches/shrinks a `time.time()` bound), and only THEN start the deadline/kill under test. Size the child's keep-alive sleep (60s) so a natural exit — which would flush buffers and false-pass — stays unreachable behind the sentinel-then-kill ordering.
- **V commit message tohto repa NIKDY nepíš bare `#N` pre CUDZIE (upstream) issue čísla — `block-commit-without-design.sh` extrahuje každé `#N` z commit message a vyžaduje preň design comment na TOMTO repe (#447, 2026-08-13).** GREEN commit citujúci `anthropics/claude-code #11431` bol zablokovaný s „no design comment posted yet for #11431 #26307 #41828". De-hash tvar: „anthropics/claude-code issues 11431, 26307, 41828 upstream". To isté platí pre TELÁ committovaných súborov čítané ľuďmi — bare #N sa v tomto repe číta ako airuleset ticket.
- **`design_gate.marker_exists()` sa volá s BARE repo menom (`'airuleset'`), nie `owner/name` — `marker_key` skladá `<repo_key>#<issue>` presne z toho, čo mu dáš, a `'zbynekdrlik/airuleset'` ticho vráti False pre marker, ktorý existuje (#447, 2026-08-13).** Pri same-turn overovaní markerov (povinnom od #436) over kľúč proti reálnemu názvu súboru v `~/.claude/design-posted/` (`airuleset#447`), nie proti tomu, čo „vyzerá správne". Druhá polovica tej istej pasce: post-record hook skúša kinds v poradí a design-shaped komentár, ktorý FAILNE arch/triage check, `break`-ne celý loop — validation-shaped obsah v SKORŠOM komentári tej dávky sa už nikdy negraduje; fix je vždy REPOST celého design komentára s chýbajúcim kusom (v2), nikdy „veď obsah tam je".

- **A prose-gate check that EXTRACTS a line and then judges its CONTENT must select the judged line LINE-ANCHORED (`^[[:space:]]*<rx>`) with an unanchored fallback — the #96 mention-vs-use taxonomy has a THIRD polarity beyond phrase-matching vs structural: structural checks deliberately read raw MSG because on a PRESENCE probe a mention can only fail OPEN (make a missing line look present), but the moment the same unanchored match picks WHICH line a substance/contradiction probe judges, a backticked mid-prose mention BECOMES the judged line and converts into a fail-CLOSED false block against a report whose real line is fine.** #446 adversarial review F2 (LIVE-triggered): prose narrating the hook's own stderr guidance ("hook si pýta riadok `✅ Výstup: n/a — <prečo>`") before the rewritten report made the mention the first match; the n/a×🌐 contradiction then fired on it. Presence stays raw/unanchored; extraction anchors first.
- **The #446 "adding a NEW mandatory `## ✅ Work Complete` line touches a FIXED five-place checklist" entry was itself INCOMPLETE — there is a SIXTH home: `skills/autopilot/SKILL.md`'s own Step 5 round-report audit enumeration** (the inline `audits — ✅ CI: green, ✅ /plan-check…, ✅ /review…, ✅ /requesting-code-review…, ✅ Deploy: <version>` list the SUPERVISOR follows when composing a whole-round `## ✅ Work Complete` report). #450 (F3 from #446): the `✅ Výstup:` line was merged into all five #446 homes but never added to this Step 5 enumeration, so a supervisor literally following Step 5's listed audits composes a round report that `stop-check-prose-violations.sh` then blocks (one self-healing but wasted blocked turn per round) — found only by an adversarial review of a DIFFERENT #450 change that happened to reference the Výstup line, never by the #446 work itself. Whenever a NEW mandatory audit line is added to the `## ✅ Work Complete` contract, grep `skills/autopilot/SKILL.md` for the Step 5 audit enumeration (the `✅ Deploy: <version>` anchor sits at its end) and add the new line there too, as a sixth checklist item alongside #446's five — the SUPERVISOR's own round report is a distinct composition path from any worker's per-ticket report, and #446's checklist only covered the shapes a worker/served session emits.
- **`MANAGED_DISABLED_PLUGINS`** (rust-analyzer-lsp, claude-md-management) —
  force-DISABLED (`False`), never wanted at all, and NOT installed.
- **`claude plugin install <key>@<marketplace>` writes `enabledPlugins[<key>]=true` into user-scope `~/.claude/settings.json` as a SIDE EFFECT, and it OVERWRITES a pre-seeded `false` — so any reconcile pass that force-DISABLES a plugin BEFORE the install loop is silently clobbered on any box where that plugin actually gets installed (registry entry missing → install runs).** #415 adversarial review F1 (proven twice in isolated `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` sandboxes): `setup_managed_plugins()` reconciles FIRST (deliberate #273 ordering — the settings write must land early to unblock the install), then installs; on a FRESH box the playwright install re-enabled the key the reconcile had just disabled, ending provisioning in the exact pre-#415 resident-Chrome regime. An already-provisioned box is unaffected (registry present → `_managed_plugin_built` short-circuits → 0 installs → the reconcile's `false` stands), which is why the headline flip-stale-true criterion passed while the fresh-box path was broken — and why a mocked-subprocess test suite (installs are side-effect-free mocks) could not see it. Fix (`setup_managed_plugins`): keep the reconcile-before-install, and RE-ASSERT the same reconcile AFTER the install loop (extracted `_reconcile_settings_file()`), a no-op write on the already-built fast path. Any future OPTIONAL/force-disabled plugin has this exact hazard — reconcile-after-install is mandatory, not just reconcile-before. The regression test must use a fake `claude plugin install` that WRITES `enabledPlugins[key]=true` into the patched `SETTINGS_JSON` (mimicking the real side effect) and assert the final on-disk file has the key `False`; a fake that only records argv cannot catch it.
## Persisting a "stable" decision from a verify: measured-answer vs the verifier's own degrade-sentinel (#474, 2026-08-14)

- **A Discord-forwarded digest/roundup block built by concatenating N lines must be sized so the WORST case stays under `notify._MAX_CONTENT` (1900 codepoints) — `notify.send` truncates a longer block, silently dropping the tail items AND the footer.** #461 (`_owner_decision_digest_block`): fixed overhead (header + "…a ďalších K" line + footer) is ~280 chars, each ticket line is ~115 worst case (long repo name + 80-char title), so a default `limit` of 15 could reach ~1.9 KB (over) while 12 is ~1.6 KB (clear). Lock the invariant with a regression test that builds a worst-case list (many long-repo + full-length-title items) at the PRODUCTION default and asserts `len(block) < notify._MAX_CONTENT` — mutation-verified against a revert of the default (15 makes it fail). A `limit`-passed-explicitly mechanism test is independent of the default and does not catch a default regression, so both are needed.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15)

- **An enrichment step that can UPGRADE a value must respect WHY an earlier step already overrode it — not just consult its own independent evidence class.** #391: `_slice_mine_and_handed`'s comment-fallback walk kept the LAST comment signal in creation order and, if it read True, unconditionally set `handed[n_num] = True` — discarding the EARLIER `prio:bounce` label override (which forces `handed=False` specifically because a bounce means "not actually done") the moment a bounce was applied via a BARE label with no accompanying gatekeeper-shaped comment (the sanctioned Discord nudge-lane bounce shape, `skills/autopilot/SKILL.md`) — a stale pre-bounce READY-FOR-REVIEW comment then silently re-flipped a genuinely-still-open bounce back to handed, a false "nothing left to do" for the reduced-authority `/goal` stop-proof. A fresh-context adversarial review caught it live via a fixture with a comment thread carrying ONLY the stale hand-off (no bounce comment at all) — none of the PRE-EXISTING bounce tests exercised this, because every one of them either had an empty comment list (verdict stayed at its label-derived default) or a comment thread that ALREADY contained a recognizable bounce comment. Fix: track which rows are currently bounce-labeled (`bounce_numbers`), and during the comment walk ALSO track whether a recognized gatekeeper-authored comment (`_comment_readiness_signal` returning `False`) was seen anywhere in the SAME thread (`saw_gatekeeper_comment`) — a bounce-labeled row may only be upgraded to handed when the bounce is itself VISIBLE in the comments (a genuine post-bounce re-hand-off); an invisible bounce (label-only, no comment) fails toward "still unhandled", the safe direction for a stop-proof. General lesson for any future recovery/fallback mechanism layered on top of an existing override: before letting a later signal overwrite an earlier one, ask whether the earlier signal was itself an OVERRIDE with its own justification (here: "this specific ticket was bounced") — if so, the later signal must independently confirm that justification no longer holds, not just win on its own terms.
- **A write-once anchor field for a bounded HOLD/grace window (stamped the first time a block is observed, read back on later sweeps to measure "how long has this been blocked") must also be explicitly CLEARED the moment the block itself lifts — never just left to be silently overwritten or preserved forever like a PRESERVE-across-re-record field (`deferred_since`, `not_boundary_since`'s own preservation logic) legitimately is.** #394-review F1: `not_boundary_since` (job 14's marker-hold grace anchor, #394) was stamped once and then only ever PRESERVED (mirroring the sibling `deferred_since`'s own correct "never reset across a re-record" shape) — but nobody ever cleared it when the marker genuinely recovered (a real boundary again) on a sweep that still didn't deliver for an unrelated reason (e.g. the pane was busy). A LATER, genuinely NEW hold episode then silently inherited the OLD, already-elapsed anchor and could deliver on a hold that had barely started — precisely the outcome the whole grace mechanism exists to prevent. The two field-lifecycle shapes are NOT interchangeable: a preserve-field answers "has this REQUEST ever needed extra time" and correctly never resets; an episode-anchor answers "how long has THIS SPECIFIC ongoing condition persisted" and MUST reset the instant the condition it's timing stops being true, or its later re-arm inherits a stale, already-elapsed clock. When adding a NEW anchor of this second shape, pair the `if not_set: stamp` branch with an `elif blocked_is_now_false and set: clear` branch in the SAME place, and add a dedicated 3-sweep test (hold → recovers-for-an-unrelated-reason → a brand-new hold) proving the anchor value at sweep 3 differs from sweep 1's, not just that delivery/refusal is correct at each individual sweep in isolation — a per-sweep-only test suite can (and did) stay green while the underlying anchor value was already wrong.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 2)

- **`card-unreported <repo> n=N` (watchdog job 25) is a REPO-scoped metric over the shared tracker, never evidence that THIS box's session is active** — on marek@subdev it read n=29 while the session had produced zero turns for six days (the same stuck candidate set re-verified every sweep); the session-scoped truths are the transcript's own last timestamp and the presence marker mtime (#247).
- **A NEW function that reads a WINDOW-BOUNDED slice of the SAME transcript file another function already reads with an established window constant must default to that SAME constant (or read the whole file) — never invent its own smaller ad-hoc window.** #393-review MAJOR-1 (independently re-verified, live-reproduced): `_goal_autopilot_reinvoked_after`'s own `tail_bytes=2_000_000` default was SMALLER than `GOAL_MARK_TAIL_BYTES = 4_000_000`, the window `scan_goal_markers` uses to bootstrap the very clear-marker this function was asked to check release evidence FOR — so a genuine release-proving entry sitting 2–4 MB from EOF was structurally unreachable even though the marker referencing it was in view. This is the SAME shape the #173 bullet elsewhere in this file already documents (a "fresh, independent re-derivation" reading only a tail window misses evidence a long-lived, continuously-writing session pushed past it) — the NEW angle worth naming explicitly: the bug here was not "should this read the whole file", it was "two functions reading the SAME file for related purposes silently disagreed about how much of it counts", which is easy to miss because each function's OWN window looks individually reasonable. When adding a function that reads a transcript slice a SIBLING function already scans, grep for that sibling's own tail-window constant and compare numbers before picking a default.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 3)

- **An open ALLOWLIST character class (`[ \w()]{0,N}`) sized to reject a KNOWN bad example is not equivalent to matching the actual small, enumerable set of real shapes it exists to recognize — when the real vocabulary is closed and short, write the closed-form pattern instead.** #393-review MINOR-1 (independently re-verified, live-reproduced): `_GOAL_HEADER_INDICATOR_RX`'s first cut (`[ \w()]{0,40}`, meant to reject a wrapped-prose continuation with real punctuation) still accepted 7 of 8 constructed continuations that simply had NO punctuation in their first 40 chars ("◎ /goal armed", "◎ /goal active and the loop keeps going") — the allowlist correctly rejected the ONE reported live incident (which had a comma) while leaving the whole surrounding class of unpunctuated false positives open, because "narrower than before" was mistaken for "narrow enough". The real render vocabulary here is tiny and fully known (bare, " active", " active (Nh/Nm)" for N up to 3 digits) — replacing the allowlist with `( active(\s\(\d{1,3}[hm]\))?)?` closed it against every constructed false positive while still matching every shape this repo's own corpus has ever rendered. Prefer a closed-form pattern over an allowlist whenever the thing being matched has a small, genuinely enumerable real vocabulary — an allowlist is the right tool only when the real vocabulary is itself open-ended.
- **A closed-form pane-render regex tuned to a repo's OBSERVED vocabulary WILL false-negative when Claude Code changes its render — #393 MINOR-2's own "widen only on a live render" clause is a promissory note, and #487 called it in.** gk's armed pane settled to `› stashed · ◎ /goal active (1d)`, breaking `_GOAL_HEADER_INDICATOR_RX` from TWO simultaneous new shapes: CC prepended the stash-slot marker (`STASH_MARKER` + " · ") onto the glyph's own header line (so `^`-anchor missed), and the age crossed to DAY granularity (`[hm]` rejected `(1d)`). The resulting `pane_goal_armed` False killed the #442/#481 lane-fill guard. Three reusable moves when widening such a regex: (1) derive any literal prefix from the repo's OWN constant (`re.escape(STASH_MARKER + " · ")`), never a hand-retyped copy; (2) verify the exact separator by HEXDUMP of the real render, not by eye — the middle dot was U+00B7 (`c2 b7`), trivially mis-typed as `.`/`*`/a plain space; (3) audit EVERY parser of the same rendered line first (`grep` the glyph across `watchdog/` + `hooks/`) — here only the ONE anchored regex needed the widening, because the two other consumers (`cross_stream.py` `"◎ /goal" in captured`, `notify-discord-pending.sh` `grep -qF`) are SUBSTRING checks that tolerate prefix/suffix render-drift for free. Direction rule: a substring `in`/`grep -qF` check absorbs render drift automatically; an anchored regex must be explicitly re-widened, so it is the only shape a render change can silently break. (Secondary #487 finding — the janitor never reclaims a stash parked >6h ago because its provenance mark expires while the parked draft persists — is filed as its own security-boundary ticket, NOT folded here.)

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 4)

- **A newly-added UNCONDITIONAL gate (e.g. #400 FIX 5's live-tasks skip) must be re-verified against the FRESHEST capture at every send point that already has a "raced since the sweep" re-verify — reusing that existing re-verify, not adding a parallel one.** #400-review MAJOR-1 (live-triggered by the reviewer's own repro): job 14's idle-send and `deliver_compact_now`'s sync-send both already re-captured the pane immediately before typing (the pre-existing #333-review "raced" check) but only re-classified that fresh capture for BOUNDARY kind (input/draft) — never for the NEWLY-added live-tasks signal, so the live-tasks gate one block above still read a capture that could be stale by the actual moment of the send. Fixed by threading the SAME fresh capture (`captured=fresh`) into `_session_has_live_bg_tasks` at both send points — zero extra tmux round-trips. Reusable check for any future unconditional gate added to a file that already has an established "re-verify against a fresh capture right before the irreversible action" pattern: extend that SAME re-verify to cover the new signal, don't assume the older re-verify's scope automatically covers it.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 5)

- **A send/delivery function with MULTIPLE branches (e.g. job 14's idle-send vs stash-send) needs its OWN dedicated test coverage per branch for every cross-cutting property — a property proven on one branch is NOT proven on a sibling branch that reaches the same helper calls through a different code path.** #400-review MAJOR-2: job 14's STASH branch (`_compact_stash_attempt`) had zero test coverage for FIX 4's cooldown timestamp-mark and FIX 6's SEND log line, even though the IDLE branch had both locked down — two of the reviewer's own mutations (removing `mark_compact_delivery_ts` from the stash branch; removing its `_log_compact_sync` call) left the ENTIRE existing suite green. When adding a new claim about "every delivery path does X", write a test PER branch that can actually deliver, not one test on whichever branch was easiest to drive.
- **A log-before-the-real-action comment ("logged before anything downstream can throw and swallow the record") must be checked against the ACTUAL statement order, not trusted from the comment text — the same bug can independently exist on multiple branches even after one is fixed.** #400-review MINOR-6: job 14's stash branch wrote its SEND line to `compact-sync.log` AFTER five other calls (`reqs.pop`, `mark_compact_delivered`, `compact_claim_set`, `mark_compact_boundary`, `mark_compact_delivery_ts`) despite its own comment claiming the opposite; `deliver_compact_now`'s independent send point had the identical ordering bug, undetected until fixed for consistency. Grep for the actual line order around a "logged first" claim rather than trusting the adjacent comment.
- **When a caller must decide whether to suppress a SIBLING nudge shape based on "did my own diagnosis apply this sweep", have the callee return an EXPLICIT boolean — never let the caller re-derive it by sniffing the callee's own LOG TEXT for substrings.** #365-review M2 (fresh-context adversarial review, 2026-08-12): job 20's `_goal_lane_occupancy_nudge` used to return only `logs`, and its caller inferred ownership as `bool(lane_logs) and not any("-> occupied" in ln or "no measurable open backlog" in ln for ln in lane_logs)`. That worked right up until the give-up branch's own one-shot ping dedup (`if not rec.get("lpinged"): ... append log`) meant a SECOND-and-later give-up sweep appended NOTHING — an empty `lane_logs` read by the old heuristic as "did not own this sweep", which let the sibling `_goal_stall_nudge` (no subagent-liveness check, no recent-human-activity gate of its own) resume typing "continue" into a session already diagnosed and escalated on. Fix: the function now returns `(logs, owns)`, with `owns` set the instant the diagnosis genuinely applies (both counts known, neither escape fired) and held `True` on EVERY subsequent return path regardless of whether new log text was appended that sweep. Any function whose SIDE EFFECT (what it typed, whether it "claimed" a sweep) a caller needs to know should say so explicitly in its return value — inferring caller-visible state from a callee's own logging is a hidden coupling that silently breaks the moment the callee's logging becomes conditional (a dedup, a rate-limit, a give-up).

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 7)
- **Adding a NEW "X never blocks the stop" disclaimer to a `/goal` template's PROOF section must be cross-checked against every OTHER clause in the SAME giant single-line template that talks about the SAME evidence — an `assertIn`-shaped test suite only proves each clause is PRESENT, never that two clauses agree with each other.** #384/#395-review MAJOR-1: fork-no-merge's pre-existing REVIEW-WATCH clause said an open hand-off ticket "is NOT done — REVIEW-WATCH: stay alive", while the SAME ticket's own new (B) proof disclaimer said the identical evidence (a nonzero `gk N`, i.e. an open ticket carrying my hand-off comment) "never blocks 🏁" — a direct behavioral contradiction the shipped test suite could not see, because it only ever checked each phrase's presence in isolation (`assertIn("REVIEW-WATCH", fk)`, `assertIn("never blocks", fk)` — both true, simultaneously, for two clauses that disagree). The sibling branch-merge template did NOT have this bug, because its REVIEW-WATCH trigger is scoped to something its OWN proof genuinely enforces ("release still pending", checked by the RELEASED command) — copying that phrasing onto fork-no-merge would have been dishonest, since fork's RELEASED proof cannot see an unmerged hand-off the same way. General check for any future template edit that adds a new "X is/isn't blocking" claim: grep the WHOLE template line for every OTHER place the same underlying fact is discussed, and read them side by side as plain English — a test suite proving each clause exists says nothing about whether they're mutually consistent.
## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 8)
- **`except OSError` is NOT sufficient to catch a broken gzip stream — `EOFError` (truncated compressed data) and `zlib.error` (corrupt deflate stream) are BOTH raised from inside Python's own `gzip`/`zlib` decompressors and are NEITHER an `OSError` subtype.** #410 review F3/F4 (MAJOR, live-triggered both times — claude-history's `_read_jsonl()` AND `_compress_transcript_file()`'s own verify-decompress step had the identical gap): a truncated `.jsonl.gz` escaped uncaught past a bare `except OSError`, crashing with a raw Python traceback instead of the SAME controlled "cannot read" message every other broken-input case in the same function produces. `BadGzipFile` (a bad MAGIC header) genuinely IS an `OSError` subtype and is caught fine by the naive form — which is why a test using bogus, non-gzip bytes (`b"not a real gzip stream"`) never reaches this gap at all; proving the fix needs a REAL, valid gzip stream (`gzip.compress(...)`) TRUNCATED mid-body. Catch `(OSError, EOFError, zlib.error)` (needs `import zlib`) on any code reading a gzip stream whose integrity you cannot fully trust.
- **Module-split (#433 item G) C5 seam audit MUST detect `patch.object(wd, "NAME")` — the repo's DOMINANT monkeypatch idiom — not just `setattr(watchdog, ...)` or the `patch("watchdog.NAME")` string form.** #433 step 1 (transcripts.py extract): a first, narrow grep for only `setattr(watchdog|wd, "NAME")` + `patch("watchdog.NAME")` reported ZERO seams for all 25 moved names — a false all-clear that would have greenlit keeping every intra-module call bare. Broadening to also catch `m.patch.object(wd, "NAME")` (where tests do `import watchdog as wd`) surfaced the REAL seams: `transcript_last_marker` + `_count_live_subagents` (test_goal_sweep) and `find_active_transcript` (test_goal_arm). The safety argument only held because those three happen to have NO internal call site inside the moved block; had one been called bare by a co-moved function, the narrow audit would have shipped a silent fleet-wide monkeypatch bypass (the design's #1 hazard). For EVERY future split step, grep all four idioms across tests/ AND hooks/: `setattr(<mod>, "NAME")`, `patch.object(<mod>, "NAME")`, `patch("watchdog[.<sub>].NAME")`, and direct `<mod>.NAME =` — plus the variable-target `patch.object(wd, some_var)` shape (enumerate what `some_var` can be). A name is safe-to-call-bare ONLY when ALL four come back empty.
## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 9)
- **A "the newest item in this group is protected/excluded" check must compare by the ORDERING VALUE (mtime, timestamp, whatever `sort()` keyed on), never by OBJECT IDENTITY of the item `sort()` happened to place first — a genuine TIE silently protects only the one row `sort()`'s (implementation-defined, stable-but-arbitrary) placement favoured.** #410 review F5 (MINOR, live-reproduced): `discover_old_transcript_candidates()`'s newest-in-directory exclusion did `newest_path = rows[0][1]; if p == newest_path: continue` after an mtime-descending sort — two files last written in the identical wall-clock second (a real, unremarkable occurrence, not an adversarial input) left the SECOND one eligible for compression, directly contradicting the function's own docstring ("the newest... is NEVER a candidate", which for a tie means BOTH are equally "the newest"). Fixed by comparing `mtime == newest_mtime` (the VALUE) instead of `p == newest_path` (the IDENTITY) — this excludes every row tied for newest, not just the first one `sort()` returned. Whenever writing this "protect the extremum of a group" pattern, capture the extremum's VALUE, not a reference to one specific row that happens to hold it.
- **A "re-capture fresh, right before send" guard (this file's own established `#176-F3` pattern, used whenever a blocking fetch sits between a sweep's stale `captured` snapshot and the eventual keystroke) must re-verify EVERY precondition the ORIGINAL stale check verified, not just the ones that happen to occur to you first.** #365-review round-2 MINOR (fresh-context adversarial re-review of an already-reviewed-and-fixed diff — SAFE TO SHIP AS-IS overall, but this one gap had zero test coverage): the round-1 fix added a fresh recapture that re-checked ONLY at-rest-ness (`_classify_boundary`/`pane_at_idle_prompt`), mirroring `_goal_template_drift`'s pattern by memory rather than by re-reading it — but that sibling ALSO re-checks `pane_goal_armed(fresh) is not True`. The blocking `_cached_backlog_count` fetch can span a whole final turn; if the loop finished and disarmed WHILE it ran, the pane returns to a bare, at-rest prompt (passes the narrower check) but the goal itself is gone, and the nudge would still get typed into a session that should never receive one. When copying an established "re-verify against a fresh capture" pattern from a sibling function, diff your new check against the SIBLING'S actual code (grep it, don't recall it) — a re-check that verifies fewer things than the pattern it's modeled on is a silent regression with no distinguishing symptom until the exact race window is hit live.
- **`card-unreported <repo> n=N` (watchdog job 25) is a REPO-scoped metric over the shared tracker, never evidence that THIS box's session is active** — on marek@subdev it read n=29 while the session had produced zero turns for six days (the same stuck candidate set re-verified every sweep); the session-scoped truths are the transcript's own last timestamp and the presence marker mtime (#247). [archived from internals-watchdog.md by #375]
