The blocking `_cached_backlog_count` fetch can span a whole final turn; if the loop finished and disarmed WHILE it ran, the pane returns to a bare, at-rest prompt (passes the narrower check) but the goal itself is gone, and the nudge would still get typed into a session that should never receive one. When copying an established "re-verify against a fresh capture" pattern from a sibling function, diff your new check against the SIBLING'S actual code (grep it, don't recall it) — a re-check that verifies fewer things than the pattern it's modeled on is a silent regression with no distinguishing symptom until the exact race window is hit live.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 10)

- **A "fix a MINOR review finding" pass must be checked against ANY existing test that ALREADY locks a stricter, opposite-direction invariant in the SAME function — a fix that reads cleanly in isolation can directly violate a #400-class ("never refresh a stale anchor") lock the reviewer never saw because it wasn't in their digest.** #402-review MINOR-2 proposed resetting `record_compact_request`'s preserved `ts` when the PRIOR entry was already past the age cap (so a genuinely fresh boundary doesn't inherit a corpse) — implementing it broke `test_ts_is_never_refreshed_across_a_re_record` (a PRE-EXISTING, deliberately-locked #400 regression test asserting the anchor is NEVER refreshed "no matter how stale it genuinely is"). Tracing the actual failure mode showed the "gated" reset was a WEAKER form of the exact #400 bug it risked reintroducing: a session producing genuine boundary events more often than `COMPACT_REQUEST_MAX_AGE_S` apart, forever, would keep resetting the anchor just past each expiry and never let the hard age cap fire — an 11-hour-stale request surviving via periodic auto-reset instead of continuous refresh, same shape, slower cadence. REJECTED the fix, reverted the code, and — because `deliver_compact`'s condition (e) is checked FIRST and unconditionally on EVERY sweep (~60s cadence, no pane resolution needed) — the real residual is only a narrow, self-healing window (<=60s) rather than a structural gap: the NEXT genuine boundary event after the sweep clears the dead entry gets a fully fresh `ts` regardless. **When a proposed MINOR fix conflicts with an existing locked test, don't just delete or weaken the test to make the fix pass — trace whether the fix's OWN failure mode reproduces the exact incident the locked test protects against, at a different frequency; if so, keep the lock and document the REJECTED fix's reasoning at the point a future reader would otherwise re-propose it.**
- **A watchdog sweep test that FREEZES `now` across a loop and relies on per-iteration state accumulation silently breaks the moment that sweep gains ELAPSED-TIME throttling (a backoff, cooldown, or park window) — the fix is to ADVANCE the clock past each window per iteration, NEVER to weaken the assertion.** #479: adding `goal_lane_occupancy_nudge`'s stash-abort backoff park (`rec['lnpark']`, staged `120/300/900/1800s`) broke `test_busy_undersaturated_stash_abort_still_reaches_giveup`, which looped 7× on a fixed `now=100000` expecting the abort streak (`rec['lna']`) to reach the give-up cap by iteration count alone; once the first abort parks every subsequent sweep, the streak never accumulates and the give-up never fires. The test's INTENT (a permanently-aborting lane still reaches the ONE give-up ping) was unchanged and still asserted verbatim — only its time model was wrong. Fixed in its OWN commit by advancing `now`/`tmtime` by a step larger than the biggest backoff each iteration (passes on both the pre- and post-#479 implementation, so it is an orthogonal test-model correction, not a weakening). Many goal-sweep tests share this fixed-`now` shape, so any future cooldown/debounce/backoff added to a sweep function should expect to update the clock model of the EXISTING tests, not just add new ones.

## Presunuté z internals-tests.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15)

- Test with `python -m pytest tests/` before committing
- **A shim-wiring test written as `assertIn("<function_name>", CAVEMAN_SHIM_CONTENT)` has NO teeth against a mutant that computes the segment and then throws the result away** (`segs.append(mdl)` → `pass`, leaving the `mdl = statusbar.model_segment(d, ...)` call itself intact) — the function-name substring still matches the surrounding comment/call, so the test stays green while the segment silently vanishes from every rendered statusline. #133 MINOR-2 confirmed this by mutation (kept both `tests.test_statusbar`'s substring test AND the full `tests.test_caveman` suite green). The teeth-bearing form already exists elsewhere in this file (`tests/test_caveman.py`'s `TestCavemanShimBehavior._run()` writes `render_caveman_shim()`/`CAVEMAN_SHIM_CONTENT` to a temp file, execs it as a REAL `bash` subprocess against a crafted stdin payload, and asserts the segment's actual rendered text is in `r.stdout`) — any NEW statusline segment's wiring test should use that pattern from the start, not a bare substring check, which this ticket's own first-cut design comment failed to anticipate.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 11)

- **The request-store `ts`-reset decision is TRANSITION-dependent — a blanket "never reset `ts`" over-generalized from the #402 lesson two lines up would silently re-break a legitimate case (#478 confirmed both halves of the compact pattern on the SIBLING `goal.record_goal_request`).** #402-review MINOR-2 REJECTED resetting `ts` on a SAME-origin automatic re-record past the age cap (the #400 refresh-forever trap). #478 CORRECTLY resets `ts` on an origin UPGRADE (weak `dark-rearm` → strong `self-callback`): that upgrade is a genuinely NEW user request (an explicit `/autopilot`), so it needs a fresh anchor or the user's brand-new arm is judged expired against the stale watchdog anchor within minutes — and it CANNOT drive refresh-forever because each upgrade requires a genuine human action, unlike an automatic re-record. Same store, same field, OPPOSITE correct answer per transition: reset on a weak→strong human upgrade, never on a same-origin automatic re-record. #478 also made `goal.record_goal_request` the SECOND live instance of the #402 origin-downgrade guard (line above: a `dark-rearm` never clobbers a pending non-`dark-rearm` entry) — the pattern is now REPEATED across the two sibling request stores (compact + goal), so any THIRD store or any store gaining a SECOND writer should carry both halves (downgrade-refused + human-upgrade-ts-reset) by default, and its dark/watchdog-initiated origin must additionally honour the recent-human gate + fail-closed on unprovable state (no transcript → `skip:no-transcript`, not a blind keystroke).
- **A function that resolves an expensive resource (a filesystem walk to LOCATE a file, not read it) UNCONDITIONALLY before calling a cheap early-exit check that was ALWAYS going to refuse for most callers anyway is paying for work its own precondition already knows is wasted — gate the resolve on the SAME condition the downstream check gates on, not just on "do we already have it".** #402-review MINOR-3: `_session_has_live_bg_tasks` resolved the transcript PATH unconditionally right before calling `_compact_self_reported_complete(origin, tpath)` — even though that function's own FIRST line refuses any non-self-callback `origin` before ever touching `tpath`. The docstring claimed "the caller never even pays anything" for a non-exempt origin, which was true only for the transcript TEXT read (inside the exemption check itself), not for the PATH resolve one level up. Fix: `if tpath is None and origin == _COMPACT_SELF_CALLBACK_ORIGIN: tpath = _resolve_tpath()` — mirror the downstream check's own precondition at the call site, not just at the point where the value is finally used. Reusable check for any future "the caller never pays for X in the common case" cost claim: trace whether ALL of X's resolution steps are actually gated on the SAME precondition the consumer checks, not just the LAST one before the consumer runs.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 12)

- **`scan_goal_markers`'s own correct "only bytes appended since the given offset" incremental contract is WRONG for a caller that wants to know "is the transcript's newest marker STILL X" across MULTIPLE sweeps — CC writes a `Goal set:`/`Goal cleared:` marker exactly ONCE, at the moment it happens, never again while a loop silently runs or dies, so a caller that re-derives its verdict ONLY from each sweep's own (usually empty) delta sees the marker exactly once and then loses it forever.** #403 (`goal_dark_watch`'s own 2-sweep debounce): the off_state originally tracked only `{"off": new_off}`; a second sweep with no NEW appended transcript content got `mark=None` back from `scan_goal_markers`, which the caller's own `if mark is None: <reset the whole debounce>` branch treated as "the marker went away" — so the 2-sweep-before-pinging design could never actually reach its second sweep in real operation. Fix: persist the LAST-KNOWN marker alongside the offset (`off_state[sid] = {"off": new_off, "mark": new_mark if new_mark is not None else prior_mark}`) and fall back to it whenever a sweep's own delta is empty — cost stays the same one-small-incremental-read-per-sweep the function's own docstring promises, since only the BOOKKEEPING widens, not the read. Found by the ticket's own new regression test (`test_debounced_across_two_sweeps_before_pinging`), not by review — a second, independent bug in the SAME test (not passing a SHARED `state=` dict between the two `goal_dark_watch(...)` calls, so each call built its own fresh empty state internally) masked the first one identically; both had to be fixed before the test told the truth. Whenever writing ANY future incremental-`scan_goal_markers` consumer that must answer "is X still true" (not just "did X just happen"), persist the derived FACT, not merely the read cursor — and when testing it across multiple sweeps, thread ONE shared `state=` dict through every call, or the test measures nothing about persistence at all.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 13)

- **`deliver_goal()`'s condition ORDER means `pane_goal_armed()` (the tri-state already-armed check) runs BEFORE `_classify_boundary()` (busy/input/no-input-line) — a genuinely BUSY pane (a spinner, no `❯` visible anywhere) is therefore UNDETERMINABLE for the armed check FIRST (no box to read at all), so it resolves to `"skip:undeterminable"`, never `"skip:busy"`, even though `"skip:busy"` is a real, separately-reachable branch in the same function.** #403: a first-draft test asserted a plain busy-spinner fixture reaches `"skip:busy"` and got `"skip:undeterminable"` instead — not a bug, the correct consequence of the documented check ordering (armed-status is resolved before boundary classification, since dropping an already-armed request is cheaper and safer to decide first). To exercise the busy-KIND branch in isolation, mock `pane_goal_armed` to return a determinable `False` directly rather than trying to construct an organic fixture where BOTH `pane_goal_armed`'s whole-capture "❯" scan and `_classify_boundary`'s bottom-up chrome-peel-based box search would need to disagree in exactly the right way (they usually agree, since both ultimately look for the same glyph) — that combination is real but genuinely hard to construct honestly, and mocking the earlier check is the honest way to test the later one in isolation.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 14)

- **A symbol SHARED between an already-extracted cluster and a not-yet-extracted one is NOT automatically the same circular-import risk class as a cluster genuinely COUPLED to `__init__.py`-resident code (the C/D finding above) — the direction of the dependency is what decides, and #433 cluster F shows the SAFE direction resolves for free via the SAME facade re-export every earlier cluster already uses.** Cluster F's `merged_closes`/`card_reconcile` call a git-primitive trio (`_default_git_run`/`_git_first_line`/`_git_base_ref`) that is ALSO used by cluster E's `delivery_state`/`_repo_label` (not yet extracted, still resident in `__init__.py`) — the earlier F/C/D investigation comment on this ticket flagged this as needing the SAME `watchdog.`-prefixed deferred-import workaround C/D need. Live-verified it does NOT: moving the trio INTO `watchdog/cards.py` (where F's own new code needs it to run standalone) and re-exporting it back into `__init__.py` via the established `from watchdog.cards import _git_first_line as _git_first_line, ...` facade means every STILL-RESIDENT `__init__.py` caller (`_git_commit_ts`, `delivery_state`, `_repo_label`) keeps calling it as a bare name, resolved through `__init__.py`'s own module globals AFTER the facade import runs — Python's late name binding means source-line ORDER between the facade import and the caller `def`s never matters, only that the import has executed by the time the caller is actually INVOKED, which it always has (module-load completes fully before `run_once()` is ever called). Confirmed empirically: `fn.__globals__ is watchdog.__dict__` True for every one of `_git_commit_ts`/`delivery_state`/`_repo_label`/`run_once`, and `python3 -c "import watchdog"` raises nothing. **The actual test that separates the two classes**: which side of the move needs something from the OTHER side? C/D's hazard is a NEW leaf module needing OLD `__init__.py`-resident symbols (a `from watchdog import X` inside the new module would try to import from a `watchdog` package still mid-initialization — genuinely circular). F's shared-helper situation is the OPPOSITE: the new leaf module (`cards.py`) needs NOTHING back from `__init__.py` at all (stdlib + a locally-imported external package only) — it is `__init__.py`'s OWN remaining code that needs something now hosted in the leaf module, which is just an ordinary, non-circular FORWARD import via the facade, identical in shape to how `run_once()` itself already calls every previously-moved cluster's functions as bare names. When a future cluster (E itself, once extracted) needs the SAME trio, it takes it via a plain `from watchdog.cards import _git_first_line, _git_base_ref` — leaf importing from leaf, still no cycle. **Before assuming a shared-symbol cluster needs the deferred-import/`watchdog.`-prefix technique, ask which way the dependency arrow points — only "new module needs old-module symbol" is the circular case; "old module needs new-module symbol" is always safe via the standard facade.**

- **`deliver_goal()`'s condition ORDER means `pane_goal_armed()` (the tri-state already-armed check) runs BEFORE `_classify_boundary()` (busy/input/no-input-line) — a genuinely BUSY pane (a spinner, no `❯` visible anywhere) is therefore UNDETERMINABLE for the armed check FIRST (no box to read at all), so it resolves to `"skip:undeterminable"`, never `"skip:busy"`, even though `"skip:busy"` is a real, separately-reachable branch in the same function.** (archive-moved from internals-watchdog.md, #433 step 16, to stay under the 50 KB cap.) #403: a first-draft test asserted a plain busy-spinner fixture reaches `"skip:busy"` and got `"skip:undeterminable"` instead — not a bug, the correct consequence of the documented check ordering (armed-status is resolved before boundary classification, since dropping an already-armed request is cheaper and safer to decide first). To exercise the busy-KIND branch in isolation, mock `pane_goal_armed` to return a determinable `False` directly rather than trying to construct an organic fixture where BOTH `pane_goal_armed`'s whole-capture "❯" scan and `_classify_boundary`'s bottom-up chrome-peel-based box search would need to disagree in exactly the right way (they usually agree, since both ultimately look for the same glyph) — that combination is real but genuinely hard to construct honestly, and mocking the earlier check is the honest way to test the later one in isolation.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31)

- **The presence marker `/tmp/claude-user-active-<sid>` is stamped ONLY on UserPromptSubmit (`clear-question-dedup.sh`) — a prompt SUBMIT, never composition keystrokes — so ANY "is a human at this pane right now" check built on it (plus `_last_human_prompt_ts`, which equally only ever sees submitted prompts) can detect submits, never un-submitted TYPING.** #442-review F1 (MAJOR): the lane-fill nudge's short recent-activity window was justified with "live typing stamps the marker every minute or two" — factually wrong, and the draft-delivery change had removed the only protection an actively-COMPOSING user had, so their half-typed draft would be stash-delivered into with near-certainty once the pane was otherwise eligible (~60s sweep cadence vs multi-minute composition). The one DIRECT evidence of live composition a sweep can get is a **two-capture box-content diff**: the function already holds the sweep-top `captured` and takes a `fresh` capture right before the send — if the input-box content differs between them, a human is typing, refuse-transient and consume nothing. Reuse this exact check (zero extra tmux round-trips when both captures already exist) in any future keystroke-sending job that must not type into a composing user; never justify a recency window with a marker-stamping claim without reading what actually stamps it.
- **A "transient, retry next sweep" delivery-abort branch whose refusal deliberately does NOT consume the mechanism's own attempt budget makes that mechanism's give-up/escalation branch STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE for a permanently-failing target — the escalation trigger must OR a bounded consecutive-abort streak alongside the landed-attempt cap.** #442-review F2 (MAJOR), the known bounded-consecutive-occurrence class at a new address: the lane nudge's stash-abort path correctly refused to advance `ln`/`llast` (a refused attempt is not a nudge), but since the give-up ping fired only at `ln >= cap`, a lane wedged on e.g. the user's OWN parked stash-slot draft (no janitor provenance will ever clear it) retried — and for keystroke-bearing abort shapes, retyped — every ~60s forever with the escalation unreachable. Fix shape: a per-target consecutive-abort counter (`rec["lna"]`, cleared on success and on the session-active reset, NEVER in a branch the counter's own prior action can cause), with the EXISTING give-up branch firing on either cap and an honest per-cause ping text. Whenever a budget-non-consumption decision ships for a fallible delivery path, immediately ask which escalation that non-consumption just disconnected.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31)

- **root on the subdev VPS is reachable ONLY from the gatekeeper VPS, never from dev1** — `ssh -i ~/.ssh/subdev_admin root@100.118.174.27`, run through a gatekeeper hop. That is the whole mechanism for any "install an OS package for the subdev streams" ticket: none of the four managed accounts there (montalu / marek / david / simap) has sudo, so `check_runtime_deps`'s auto-install arm can never fire and its loud `MISSING RUNTIME DEP` warning is the designed outcome, not a bug to route around. One `apt-get install -y <pkg>` as root covers all four accounts at once; verify afterwards with `command -v <pkg>` under each ACCOUNT rather than under root, which is what such a ticket is actually asking for (#171).
- **Enabling an MCP plugin provisions the SERVER, not its runtime DEPENDENCIES — a plugin whose backend needs its own downloaded binaries (Playwright's browser cache under `~/.cache/ms-playwright`) can be "installed and enabled" while every real tool call still fails.** #158: adding `playwright@claude-plugins-official` to `MANAGED_PLUGINS` correctly installed the plugin's cache + enabled it in `enabledPlugins` fleet-wide, but a live fleet audit (an adversarial review, not the original implementation) found THREE accounts (montalu2/montalu3/montalu4) with node present, the plugin enabled, and a completely EMPTY `~/.cache/ms-playwright` — the MCP server would start fine and every `browser_*` tool call would fail with "Executable doesn't exist" the first time anyone actually tried to use it. The plugin's own manifest/cache-glob check (`_managed_plugin_built`) has no way to see this, because it is checking a DIFFERENT artifact (the plugin's own extracted files, e.g. `.mcp.json`) than the one the runtime actually needs (the downloaded browser binary). Fixed with a SEPARATE, dedicated check-and-provision step (`ensure_playwright_browsers()`: content-based "is the cache dir non-empty, not just present" check, then a best-effort time-boxed `npx playwright install chromium`, no sudo needed since it's a per-user `$HOME` cache) wired in AFTER the plugin-enable step. General lesson: when a NEW managed dependency has its OWN secondary runtime-fetched payload (browser binaries, model weights, a bundled toolchain download), audit and provision that payload as its own explicit step — "the plugin cache glob matched" only proves the WRAPPER is present, never that the thing it wraps can actually run.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31b)

- **"Never touches an EXISTING session" is not the same claim as "never RESURRECTS a session" — a check gated on has-session alone cannot tell "never provisioned" apart from "a human deliberately killed it".** #263's `ensure_stream_tmux_session` (bootstrap a subdev stream account's tmux session on every `install`/`push`) correctly never touched a session that WAS still alive — but a session a human killed on purpose (out of token budget, done for the day, a VPS reboot) ALSO reads as "doesn't exist" to `has-session`, so the old code would silently re-create it and auto-launch `claude` into it on the very next push. That is exactly the standing, already-recorded user complaint in this file about NOT touching a deliberately-stopped session (see the `feedback_never_touch_stopped_sessions` entry) — just one level up: not "don't type into a stopped pane", but "don't even RE-CREATE a session that once existed and is now gone". General fix shape for any future "provision X if it's missing" job that could plausibly run against something a human deliberately removed: gate the WHOLE decision behind a ONE-TIME-EVER sentinel (a marker file written the MOMENT the decision is made, before acting, so even a failed first attempt never retries) rather than re-deriving "should I act" from current absence on every run.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31c)

- **Sizing a "raise this timeout" fix: the new bound must exceed the SUM of every INNER timeout the same call can burn through, not just be "bigger than the old number".** #263's `REMOTE_DEPLOY_TIMEOUT_S` (bounding a single `ssh user@host 'python3 airuleset.py install'` call) was first raised 60s → 300s — better, but still LESS than the real worst case: `check_runtime_deps()`'s `apt-get install` (300s PER missing package) + a new `ensure_claude_cli_installed()` curl-install (180s) + `ensure_playwright_browsers()`'s npx install (300s) — up to ~780s of best-effort network work alone inside ONE remote install, before any of the ordinary steps. On the EXACT scenario the ticket existed for (a fresh account missing both the claude binary and the Playwright cache), the outer bound would still fire first, SIGHUP the remote's own still-running install mid-way, and silently skip every step scheduled after the kill point. Before raising an outer timeout that wraps a call which can invoke several INNER best-effort steps, sum every inner step's own worst-case bound and set the outer one comfortably above that sum — not above the previous outer value.
- **Writing a NEW idempotent marker-block function (bashrc/conf-file add-or-remove-between-markers): use the SAFE positional left-to-right span scan from day one, never the older `START.*?END` lazy-regex shape** — this repo already has BOTH in production: `apply_tmux_history_limit`'s `_clean_tmux_block_spans` (safe, #235's own fix) and `apply_ultracode_launcher` (still the older buggy shape, deliberately left unfixed and tracked as its own follow-up, #237). A new function copying the MORE COMMON existing pattern (`apply_ultracode_launcher`, since it's the one most other code in the file resembles) would silently reintroduce the exact corruption class #235 already found and fixed elsewhere in the SAME file. #263/#264's `apply_stream_ssh_attach`/`_stream_marker_block_spans` used the safe shape from the start — cheap when writing fresh code, and it closes the WORST failure of that class (a stray leftover START silently eating real content up to a LATER genuine block's END). Note the honest residual even the safe scan doesn't close: a single ISOLATED stray start/end pair with no OTHER marker literal between them is structurally indistinguishable from a real block to ANY purely positional scan, and its content IS still lost on rewrite — document that limitation explicitly rather than overclaiming "self-heals" (an adversarial review caught exactly this overclaim in the first draft of both functions' docstrings).

## Presunuté z internals-hooks.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31d)

- **Moving rule prose that tests pin by literal substring: keep every pinned phrase on ONE line — a hard-wrapped paragraph breaks `assertIn` on the newline, and the failure output is a full-file dump that hides the cause.** Hit 6 times in one pass moving the model-awareness justification layer into `skills/fable-advisor/SKILL.md` (#92): the source module used long unwrapped lines, the new home was written wrapped at ~78 cols, and phrases like `Opus 5 ships thinking ON by default` silently became `ON by\ndefault`. Every one of the 6 failures looked like "the content is missing" while the content was present and correct. Write a receiving home for pinned prose UNWRAPPED (match the source's line discipline), or reflow the assertion — never assume a substring survives a re-wrap.
- **Before trimming a module whose prose lists banned phrases, RUN the hook against each phrase — never trust the module's own claim about what it enforces.** `autonomous-quality-discipline.md` asserted its merge-bypass family was "HARD-blocked at Stop by stop-check-prose-violations.sh"; the block printed `VIOLATION:` to stderr and never called `add_hard`, so Stop was never prevented (#92 item 4). The distinction matters because a NON-BLOCKING Stop hook's stderr is not fed back to the model — a soft warning corrects nobody, so "hook-covered" prose was in fact unenforced. Reuse the existing harness (`json.dumps({"session_id":…, "last_assistant_message":…})` piped to the hook, check stdout for `"decision": "block"` = HARD, stderr for `VIOLATION` = SOFT) and build the payload with `json.dumps`, never hand-escaped — phrases contain quotes. Grepping for `add_hard` in the hook is the fast first pass: a branch that only `echo`es to `>&2` is soft by construction.
- **A banned-phrase gate should be split by AMBIGUITY, not enabled wholesale.** When making the quality-bypass family hard (#92), phrases naming a merge/gate bypass explicitly (`admin-merge`, `merge despite`, `functionally ready`, `UNSTABLE … merge`) can block safely, but bare delegation phrases (`your call`, `cheaper option`, `easier path`) have innocent readings ("the cheaper option is a smaller VM") and must stay warn-only or the gate blocks honest messages. Keep both branches firing; only the consequence differs.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31e)

- **A session-active reset gated on `idle < GOAL_LANE_IDLE_S` is ALWAYS true on a BUSY session (fresh transcript mtime), so if it also zeroes a streak/give-up counter, that counter can never accumulate on the busy path and every escalation it feeds becomes STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE — scope such a reset to the branch that genuinely goes quiet, never at the top of a function a busy path also flows through (#442 THIRD GAP + its own F1 review).** The whole #442 THIRD GAP fix (`goal_lane_occupancy_nudge`, `watchdog/goal.py`) is: the top-of-function idle gate `if idle < GOAL_LANE_IDLE_S: return logs, False` was a SILENT early-return (empty `logs`) that ran BEFORE `live_workers`/`backlog_n` were counted — a busy under-saturated supervisor (2 workers, turns spinning → mtime always fresh) never reached the fill-the-cap decision and the guard journalled NOTHING (gk: 2 workers, I 32, silent 20+ min). Fix = move the idle gate DOWN, per-branch: the 0-worker EMPTY-lane branch keeps the 15-min requirement (and now LOGS `skip:idle` with numbers instead of returning empty); the UNDER-SATURATED (1..4 workers + backlog>10 + mem OK) fill branch has NO idle floor (fires while busy — the whole point) and NO permanent give-up (`GOAL_LANE_MAX_NUDGES` is 0-worker-only via `count_gaveup = (not under_saturated) and n >= MAX`), repeating with the 15-min `GOAL_LANE_INTERVAL_S` cooldown. Delivery stays gated by `_boundary_ok` (idle prompt) + the two-capture draft-diff + cooldown, so removing the under-sat idle floor never spams. **The F1 recurrence (the trap this bullet exists for):** the session-active reset (`ln/lna/lpinged`) was left at the top gated on `idle < GOAL_LANE_IDLE_S` — always true on a busy session — so it zeroed the stash-abort streak `lna` every sweep and a permanently-aborting lane's give-up ping (`aborts >= GOAL_LANE_MAX_STASH_ABORTS`) became unreachable (harmless in the OLD code because the top idle gate early-returned before any stash delivery; exposed the instant the busy path became reachable). Fix = move the reset INTO the 0-worker empty-lane branch only (fires at the idle prompt, the only point the give-up is ever evaluated). Every reaching branch now logs its decision with numbers (`skip:idle`/`skip:saturated`/`skip:backlog-small`/`skip:low-mem`/`skip:cooldown remaining=Ns`/`fire`) — silence is no longer undiagnosable. Both the outcome close-criteria (a journal showing guard decisions with numbers; ≥5 live workers at backlog>10 across ≥3 checks ≥2h apart) are a LIVE-gk gate the supervisor owns, never satisfied by green tests + merge alone.

## api-watchdog — #442-review cluster (archived from internals-watchdog.md under byte cap, #497 batch 2)

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31f)

- **Folding a `:count`/generation SUFFIX into an existing `notify.send` `dedup_key` (goal-dark re-pings, or any staged-reping feature) must keep the FIRST occurrence's key BYTE-FOR-BYTE the legacy shape — appending the suffix to `count==1` yields ONE duplicate ping across the live deploy boundary, because the api-watchdog runs the working tree every 60s and a pre-deploy on-disk dedup marker (`~/.claude/autopilot-notify-sent/…`, 14-day TTL) was written under the OLD key and does not collide with the new `:1`-suffixed key.** #459 (staged dark-goal re-ping, `goal_dark_watch`): the fix branches the key — `count==1` → `"goal-dark:%s:%d" % (sid, mark_ts)` (exactly what #403 shipped), suffix `:%d` appended only for `count>=2`. The message text was already identical; only the key had drifted, and a green test suite that only counts `len(sent)` never sees it (a fresh-context adversarial review's MINOR finding, live-reachable during that one deploy tick, self-heals after). General rule for THIS repo's dedup work: when adding a new segment to any deployed `dedup_key`, preserve the exact legacy key for whatever value the pre-deploy marker was written under (usually the first/count==1 case), and LOCK the first occurrence's message + key with a test that captures the `dedup_key` kwarg (not just the message), mutation-verified against a revert of the branch — `len(sent)`-only assertions have no teeth on key shape.
- **Fixing a `notify.send` dedup-DELIVERY bug in one cross-stream job by mirroring a proven sibling's dedup-key fix (#360 `bounce_backstop` mirroring #353's `gk_request_backstop`) must ALSO mirror the sibling's send-RESULT logging (`result = send_fn(...)` + a `(send=%r)` journal suffix), or the fix is UNVERIFIABLE on a live box.** #360: the bounce Discord branch logged `bounce-ping %s %s` identically whether notify returned `'sent'` or `'dedup'`, so a recurrence of the exact swallow-bug being fixed is invisible in the journal, and the ticket-validator's manifestation search (grep `bounce-ping` lines) structurally cannot tell a delivered re-ping from a swallowed one; the sibling `cross_stream.py:761` already logged `(send=%r)` for exactly this reason. Distinct from the #459 deploy-boundary/key-shape bullet above — THIS is observability PARITY between sibling delivery jobs: copying job A's delivery-key fix into job B must also copy A's send-result journal line, or B's own fix has no live-box signal. Both fresh-context adversarial rounds flagged the gap.
- **The client-side single-definition pattern (#367):** to split/partition the
  ticket set, partition the ONE already-fetched rows dict (`_union_open_issues`/
  `_slice_mine_and_handed` already fetch `labels`) client-side (`_partition_user_
  waiting`) — NEVER a second gh query. A second query is exactly the "parallel
  derivation" that lets the footer and the `/goal` stop-proof drift apart. Keep
  empty-result refuse gates (`_refuse_unless_empty_is_trustworthy`) on the FULL
  fetched set (`not seen`/`not rows`), not the partitioned subset — a non-empty set
  that is ENTIRELY parked is a real, trusted workable-0.
- **Satisfying the #442c every-sweep logging contract when extending a guard whose early-returns are SILENT (`return logs, False` with `logs` never appended): either LOG a decision line or explicitly document the path as deliberately-silent with a stated reason — never leave a bare silent early-return, since the contract's whole point is that every sweep journals WHY no action fired.** #475 (`goal_lane_occupancy_nudge`, `watchdog/goal.py`): 9 guard early-returns + the `goal_lane_sweep` driver's `armed is not True: continue` were all silent — on a saturated supervisor `pane_goal_armed` reads `None` (footer obscured), so the driver skipped a genuinely-armed session and the guard never ran, producing 46 of 57 sweeps with no decision line. The reusable extension shape: (1) a tiny module-level helper `_lane_skip(logs, loc, reason)` that appends `"lane-occupancy %s -> %s" % (loc, reason)` — mirror the EXISTING decision-line format (`lane-occupancy <loc> workers=%d ... -> <decision>`) so a journal grep stays consistent; (2) early skips that run BEFORE counts (`live_workers`/`backlog_n`) are computed name the GATE (`skip:awaiting-user`, `skip:blocking-dialog`, `skip:compacting`, `skip:already-handled`, `skip:not-idle-prompt`), NOT numbers they don't have yet — a later skip that DOES have counts (`skip:working-no-tasks`, `skip:gave-up`) includes them; (3) a genuinely deliberately-silent path (a wiring/injection guard, a structurally-N/A reduced-authority path) gets a `# deliberately silent: <reason>` comment and a test asserting `logs == []`, so "silent" is a recorded decision not an oversight; (4) the driver's own tri-state skip logs `skip:armed-undeterminable` for the `armed is None` INCIDENT case (footer obscured this sweep) while leaving `armed is False` (readable footer, no armed goal = not a candidate) deliberately silent. Test teeth: ONE dedicated fixture per now-logging path asserting its specific `skip:<reason>` substring is in `logs` (RED proven genuine — `AssertionError: []` empty logs against pre-fix code, NOT AttributeError, because the fixtures drive the REAL functions that exist in both versions); a `_lane_skip`-format append is append-then-return-the-same-`(logs, bool)` so control flow is provably unchanged (a fresh-context review's REFUTE checklist confirmed every branch's return value/ordering is byte-identical to pre-fix). Reuse this exact shape (mirror-format helper, gate-not-numbers for pre-count skips, deliberately-silent comment + `logs==[]` test for the genuinely-N/A paths) for any future watchdog guard being brought into the every-sweep logging contract.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-15, kolo 31g)

- **When a job persists a decision as STABLE-forever based on a verify call (e.g. "this ticket was verify-REJECTED, stop re-checking it"), the verify's own empty result is NOT enough — you must distinguish a GENUINE measured answer from the verifier's OWN failure/degrade sentinel, or you persist a TRANSIENT failure as permanent.** This is a sharper form of the recurring "an empty read means unmeasurable OR genuine-negative" class (#199/#88/#172), now for a PERSISTED decision rather than a one-sweep one. Job 25 `card_reconcile` (`watchdog/cards.py`) gained a persistent `rejected` map (parallel to `pinged`) so a candidate a `closed_fetch` verify rejected is never re-verified/re-logged every sweep — but the load-bearing correctness point is: `_normalize_closed(None)` and `_normalize_closed({})` BOTH yield an empty confirmed set, yet `None` is `_watchdog_closed_fetch`'s documented degrade-sentinel (it returns `None` on `gh returncode != 0`, "degrade to the local answer rather than silence") while `{}` is a genuine measured "nothing merged-closed this window". Fix shape, reusable for any future verify-and-persist: capture the RAW return, set `verify_ok=False` on BOTH a raised exception AND a `None` (unmeasurable) return, and PERSIST the rejection only when `verify_ok` (a genuine measured answer, even an empty dict). A transient failure persists NOTHING, so the finding is retried once the verifier recovers — proven by the boom-verifier regression test (raises) AND the `_watchdog_closed_fetch`-returns-None regression test (gh failure); the two look identical to a `try/except`-only guard, which is exactly why a `None`-specific check is needed alongside it. Companion MINOR (review-caught): the transient-failure paths must NOT log the misleading `verify-rejected` line ("GitHub confirmed not-merged") — gate that log on `verify_ok`, and give the `None` path its own honest `verify-unmeasurable` line (the exception path already has `verify-failed`), or a sustained graphql outage re-logs a false "rejected" every sweep.

## Presunuté z internals-tests.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 31h)

- **`gh issue create --label X` SILENTLY DROPS the label when the actor lacks push access, while still returning success and creating the issue — a `r.returncode == 0` check on that combined call only ever proves the CREATE succeeded, never that a privileged field (label/assignee/milestone) actually landed.** #221: GitHub's `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/issues` endpoint enforces push access for `labels`/`assignees`/`milestone` by silently IGNORING them, never by failing the request — the DEDICATED `POST .../issues/{n}/labels` endpoint (`gh issue edit --add-label`) is what correctly 403s for the same actor. `cmd_gk_request`'s create-mode path baked `--label needs-gatekeeper` into the single create call, so a read-only-fork actor's request reported "gk-request filed" (rc=0, issue genuinely created) with no label ANYWHERE on the resulting ticket — invisible to every downstream `needs-gatekeeper` label search. Reproduced entirely LOCALLY (`unittest.mock` over `subprocess.run`, no ssh, no remote box) by mocking the create call to return rc=0 for an argv that still carries `--label needs-gatekeeper`, mirroring the documented GitHub behaviour rather than needing a live fork to trigger it. Fix pattern, generalizable to any future `gh issue create --label X`/`--assignee X` call: never bake a privileged field into the create call — create bare, then apply the field via its OWN dedicated `gh issue edit --add-label`/`--add-assignee` call and check THAT call's own exit code, which is honest. This is the SAME shape #191/M4 already established for a DIFFERENT label on the SAME function (the `handed-by:<user>` origin marker) — when a fix already exists for one field on a function, check whether a SIBLING field on that same function (here, the PRIMARY `needs-gatekeeper` label, not just the secondary origin one) has the identical vulnerability before closing the ticket.

- (moved from internals-tests.md, #500 ratchet room) **A test fake modeling only SOME of a pane's keystroke vocabulary (Enter/BSpace but not `C-s`) makes a load-bearing hardcoded boolean parameter (`parked=False`) MUTATION-INVISIBLE — the mutant fires the unmodeled keystroke and the fake silently no-ops it, so state-only assertions (`box == ""`, `stash is None`) pass either way.** #306 adversarial review MINOR-1: mutating `_send_goal_verified`'s `_undo_and_release_slot(..., False, ...)` call to `True` passed both new tests, because `_SendVerifiedSwallowFake` (tests/test_goal_rearm.py) had no `C-s` handler at all. Fix: teach the fake the SAME single-slot stash model `FakePane.key` already has (tests/test_stash_unconditional.py), and assert the KEYSTROKE itself is absent (`assertNotIn("C-s", tmux.keys(), ...)`), not just the resulting state — a stray `C-s` against an empty box + empty slot is a genuine no-op in the fake's OWN model, so only checking whether the keystroke was SENT catches a mutant whose effect the fixture happens not to render. Verified with the established backup/mutate/restore technique (`cp` the file, one targeted `str.replace` with `assert mutated != src`, confirm a real `AssertionError`, restore).

- **Adding an injected `run_once(..., new_param=None)` param re-breaks `test_vault_purge_job.py::TheUnwiredGuardHasTeeth._mutant()`'s pinned signature anchor (the recurring #182/#172/#160/#312/#440 re-pin), AND the re-pin has a NEW NameError trap the earlier re-pins never hit: the mutant's `mutated` replacement string historically TRUNCATED the signature (dropping trailing params to stay short), which was safe only because every earlier dropped param's body reference sits INSIDE a try/except — a brand-new param whose run_once body uses it in a bare `if new_param is not None:` guard OUTSIDE any try/except then NameErrors in the mutant for a reason UNRELATED to the vault_purge guard under test (`test_the_guard_fails_against_a_live_default` failed with `watchdog/__init__.py:<N>: NameError` instead of the expected `AssertionError`).** #461: fix the `_mutant()` to mutate ONLY the guard's default (`old.replace("vault_purge=None", "vault_purge=lambda: []")`, preserving every OTHER param verbatim) rather than a hand-written truncating tail — this is both correct (the mutant is now byte-identical to real run_once except the one guard flip, the cleanest possible mutant) AND future-proof: it survives the NEXT injected-param addition with no re-pin needed at all, since it no longer hardcodes the signature tail. Whenever you add an injected param to `run_once`, re-pin the `old` anchor as usual, but ALSO check the `mutated` replacement is a `old.replace("vault_purge=None", ...)` shape (preserve-all-params), never a truncating rewrite — if it still truncates, convert it while you're there.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 32a)

- **The design's stated block LINE-RANGE is an estimate; the authoritative move set is the NAME LIST, and a constant a moved body depends on can legitimately sit OUTSIDE that set (assigned to a LATER step).** #433 step 1: the design said "move block 395-863" but the last named function (`project_label`) ends at 835 — lines 836-863 are `is_usage_cap`'s intro comments + `_USAGE_CAP_RX`/`_TRANSIENT_RX`, which belong to step 4 (decide.py). Moving the literal range would have dragged step-4 material into transcripts.py. Re-locate by def NAMES, cut at the last named def's real end. Conversely `_SENTINELS` (line 388) is used by 4 moved functions but is NOT in step 1's name set (it's in step 4's 253-394 range) — so it STAYS in `__init__` and the moved module reaches it via `from watchdog import _SENTINELS` at module top. That from-import is legal ONLY because (a) `_SENTINELS` is bound ABOVE the facade-import position (388 < 395), so it is never the banned below-position-FUNCTION import shape, and (b) a grep proved it unpatched (a patched constant would need `watchdog._SENTINELS` call-time instead, to avoid freezing a stale value at import). When a moved body references a name outside the move set, check its definition position vs the facade position AND its patch status before choosing from-import vs call-time attribute access.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16)
- **A `cmd_install()` step that can now `sys.exit()` needs the SAME "track it, don't abort the loop" treatment `cmd_push()`'s remote-deploy loop already has for its OWN failures — and it is easy to add the exit without noticing the caller never catches it.** #273's adversarial review: `cmd_push()`'s "2. Install locally" step called `cmd_install(args)` in-process with no `try/except` at all; giving `cmd_install()` a new `sys.exit(1)` path (to make a still-failing managed-plugin install a loud, non-zero deploy failure — the ticket's own explicit ask) meant that `SystemExit` propagated straight out of `cmd_push()` BEFORE the remote-deploy loop (step 3) ever ran. Since `git push` to GitHub happens in step 1 (strictly before step 2), main had ALREADY advanced by the time the local install died — so the fix would have deployed to `main` and then silently deployed to **zero** of the 9 remote hosts, including the very montalu2/montalu3/montalu4 accounts the ticket exists to fix. The general check, worth running on ANY future change that adds a `sys.exit()`/raise to a function ALREADY called from inside a bigger orchestrator: grep every call site of that function and ask whether IT already has failure-tolerant wiring around ITS OWN peer steps (here, the remote loop's `try/except subprocess.TimeoutExpired` + `failed.append(...)` + `continue` pattern) — if the orchestrator's sibling steps are already resilient to failure but the NEW exit-capable call isn't wrapped the same way, that asymmetry is the bug. Fix: wrap the local step in `try/except SystemExit`, feed the SAME `failed` list the remote loop appends to, and hoist `failed = []` above BOTH steps so one shared list tracks every failure across the whole `cmd_push()` run.
- **A "safe read that never crashes" helper (`read_file_safe`) only guards against a MISSING path — swapping a filesystem glob for a direct file read in an existing check needs its OWN crash-safety audit, because a glob genuinely never raises on a directory/permission/encoding weirdness where a `path.read_text()` call does.** #276's own adversarial review: `_plugin_registry_keys()`'s docstring promised "unreadable file -> empty set", but `read_file_safe()`'s `exists()` → `read_text()` shape only catches the FIRST of three ways a path can fail to be read — a directory-shaped path raises `IsADirectoryError`, a permission-denied file raises `PermissionError`, invalid UTF-8 raises `UnicodeDecodeError`, and all three propagated UNCAUGHT. The raise escaped `_managed_plugin_built()` at `setup_managed_plugins()`'s own `if _managed_plugin_built(key): continue` — sitting OUTSIDE the per-plugin `try/except` that only wraps the `subprocess.run` install call — so `cmd_install()`'s outer try/except silently swallowed it as "(non-fatal)": remaining plugins and `ensure_playwright_browsers()` never ran, yet "Install complete." was still reported. This is a STRICT REGRESSION vs the code it replaced (`glob.glob()` never raises for any of those three shapes) — a genuine, if low-probability (needs a root-owned/corrupt/dir-shaped file on a managed box), silent-forever-skip reintroduction of the EXACT bug class the ticket existed to kill. Fix: `try: raw = read_file_safe(path)\nexcept (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as e: print(...); return set()` — `OSError` covers `IsADirectoryError`/`PermissionError`/`FileNotFoundError` in one catch. General lesson: replacing a glob (which structurally can't raise on read-permission/shape problems — it just returns no matches) with a direct file read is not a drop-in swap; audit every way the NEW read call can fail that the OLD glob call structurally could not, and check whether the raise would escape into an unguarded caller.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 32b)

- **A per-user binary provisioned onto the no-sudo subdev stream accounts must land in `~/.local/bin`, never `~/bin`** — only `~/.profile` (a LOGIN shell) adds `~/bin` to PATH, and a Claude Code Bash tool call is not a login shell. `~/.local/bin` is the ONE directory this repo's own managed claude launcher (`CLAUDE_LAUNCH_SCRIPT_CONTENT`, see the `case ":$PATH:" in ...` line, "claude installs to ~/.local/bin") already prepends to PATH on every session start, so every Bash tool call inside that session inherits it for free — no new PATH machinery needed. #275 adversarial-review MAJOR-2, caught only because a fresh-context reviewer checked WHICH shell actually reads `~/.bin`'s PATH addition; a green test suite could not have caught it, since the tests never exercise a real login-vs-non-login shell distinction.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 33a)

- **A def-time-default constant that is ALSO a def-time default of a function STAYING in `__init__` (`run_once`'s own signature) is still safe to MOVE + re-export, NOT forced to stay — because the positional facade lands ABOVE the staying function's def, so its def-time default resolves through the re-export.** #433 step 14 (sweep_jobs.py): `PENDING_DONE_GRACE`/`PENDING_PREFIX` are `deliver_pending_done`'s def-time defaults AND `run_once`'s signature defaults (`done_grace=PENDING_DONE_GRACE`), and run_once stays in `__init__`. The step-14 design rule ("def-time defaults → move + re-export") wins over the `_SENTINELS`-style "stay + from-import" because these constants' ONLY consumers are sweep_jobs-domain (deliver_pending_done + the run_once call that delegates to it) — sweep-domain constants move with their code; package-wide config (`PROJECTS_DIR`/`STATE_PATH`) stays. Two cross-checks make the move safe: (a) the facade re-import at the removed block's position (~L5811 after the earlier constant removal) is above run_once's def (~L6150), so run_once's def-time default binds the re-exported value; (b) a body-only constant that grep-proves UNPATCHED (`_EXEC_MARKER_PREFIXES`) MOVES module-local rather than staying-with-`watchdog.<CONST>`, keeping it verbatim-co-located with its def-time-default sibling (`MAIN_EXEC_MARKER_MAX_AGE_S`) under one section comment — the "stay + call-time access" branch of the design rule only earns its keep when a test actually patches the constant. A `test_run_once_signature_defaults_resolve_the_reexported_constants` inspect-signature test locks cross-check (a): it NameErrors at import if a future reorder drops the re-export below run_once's def.
- **In a BACK-REFERENCE split module, a constant referenced as a DEF-TIME DEFAULT (a parameter default) and one referenced only in a function BODY get OPPOSITE treatment — a single "use call-time `watchdog.<CONST>` for everything" rule is WRONG for the def-default.** #433 step 13 (handoff_alarm.py): `_stale_handoff_alarm(..., schedule=GKREQ_REPING_SCHEDULE_S)` binds its default at DEF time, so it structurally CANNOT be call-time `watchdog.GKREQ_REPING_SCHEDULE_S` — it MUST be `from watchdog import GKREQ_REPING_SCHEDULE_S` at module top, which is legal ONLY when the constant is (a) bound in `__init__` above the facade position AND (b) grep-proven unpatched (a def-default that a test DOES patch is unpreservable without changing the signature, which C1 forbids — that would be a genuine blocker, not a style choice). The SAME function's BODY constants (`GKREQ_STALE_HANDOFF_S`, `STALE_HANDOFF_ALARM`) and its cross-module helper call (`_gkreq_reping_due`, re-exported from cross_stream.py) stay call-time `watchdog.<name>` to keep the monkeypatch seam live — so ONE module legitimately mixes both idioms. The positive proof a leaf module (step 1) never needed: a teeth-bearing test that PATCHES `watchdog.<CONST>` / `watchdog.<fn>` at the PACKAGE level and asserts the moved body's behaviour changes (template-sentinel appears in the ping, threshold suppresses the ping, the patched reping helper is reached) — mutation-verified RED against a reverted frozen-`from watchdog import` regression; an identity-only re-export test passes under that regression and so cannot prove the call-time seam at all.
- **A FUNCTIONAL back-reference test has teeth ONLY for a called name that STAYS in `__init__` (a bare call NameErrors in the new module's namespace) — for a CO-MOVED intra-module cross-call a bare name resolves module-locally and PASSES every functional test while silently bypassing the `patch.object(watchdog,"NAME")` seam (the design's #1 hazard). The co-moved cross-call needs a PATCH-OBSERVING test, not a functional one.** #433 step 3 (pane_text.py, back-reference module): the first-cut functional test mutated `watchdog._above_box_scan(captured)` → bare `_above_box_scan(captured)` inside co-moved `_pane_has_queued_compact` and STILL PASSED (1 passed), because `_above_box_scan` is defined in the same new module — the mutation only removes the monkeypatch seam, which no functional call can observe. Fix: add a `CoMovedCrossCallsGoThroughPackageSeam` test that `mock.patch.object(watchdog, "_above_box_scan"/"_above_input_box", return_value=<sentinel>)` and asserts the CALLER observes the sentinel; reverting either co-moved prefix to bare then FAILS both assertions (proven by live mutation). For every future back-reference split, split the seam tests two ways — functional calls (teeth for `__init__`-resident helpers) AND `patch.object(watchdog,...)` observation tests (the only teeth for co-moved intra-module cross-calls).

## api-watchdog — archived from internals-watchdog.md (#505 byte-cap move, 2026-08-16)

- **A module-split design's C4 "module-local, co-moved" classification of a `run=`/injection helper default must be re-checked against the ACTUAL code shape — a body `X = X or _helper` fallback is NOT a def-time default, and if C5 proves `_helper` a live seam it needs `watchdog._helper` regardless of what the design estimated.** #433 step 2 (tmux_io.py extract): the binding design wrote "`run=_default_run` defaults are module-local (co-moved)", but NONE of the moved tmux functions actually use `run=_default_run` as a def-time default — they all use `run=None` + a BODY `run = run or _default_run` fallback (7 sites), and C5 proved `_default_run` a live seam (`patch.object(wd, "_default_run", tmux)` in test_goal_arm drives the whole tmux path through a fake). Keeping it bare/module-local as the design's estimate said would have silently bypassed that patch after the move (bare `_default_run` resolves in the new module's globals, not `watchdog._default_run`) — the design's own #1 hazard. All 7 went through `watchdog._default_run`; test_goal_arm + test_compact (which also patches it) confirmed green. The design's C4 note is written from a pre-split digest and can mis-name a body-fallback as a def-time default; the authoritative signal is (a) the real syntactic shape of the reference and (b) the C5 patch-status of the name, never the design's prose classification. Same "design estimate vs authoritative code/C5" principle as the block-LINE-RANGE lesson above, one level down at the def-time-vs-body-reference distinction.
- **A module-split's C5 seam sweep must ALSO grep tests for a plain `assertIn("def <movedname>", <file>.read_text())` SOURCE-PRESENCE lock — a class distinct from monkeypatch seams (`patch.object`/`patch("mod.NAME")`/direct-assign) AND from the cluster-L2 stdlib-attr string-patch — and it routinely lives in a test file named for an UNRELATED feature, so the touched-file suite stays green while the FULL `unittest discover` gate (cmd_push) goes RED.** #433 step 6 (discord_api.py): moving `parse_discord_reply` out of `__init__.py` broke `tests/test_channels_transport_defer_418.py::TestKeptTransportStillExists::test_job7_reply_parser_kept`, a #418 "native-now-defer KEPT" lock that asserts the reply-routing machinery still exists by grepping `"def parse_discord_reply"` in `watchdog/__init__.py` — round-1 adversarial review (seam files + the new split test only) passed it SAFE; round-2 (which actually ran the defer file) caught it. The machinery is KEPT, only moved, so the fix is to repoint the lock to the def's new home (`watchdog/discord_api.py`) with a stated `#433 step N` reason, never a silent drop (#317). Reusable sweep for EVERY split step, run BEFORE claiming seams repointed: `grep -rnE '"def (<each-moved-name>)"' tests/` across the WHOLE tests tree (not just the domain's own files) — a source-presence `assertIn("def X", ...)` lock is invisible to a `patch`-shaped seam grep, and a KEPT-machinery defer lock deliberately lives under another ticket's filename. Two ancillary confirmations from the same step: (a) the design's re-import-POSITION rationale ("1863 < 4235 because deliver_discord_replies def-time-binds fetch=fetch_channel_messages") was OBSOLETED by batch-27 — the fn now resolves `fetch = discord_fetch or fetch_channel_messages` at CALL time, so a fresh grep for def-time default-param bindings of the moved names (found NONE) is the authoritative position check, not the stale design prose; (b) `size_ratchet.py --update` only ratchets a ceiling DOWN — a DELIBERATE test-file growth (a review-fix's added tests, or a #317 stated-reason comment) needs a BY-HAND ceiling raise in `tests/size_ratchet.json` with the reason in the commit message, exactly as the violation message instructs.

- **A REPO-scoped, BOX-WIDE aggregate pinged to the BOX OWNER (owner-decision digest #461) LEAKS internal tickets to an EXTERNAL sub-dev on a reduced-authority box (#489) — same class as #247's card-unreported (a repo-scoped metric mis-attributed to whoever ran it).** Gate any box-wide-owner-pinged aggregate on `_box_authority()` = `AUTHORITY_BY_USER.get(_current_user(), "full")` (cwd-INDEPENDENT), NEVER `resolve_authority()`: the latter honours a per-repo `airuleset:authority=full` CLAUDE.md marker relative to cwd, so a stray marker in the watchdog's cwd would re-open the leak. Place the gate AFTER the bucket dedup and STAMP the bucket on skip, so a reduced box logs the skip at most once/day (not every 60s sweep — but a repeated `--dry-run` then dedups to `[]`, so grep the journal for `reduced-authority box`). Fail-open for an UNMAPPED user is the SAME systemic property the whole authority model already has (`resolve_authority`, skill-scoping) — no weaker.

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 34a)

- **A transcript reader that decides "did this session RECOVER from an api-error" must NOT treat a bare `user`-entry tail as proof of progress — the watchdog's OWN injected `continue` nudge lands as a plain-text `user` entry, so "any `user` tail → recovered" RELOCATES the endless-ping bug from the subagent path (job 1b) to the main path (job 1).** #484: append-only jsonl keeps a historical api-error line forever, so `transcript_last_error` must read recency-of-error-vs-later-GENUINE-activity. The signal for genuine progress is narrow: an assistant `tool_use` block (checked BEFORE the empty/sentinel skip, since a pure tool_use entry has empty text ∈ `_SENTINELS`) OR a `user` entry carrying a `tool_result` block (proof the harness actually RAN a tool → session alive) — NOT a plain-text `user` entry. A plain-text `user` entry is either a resume prompt not yet acted on OR job 1's own `continue` nudge (lands at submit-ACCEPT, before any response; a swallowed keystroke writes nothing) — reading it as recovery returned `''`, so job 1 skipped `stalled.add(key)` (`__init__.py:6337`), the sweep cleanup wiped the episode, and the #175 back-off reset to nudge#1 every time CC's retry re-wrote the error line (round-A adversarial-review 🔴, live job-1 call-site verified). Two more reusable checks the review rounds surfaced: (1) such a reader must scan the SAME tail-window constant as its sibling (`transcript_text_toolcall_stall` uses `max_lines=200`, not the default 60) or a genuine stall buried under >60 trailing hook/bookkeeping writes is silently missed — the same "two functions reading the same file must agree on window" class as the #393 bullet above; (2) system + non-conversational bookkeeping entries (`queue-operation`/`ai-title`/`file-history-snapshot`/`mode`/`permission-mode`/`pr-link`) are SKIPPED (keep scanning), never treated as progress, else a genuine stall with a trailing bookkeeping line reads as recovered. Divergence from the sibling (which keeps the broad `non-assistant → not-stalled` rule) is deliberate: a miss in `transcript_last_error` = an un-resumed dead session, a miss in the sibling = one benign skipped nudge. No new suppression gate/threshold (#486 — read the right signal, don't add a gate).

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 34b)

- **#433 step 4 (`decide.py`) surfaced THREE split idioms the earlier steps had not: (a) a constant DEFINED inside the extracted cluster but CONSUMED by a STAYING function as a BARE MODULE GLOBAL (not a def-time default) must MOVE + be re-exported — the facade re-export makes the staying function's bare-global read resolve AND stay patchable, exactly like the step-14 def-default case but one level down at a body read; (b) a split module whose NAME equals an exported FUNCTION name (module `decide` exporting `decide`) has its `watchdog.decide` ATTRIBUTE rebound by the facade to the FUNCTION — so `import watchdog.decide as x` binds the function (impl-dependent) and the MODULE object is reachable only via `sys.modules["watchdog.decide"]`; the split test must assert re-export identity against `sys.modules[...]`, never the shadowed attribute; (c) a back-ref call sitting INSIDE a `try/except Exception` (here `session_user_stopped`→`_iter_jsonl_tail`, `parse_reset_epoch`→`_above_input_box`) has its bare-reversion NameError SWALLOWED into the fail-safe return, so a FUNCTIONAL teeth test passes on a broken bare call — only a PATCH-OBSERVING test (patch `watchdog.<name>`, assert the caller observes it) has teeth there; a new class beyond step 3's co-moved-cross-call.** Concretely: `SESSLIMIT_RETRY_S`/`SESSLIMIT_MAX_TRIES` are read bare by run_once job 6 → moved + re-exported; `_LIVE_BG_TASK_RX` (only the two pane readers use it) stayed resident, read `watchdog._LIVE_BG_TASK_RX`. Salvage/resume shape that worked: a session-limit-killed prior worker left an uncommitted `decide.py`; a reverse-substitution byte-proof (strip the 5 C3 prefixes → must reproduce the `__init__` blocks byte-for-byte, 0 residual `watchdog.`) validated it verbatim against the CURRENT base before adopting, then the facade + breadcrumb were rebuilt on the current tree (never adopt the dead worker's `__init__` wholesale — it lacks the base's later merges).

## Presunuté z internals-watchdog.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16, kolo 34c)

- **Co-moving a constant whose ONLY consumer is a PREVIOUSLY-extracted SIBLING module (not `__init__`, not the moved cluster) can strand a stale location-comment in that sibling's OWN docstring — test-invisible, so the source-presence-LOCK sweep (#433 step 6) never catches it.** #433 step 11 (`long_turn.py`): `_QUEUED_COMPACT_RX`'s only consumer is `watchdog/pane_text.py` (step 3, already merged); co-moving it for block contiguity (reached via the re-export as `watchdog._QUEUED_COMPACT_RX`) left pane_text.py's docstring still saying the constant is "defined in `__init__.py`" — now false, caught only by a round-2 reviewer READING the sibling, never by any test (unlike a `assertIn("def X", …)` LOCK, which breaks the suite). Reusable sweep for any step co-moving a name whose consumer sits in an already-extracted sibling: `grep -n '<name>' watchdog/<sibling>.py` for a home/location comment and correct it in the SAME commit.
- **A delivery job whose send lacks the post-send verify its siblings have: mirror the FULL sibling swallowed-submit recovery, and #271/#360 recur on the draft-rescue call.** #375: `deliver_compact`→`send_continue` never verified — a swallowed Enter (agent-strip selector / menu overlay, #36) reported "sent", cleared the pending request AND started the 30-min cooldown → the `/compact` intent was lost while goal/stash already recover. `_compact_submit_verified` mirrors `_send_goal_verified`/`deliver_with_stash`: pre-type bare re-check + `_draft_rescue_persist(logs=...)` right before `send_continue` (round-1 review: the caller's fresh recapture proves bare, but round-trips pass before the type and `send_continue` only checks strip-selection); ONE corrective Escape+Enter (never a 2nd Escape #35); persistent swallow → `_undo_and_release_slot(parked=False)` off the bare-verified box AND a NON-TERMINAL skip word so the caller LEAVES the request pending (never `mark_compact_delivery_ts` → no cooldown blocking the retry; janitor job 20 backstops a failed undo). `_draft_rescue_persist` MUST get + forward `logs=` (round-2 MINOR: a silent rescue failure is the #271/#360 gap); a 3-way outcome string (sent/swallowed/raced-busy) beats a bool that collapses distinct causes into one dishonest `NOT-DELIVERED reason=` log.
