- **#433 step 7 (`questions.py`): on a LATE split step the authoritative "prefix with `watchdog.`" set is NOT just the pre-split top-level defs/constants — it MUST also include every PRIOR step's FACADE re-exports, which a natural first-pass free-variable analysis that classifies against `{ast FunctionDef ∪ ast Assign}` ONLY silently MISSES because those names enter `__init__`'s namespace via `ast.ImportFrom` (`from watchdog.<sub> import X as X`), not a def/assign.** The first analysis flagged only the co-moved helpers + `__init__`-defined constants and reported `clean_reply_text`/`_snowflake_ts` (←discord_api), `encode_project_dir`/`project_label` (←transcripts), `_cache_repo_roots`/`_gh_env` (←cross_stream) as UNCLASSIFIED — they are watchdog-package attrs (re-exported by steps 4/5/6's facades) and MUST be call-time `watchdog.<name>` in the new module, or they NameError (not local) / worse, silently bypass a seam if a later step co-moves them. The correct classifier for any step N: free-name minus `{builtins, params, local vars, function-LOCAL imports}`, then split the remainder into (a) top-level STDLIB/3rd-party imports → replicate the import at the new module top, bare; (b) `watchdog.<sub>` facade re-exports ∪ `__init__` defs/assigns ∪ co-moved step names → prefix `watchdog.`; (c) the one def-time default (`PROJECTS_DIR`) → module-top `from watchdog import`. A reverse-substitution byte-proof against `git show HEAD:__init__.py` with a **0-residual-`watchdog.`** assertion catches an over- OR under-prefix instantly. Companion teeth lesson: a patch-observing seam test has teeth ONLY when the fixture makes the REAL return DIVERGE from the patched value — `_in_sleep_window(10**9)` is hour-3 Bratislava (real `True`), so patching `True` is indistinguishable from reality and a bare-revert still passes (round-1 review MINOR); use a NOON epoch (`1704106800`, real `False`) so patched `True` diverges and the bare-revert goes RED. Base-sync gotcha: the worktree was cut from an OLDER origin/main lacking steps 4/5/6 — `git merge --ff-only main` (local main, 43-ahead/unpushed) restored the ~6037-line base the design's positions assume before any work.

<!-- moved from internals-watchdog.md (#511 playbook append, cap overflow) -->
- **A split step's design range "block X minus names moved in step N" is un-followable when step N is NOT actually merged on your base (the dispatch's "step N merged" claim can be STALE) — the sibling-step names stay INTERLEAVED, so move ONLY your step's EXPLICIT NAME LIST as a small set of blocks (C1), leaving the un-moved sibling names in place; one positional facade at the earliest removed position covers the non-contiguous set.** #433 step 8 (card_flags.py): the dispatch said step 6 was merged but on the real base it wasn't, so three step-6 names (`parse_discord_card_reply`/`fetch_reaction_users`/`_reacted_by_owner`) sat interleaved among the eleven step-8 functions — moving the literal range would have dragged them into the wrong lane. The authoritative move set is always the step's own NAME LIST by def name, never the estimated range: a SECOND, distinct reason the step-1 block-LINE-RANGE-is-an-estimate lesson bites — a sibling step's names live INSIDE the range, not just an off-by-a-few boundary.
- **A stale-base extraction of a function that `origin/main` modified IN PLACE is a merge-forward TRAP the base-only byte-proof cannot see — resolve by PORTING main's in-place delta INTO the new module, never `--ours`/`--theirs`, and byte-prove against `origin/main` not just the dispatch base.** #433 step 10 (goal_scan.py): base 8b62e5c was BEHIND origin/main, which had landed #386 (0feed39) modifying `pane_goal_armed` in place — the exact function step 10 moves out. The extraction was byte-verbatim vs the base and the round-1 base-only proof passed SAFE, but `git merge origin/main` then produced a `deleted-by-us/modified-by-them` conflict on that one function; an automated integrator taking `--ours` silently reverts a live-incident fix (the reviewer flagged it via `git merge-tree HEAD origin/main`). Fix: keep the `__init__` deletion, port #386's exact body change into `goal_scan.py`'s copy (re-applying the SAME C3 `watchdog.` prefixes), then a POSITION-INDEPENDENT `ast.get_source_segment` byte-proof of ALL moved funcs against `git show origin/main:` (not the base) AND run main's OWN added tests (`test_goal_footer_draft_glyph.py`) through the ported function to prove the fix survived, not silently reverted. Two systematics for every remaining step: (1) `git fetch && git merge origin/main` (base-sync) BEFORE extracting, so a mid-flight in-place fix to a moved function surfaces as a normal edit rather than a post-hoc conflict; (2) the adversarial review MUST diff each moved function against `origin/main`, not only the dispatch base — a base-only proof is blind to a sibling/hotfix landing on main during the worktree's lifetime. Also new here: the C4 co-move rule's COMPLEMENT to step 13's above-facade `from watchdog import` case — a def-default constant (`GOAL_MARK_TAIL_BYTES`, `scan_goal_markers`'s `tail_bytes=`) whose `__init__` home sits BELOW the facade re-import position CANNOT be `from watchdog import`ed (import-time forward reference) → it MUST co-move module-local + re-export.
- **Sweeping a module for test locks: run the 8/10-word n-gram script over the whole `tests/*.py` corpus, not a handful of manual greps.** ~15 lines (read every test file into one string, slide 10/8/6-word windows from each module line, report first hit + which file). On `model-awareness.md` it found locks in FOUR test files (`test_model_tiering`, `test_workflow_cost_discipline`, `test_ruleset_conversion_wave2`, `test_airuleset`) plus two more found only by a filename grep (`test_dynamic_application`, `test_main_implementation_guard`) — so run BOTH: the n-gram sweep for prose locks and a filename grep for locks that reference the module by path. Neither alone is complete.
- **Measuring a session-start prefix change: the isolated probe profile must be REFRESHED between the before and after runs.** `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.claude/skills/` are copies, so a probe run after editing `modules/**` still measures the OLD prefix unless `airuleset.py install` runs first and the copies are re-made. The @imports inside the copied `CLAUDE.md` resolve against the REAL `$HOME`, so module edits DO show up live — but the managed CLAUDE.md itself and the skill listing do not. Sequence: edit → `airuleset.py install` → re-copy `CLAUDE.md` + `skills/` into the probe config → run `claude -p` → read the first assistant turn's `usage`.
- **Report a prefix saving in TWO numbers when the project `CLAUDE.md` changed.** The measured delta in the repo's own cwd includes its project `CLAUDE.md`, which no OTHER project loads; the portable saving is the `modules/**` delta alone. Quoting only the in-repo number overstates what every other project gets.
- **`gh run view --json X --jq Y` applies `--jq` INSIDE `gh` itself — the filter never reaches a separate `jq` process.** A test stub that fakes `gh` on `$PATH` must print the ALREADY-FILTERED value (`echo 'completed success'`), never the raw JSON (`echo '{"status":"completed",...}'`) — a raw-JSON stub silently breaks a `case "$s" in completed*)` check (`$s` is the whole JSON object, which never starts with `completed`) and the failure LOOKS like the loop under test is broken (empty output, exit 0) when it is really just a wrong stub. Hit live building #90's `TestCiMonitoringPollSnippetSelfBounds` — the first stub version made every real-`gh`-shaped test silently pass through with zero output instead of exercising either branch.

## Presunuté z internals-cli.md (ratchet cap, 2026-08-16)

- **Mutation-verifying a small, self-contained fix without the full mutation-testing skill's machinery: `cp` the target file aside, apply ONE targeted `str.replace()` with an `assert mutated != src` guard (so a no-op replace never silently "passes"), run ONLY the specific new test the finding is about, confirm a genuine failure (not an unrelated crash), then `cp` the backup back and re-run the file's full test class to confirm byte-identical restoration.** #133 used this for all 4 of its adversarial-review-driven fixes (drop `str()` coercion, drop the `"other"`-managed-tier guard, no-op the shim append, drop `_managed_model`'s `try/except`) — every one was independently confirmed KILLED before the GREEN commit landed. This is the same recipe several earlier tickets in this file already document (#186, #235's `test_apply_ultracode_launcher_never_...`, the mutant-testing bullets throughout) — worth reaching for on ANY adversarial-review finding whose fix is a one-or-two-line diff, since it costs seconds and directly answers "does the new test actually catch what it claims to catch" rather than trusting that a green suite implies teeth.
- **A single-plugin "why does the version differ across accounts" investigation needs the FULL version-source hierarchy, not just "git-backed checkout vs not" — a plugin can ALSO declare its own version directly, which wins first and needs no git at all.** #279: reading real `plugin.json` files across several plugins in the SAME marketplace checkout (`discord@claude-plugins-official` declares `"version": "0.0.4"` in its own manifest and shows exactly that; `playwright`/`context7`/`frontend-design` declare no version field at all and fall through to whatever the CHECKOUT provides — a git-derived commit sha if it's a real `git clone`, else the literal `"unknown"`). A reviewer catching this by reading SIBLING plugins' manifests inside the same investigation, not just the one plugin the ticket named, is what surfaced the missing first tier of the hierarchy. Whenever explaining a Claude Code plugin's displayed "version", check the plugin's OWN `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` for a declared field before reasoning about the marketplace checkout's git state at all.
- **A widened structural "this call chain must never gain gate X" scan needs to cover EVERY function in the chain a hypothetical gate could naturally land in — not just the CLI entry point, which is rarely where a future editor would actually add one.** #228: the first-draft test scanned only `cmd_compact_request`'s `--self` branch for an `agent_type` check; a fresh-context review's own mutant — a gate inserted at the TOP of `deliver_compact_self` (the function that actually resolves the session, and so the more natural place for a future "only for the worker" restriction) — passed the test clean. Widening the scan to `inspect.getsource()` on all three functions in the chain (`cmd_compact_request`'s branch, `deliver_compact_self`, `resolve_self_pane`), then re-running the SAME mutant, is what actually proved teeth. When a mechanism's safety rests on "no gate anywhere in this call chain", enumerate the chain explicitly rather than anchoring on the single function a ticket happened to name.

- **Which grouped tmux session survives a multi-member `destroy-unattached` cleanup sweep is ITERATION-ORDER-ARBITRARY, not "the first-created" or "the exact-username-named" one.** Live-verified on an isolated `-L` scratch server: a 2-member group (`zbynek`, never attached + `zbynek-dup`, attached then detached) converged to `zbynek-dup` surviving, NOT `zbynek` — the opposite of what "the base session survives" intuition predicts. This matters because the managed launcher's `tmux new-session -A -s "$(whoami)"` only ever checks for a session with that EXACT name (never searches the whole group) — if a cleanup sweep destroys the exact-username-named session while a differently-named sibling survives, the next launcher invocation silently creates a fresh EMPTY session instead of reattaching to the surviving, populated one. Confirmed this is a PRE-EXISTING condition, not something #254's own fix introduces: this box's own real live sessions right now are `zbynek-4`/`marek-0` (no bare `zbynek`/`marek`), under the CURRENT unfixed default `off` conf. Filed as #284 (Scope-gate: needs-user-decision) rather than fixed, since the right answer needs the actual external mechanism that creates grouped duplicates identified first — grepping this repo's own `airuleset.py` end to end confirmed neither the dev1/dev2 launcher nor the subdev ssh-auto-attach block ever calls the `new-session -t <existing>` shape that links a session into a group, so whatever creates `zbynek-1..4` is external to this repo entirely.
- **An UNVERIFIED `identity:` assumption in a NEW `REMOTE_HOSTS` entry is a fail2ban risk, not just a maybe-wrong config value — the difference between "mirror simap's shape" (safe: a data-only registration, inert until `push` runs) and "assume simap's IDENTITY is also this new account's identity" (unsafe: the very next push is a genuine, untested ssh auth attempt against a box that bans on a few wrong-key tries, collateral-damaging every OTHER already-working subdev target in the same run) is worth stating explicitly whenever mirroring a sibling stream's registration.** #300 (miva1): the ticket said "same phase-1 isolated shape as simap" for the REGISTRATION, but never confirmed miva1's `authorized_keys` actually accepts the same operator key — unlike simap's own #143 commit, which recorded a LIVE-VERIFIED fact ("simap's authorized_keys are the SAME operator keys as marek"). A worker banned from ssh cannot close this gap itself; the correct move is registering the entry (inert data) AND filing a follow-up naming the exact pre-push verification needed (confirm the identity via the sanctioned `root@subdev` hop, or gatekeeper's own confirmation), so the supervisor's post-merge push does not become the first live test of an unverified auth assumption. The account's OWN `~/devel/airuleset` checkout is a SEPARATE, lower-stakes gap — `cmd_push` has no clone-if-missing bootstrap, so a target with no checkout yet just fails cleanly (`rc=1`, reported, not dangerous — the SAME expected shape every brand-new stream hits) until it's cloned (the repo is public, no credential needed).


- **#433 step 9 (`discord_replies.py` — the 459-line `deliver_discord_replies` + 9 helpers + 8 consts): for a move too big to hand-edit its ~43 C3 seam sites reliably, insert the `watchdog.` prefixes MECHANICALLY with `tokenize`, NEVER `ast.unparse` (reflows + drops comments → kills verbatimness) nor a whole-word string-replace (corrupts docstrings/comments that NAME the moved symbols — this cluster's comments say "capture_pane"/"_react_ok"/"_input_line_text" everywhere).** Compute the seam set with `symtable` (each moved function's `is_global()` names ∩ the `__init__` module-level namespace, MINUS co-moved-unpatched names, PLUS the co-moved-PATCHED ones — here `_gh_comment` + the three `clean_reply_text` regex/cap constants); then tokenize each block and insert `watchdog.` ONLY before a NAME token that (a) is in the seam set, (b) is NOT preceded by an OP `.` (attribute access) and (c) is NOT preceded by `def`/`class` (its own definition — `_gh_comment` is both a def here AND a seam, the `def`-guard disambiguates); apply insertions per-line BACK-TO-FRONT so no other byte moves. **The trap that a naive "name ∈ wd namespace → prefix" classifier hits: a stdlib MODULE you import directly in the new module (`time`) is ALSO a `watchdog`-namespace member because `__init__` does `import time`, so it FALSE-POSITIVES into `watchdog.time` — exclude every stdlib module you replicate at the new module top (`re`/`time`), keep them bare.** The automatic per-function reverse-substitution byte-proof (`xf.replace("watchdog."+name, name)` for every seam name == the original block AND insertions == replacements) catches an over- OR under-prefix instantly, teeth-confirmed by mutating a `watchdog.X` back to bare / to a frozen module-local copy (both flip the patch-observing seam test RED). Two more: (a) the design's def-time-binding ordering rationale ("6 & 8 before 9 because `card_gh=_gh_call`") is OBSOLETE — the real signature is call-time `card_gh_fn=None` + `card_gh_fn or watchdog._gh_call`, so step 9 lands SAFELY before steps 7/8 even when `card_flags.py`/`questions.py` are NOT on the base (the dispatch's "6 & 8 merged" claim was stale; the "intermediate states safe by construction" guarantee is literally relied upon — every cross-ref resolves call-time through the package namespace regardless of which module physically owns the name); (b) a constant used ONLY by moved code but NOT in the design's explicit 8-const move list (`_DREPLY_TYPED_TTL_S`) STAYS in `__init__` per the binding design, reached `watchdog._DREPLY_TYPED_TTL_S` — never silently widen the move set to "tidy up" a sibling constant.
